let  $\tilde{\phi}_t^\infty$  and  $\tilde{\mathbf{b}}_t^\infty$  be the  $n\to \infty$  limits of  $\tilde{\phi}_t$  and  $\tilde{\mathbf{b}}_t$ , and let us introduce

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} = n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {z} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {z} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {t} \end{array} \right).
$$

then by  $t^{(c)}$  and proposition e.5,

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} \rightarrow \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left(\begin{array}{c c}\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}&\tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\\\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top}&\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\end{array}\right). \tag {a.29}
$$

to check that this limit  $\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_t^\infty$  is invertible, observe that its  $2t\times 2t$  submatrix removing row and column  $t + 1$  is just  $\mathbf{m}_t^\infty$  from (a.21), which is invertible by  $t^{(d)}$ . the schur-complement of the  $(t + 1,t + 1)$  entry is exactly (a.22), which we have shown is positive in (a.24). thus  $\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_t^\infty$  is invertible, so  $(\mathbf{u}_{t + 1},\mathbf{z}_t)$  has full column rank  $2t + 1$  for all large  $n$ . then by proposition f.1, the conditional law of  $\mathbf{o}$  is

$$
\left(\mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \quad \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t}\right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {z} _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} + \pi_ {(\mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} \pi_ {(\mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {z} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top}
$$

where  $\tilde{\mathbf{o}}$  is an independent haar-orthogonal matrix. thus, to analyze the joint behavior of  $(\mathbf{u}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{u}_{t + 2},\mathbf{z}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{z}_{t + 1},\mathbf{e})$ , we may replace the update  $\mathbf{s}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{o}^{\top}\boldsymbol {\lambda}\mathbf{r}_{t + 1}$  by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {s} _ {t + 1} = \mathbf {s} _ {\parallel} + \mathbf {s} _ {\perp} \\ \mathbf {s} _ {\parallel} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {z} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & 0 \\ - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \\ \mathbf {s} _ {\perp} = \pi_ {(\mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {z} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} ^ {\top} \pi_ {(\mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {a r} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {\lambda r} _ {t + 1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

to analyze  $\mathbf{s}_{\parallel}$ , recall the notation  $\mathbf{l}_t^{(k,\infty)}$  from (a.25) and set

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(k, \infty)} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(k, \infty)} \\ (\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(k, \infty)}) ^ {\top} \end{array} \right), \qquad \tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(k, \infty)} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(k, \infty)} \\ l _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(k, \infty)} \end{array} \right).
$$

then applying  $t + 1^{(b)}$

$$
n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {2} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \to \left( \begin{array}{c} \tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1, \infty)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2, \infty)} \end{array} \right).
$$

introducing the matrices

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} & \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} + \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \\ \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} + \mathrm {i d} _ {t \times t} \end{array} \right), \qquad \tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \lim _ {n \to \infty} \tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t},
$$

we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & 0 \\ - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \to \left(\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & 0 \\ (\tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t}\right) ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1, \infty)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2, \infty)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & 0 \\ 0 & \left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \end{array} \right) \left(\left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {\upsilon}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1, \infty)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2, \infty)} \end{array} \right). \tag {a.30} \\ \end{array}
$$

this also shows that  $\tilde{\upsilon}_t^\infty$  is invertible.

let us introduce the block notations

$$
\mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {b} _ {t} & \mathbf {b} _ {t} \\ 0 & b _ {t + 1, t + 1} \end{array} \right), \qquad \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {b} _ {t} \\ b _ {t + 1, t + 1} \end{array} \right), \qquad \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \sigma_ {t + 1, t + 1} \end{array} \right)
$$

and denote with  $\infty$  their  $n\to \infty$  limits. defining  $\upsilon_{t + 1}$  by (a.15) and writing this in block form, it may be checked that

$$
\boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\upsilon}} _ {t} & * \\ 0 & * \end{array} \right)
$$

where  $\tilde{\upsilon}_t$  constitutes the first  $2t + 1$  rows and columns. applying the identity (a.16) with  $t + 1$  and  $k = 1$  yields

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(1)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) = \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {1, j} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {j}) ^ {\top} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {1, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) = \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right),
$$

the second equality identifying  $c_{1,j} = \kappa_{j+1}$  and applying the definitions of  $\mathbf{b}_{t+1}$  and  $\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t+1}$  in (4.7). then equating the first  $2t+1$  entries of the last column on both sides, and taking the limit  $n \to \infty$ , we get

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c} \tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1, \infty)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2, \infty)} \end{array} \right) = (\tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \end{array} \right).
$$

inverting  $(\tilde{\mathbf{t}}_t^\infty)^\top$  and applying this to (a.30),

$$
\mathbf {s} _ {\parallel} \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} \left( \begin{array}{c c c} u _ {1} & \dots & u _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + \left( \begin{array}{c c c} z _ {1} & \dots & z _ {t} \end{array} \right) (\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

for  $\mathbf{s}_{\perp}$ , note that we have shown  $\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_t^\infty$  in (a.29) is invertible. applying the definition of  $\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_t$  and the identity (a.28), we also have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \lim _ {n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathrm {i d} & 0 \\ - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & 0 \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {2} \mathbf {r} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathrm {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} \\ 0 & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \\ = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & 0 \\ - (\tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} & 0 \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(0, \infty)} & \tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1, \infty)} \\ (\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1, \infty)}) ^ {\top} & \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2, \infty)} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \\ 0 & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right). \\ \end{array}
$$

thus the matrices

$$
{ } _ { n ^ { - 1 } } \left( \begin{array} { c c } \mathbf { r } _ { t + 1 } ^ { \top } \mathbf { r } _ { t + 1 } & \mathbf { r } _ { t + 1 } ^ { \top } \boldsymbol { \lambda } \mathbf { r } _ { t } \\ \mathbf { r } _ { t } ^ { \top } \boldsymbol { \lambda } \mathbf { r } _ { t + 1 } & \mathbf { r } _ { t } ^ { \top } \boldsymbol { \lambda } ^ { 2 } \mathbf { r } _ { t } \end{array} \right) , \qquad \left( \begin{array} { c c } \mathbf { l } _ { t + 1 } ^ { ( 0 , \infty ) } & \tilde { \mathbf { l } } _ { t } ^ { ( 1 , \infty ) } \\ ( \tilde { \mathbf { l } } _ { t } ^ { ( 1 , \infty ) } ) ^ { \top } & \mathbf { l } _ { t } ^ { ( 2 , \infty ) } \end{array} \right) \qquad \qquad ( a . 3 1 )
$$

are also invertible (the former almost surely for all large  $n$ ). observe then that

$$
\begin{array}{l} n ^ {- 1} \left\| \pi_ {\left(\mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t}\right) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \right\| ^ {2} \\ = n ^ {- 1} \| \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \| ^ {2} - n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {2} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {2} \mathbf {r} _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {2} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \\ \rightarrow \left(l _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2)} - \left(\begin{array}{c}\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}\\\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)}\end{array}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\begin{array}{c c}\mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(0)}&\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}\\(\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top}&\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)}\end{array}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\begin{array}{c}\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}\\\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)}\end{array}\right)\right) ^ {\infty} \\ \end{array}
$$

then by proposition f.2,

$$
\mathbf {s} _ {\perp} \xrightarrow {w} s _ {\perp} \sim \mathcal {n} \left(0, \left(l _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2)} - \binom {\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}} {\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)}} ^ {\top} \binom {\mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(0)}} {(\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top}} \binom {\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}} {\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)}} ^ {- 1} \binom {\tilde {\mathbf {l}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}} {\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)}}\right) ^ {\infty}\right) \tag {a.32}
$$

where this limit  $s_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(u_1,\ldots ,u_{t + 1},z_1,\ldots ,z_t,e)$ . combining the above, we obtain

$$
\mathbf {s} _ {t + 1} \xrightarrow {w} s _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{l l l} u _ {1} & \dots & u _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + \left( \begin{array}{l l l} z _ {1} & \dots & z _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + s _ {\perp}. \tag {a.33}
$$

then, since

$$
\mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} = \mathbf {s} _ {t + 1} - \left(\mathbf {u} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}\right) \left(\tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t}\right),
$$

applying propositions e.2 and e.4, this shows

$$
\left(\mathbf {u} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {u} _ {t + 2}, \mathbf {z} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {e}\right) \stackrel {{w}} {{\rightarrow}} \left(u _ {1}, \dots , u _ {t + 2}, z _ {1}, \dots , z _ {t + 1}, e\right)
$$

where  $u_{t + 2} = u_{t + 2}(z_1,\ldots ,z_{t + 1},e)$  and

$$
z _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} z _ {1} & \dots & z _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + s _ {\perp}.
$$

in particular,  $(z_{1},\ldots ,z_{t + 1})$  has a multivariate normal limit independent of  $(u_{1},e)$

to conclude the proof of  $t + 1^{(c)}$ , it remains to compute

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left( \begin{array}{c c c} z _ {1} & \dots & z _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} z _ {t + 1} \right], \qquad \mathbb {e} [ z _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ]
$$

and show that these are given by  $\sigma_t^\infty$  and  $\sigma_{t + 1,t + 1}^{\infty}$ . observe that  $\mathbb{e}[z_ss_\perp ] = 0$  for all  $s\leq t$ , since  $s_{\perp}$  has mean 0 and is independent of  $z_{s}$ . then

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \left( \begin{array}{c c c} z _ {1} & \dots & z _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} z _ {t + 1} ] = \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {- 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

to compute  $\mathbb{e}[z_{t + 1}^2]$ , note that (a.33) may be written as

$$
s _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} u _ {1} & \dots & u _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + z _ {t + 1}.
$$

taking the expected square on both sides,

$$
\mathbb {e} [ s _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \mathbb {e} \big [ \big (\left( \begin{array}{c c c} u _ {1} & \dots & u _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \big) ^ {2} \big ] + 2 \mathbb {e} \big [ \big (\left( \begin{array}{c c c} u _ {1} & \dots & u _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \big) z _ {t + 1} \big ] + \mathbb {e} [ z _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ].
$$

since  $\mathbf{s}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{o}^{\top}\pmb{\lambda}\mathbf{r}_{t + 1}$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ s _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \lim _ {n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {s} _ {t + 1} \| ^ {2} = \lim _ {n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \pmb {\lambda} ^ {2} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} = (\mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2, \infty)}) _ {t + 1, t + 1}.
$$

identifying  $\tilde{\mathbf{b}}_t$  as the last column of  $\mathbf{b}_{t + 1}$ , we have also

$$
\mathbb {e} \big [ \big (\left(u _ {1} \dots u _ {t + 1}\right) \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \big) ^ {2} \big ] = (\tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = ((\mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}) _ {t + 1, t + 1}.
$$

applying

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (u _ {1}, \ldots , u _ {t + 1}) ^ {\top} z _ {t + 1} ] = \lim _ {n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {u} _ {1}, \ldots , \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} = \tilde {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty},
$$

we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left(u _ {1} \quad \dots \quad u _ {t + 1}\right) \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) z _ {t + 1} \right] = (\tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left(\left(\mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}\right) _ {t + 1, t + 1} \\ = (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} (\tilde {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}) _ {t + 1, t + 1} \\ \end{array}
$$

thus

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ z _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \left(\left(\mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2)} - \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \pmb {\delta} _ {t + 1} \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} - \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \pmb {\phi} _ {t + 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {t + 1} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t + 1} \pmb {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {\infty}\right) _ {t + 1, t + 1} \\ = \left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}\right) _ {t + 1, t + 1} = \sigma_ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {\infty}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second equality applies the identity (a.14). this concludes the proof of  $t + 1^{(c)}$ .

finally, to show  $t + 1^{(d)}$ , observe that

$$
\left(\left( \begin{array}{l l} \operatorname {i d} & 0 \\ \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{l l} \operatorname {i d} & \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} \\ 0 & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty} = \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(0, \infty)} & \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(1, \infty)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(1, \infty)} & \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2, \infty)} \end{array} \right) \tag {a.34}
$$

by lemma a.2. the upper-left  $(2t + 1) \times (2t + 1)$  submatrix of (a.34) is exactly the second matrix of (a.31), which we have already shown is invertible. so to check invertibility of (a.34), it suffices to show that the schur complement of the lower-right entry is non-zero. by (a.32), this schur complement is equal to  $\operatorname{var}[s_{\perp}]$ . thus, we must show that  $\operatorname{var}[s_{\perp}] > 0$ .

interpreting the  $(s,s^{\prime})$  entry of  $\mathbf{l}_t^{(k,\infty)}$  as  $\mathbb{e}[\lambda^k r_sr_{s'}]$ , note that  $\mathrm{var}[s_{\perp}]$  in (a.32) is the variance of the residual of the projection of  $\lambda r_{t + 1}$  onto the linear span of  $(r_{1},\ldots ,r_{t + 1},\lambda r_{1},\ldots ,\lambda r_{t})$  with respect to the  $l_{2}$ -inner-product  $(x,y)\mapsto \mathbb{e}[xy]$ . thus, if  $\mathrm{var}[s_{\perp}] = 0$ , then

$$
\lambda r _ {t + 1} = \alpha_ {1} r _ {1} + \dots + \alpha_ {t + 1} r _ {t + 1} + \beta_ {1} \lambda r _ {1} + \dots + \beta_ {t} \lambda r _ {t}
$$

for some scalar constants  $\alpha_{1},\ldots ,\alpha_{t + 1},\beta_{1},\ldots ,\beta_{t}$  almost surely. substituting (a.23) and rearranging to isolate  $r_{\perp}$ , we get

$$
(\lambda - \alpha_ {t + 1}) r _ {\perp} = f (r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t}, \lambda)
$$

for some quantity  $f(r_{1},\ldots ,r_{t},\lambda)$  that does not depend on  $r_{\perp}$ . by assumption 4.2(e),  $\lambda$  is not a constant random variable, so on an event of positive probability, we have  $\lambda \neq \alpha_{t + 1}$ . then conditioning on  $(r_1,\dots ,r_t,\lambda)$  and on this event, we have  $r_{\perp} = f(r_{1},\dots ,r_{t},\lambda) / (\lambda -\alpha_{t + 1})$ , implying that the conditional law of  $r_{\perp}$  is constant. recall that  $r_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(r_{1},\dots ,r_{t},\lambda)$  thus  $r_{\perp}$  must be a constant random variable unconditionally. however,  $r_{\perp}$  is a mean-zero normal variable with positive variance by (a.24). this is a contradiction, so  $\mathrm{var}[s_{\perp}] > 0$ . this shows  $t + 1^{(d)}$ , concluding the induction.

# appendix b. proof for rectangular matrices

in this appendix, we prove theorem 5.3 using similar ideas. let us write the iterations (5.3-5.6) as

$$
\mathbf {r} _ {t} = \mathbf {o u} _ {t}
$$

$$
\mathbf {s} _ {t} = \mathbf {q} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t}
$$

$$
\mathbf {z} _ {t} = \mathbf {s} _ {t} - b _ {t 1} \mathbf {v} _ {1} - \dots - b _ {t, t - 1} \mathbf {v} _ {t - 1}
$$

$$
\mathbf {v} _ {t} = v _ {t} (\mathbf {z} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {z} _ {t}, \mathbf {f})
$$

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {t} = \mathbf {q} \mathbf {v} _ {t}
$$

$$
\mathbf {q} _ {t} = \mathbf {o} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t}
$$

$$
\mathbf {y} _ {t} = \mathbf {q} _ {t} - a _ {t 1} \mathbf {u} _ {1} - \dots - a _ {t t} \mathbf {u} _ {t}
$$

$$
\mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} = u _ {t + 1} \left(\mathbf {y} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {y} _ {t}, \mathbf {e}\right)
$$

note that  $\mathbf{u}_t, \mathbf{r}_t, \mathbf{q}_t, \mathbf{y}_t \in \mathbb{r}^m$  while  $\mathbf{v}_t, \mathbf{p}_t, \mathbf{s}_t, \mathbf{z}_t \in \mathbb{r}^n$ .

in the proof, we will identify the limits of the quantities

$$
m ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {s} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {r} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \equiv m ^ {- 1} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {w} \mathbf {w} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {u} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \tag {b.1}
$$

$$
m ^ {- 1} \mathbf {p} _ {s} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {r} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \equiv m ^ {- 1} \mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {w} \mathbf {w} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {u} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \tag {b.2}
$$

$$
n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {s} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \left(\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {p} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \equiv n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {v} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \tag {b.3}
$$

$$
n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {p} _ {s} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {p} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \equiv n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {w} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w}\right) ^ {k} \mathbf {v} _ {s ^ {\prime}}. \tag {b.4}
$$

in addition to the matrices  $\theta_t^{(j)}$  and  $\xi_t^{(j)}$  in (5.9-5.10), let us define

$$
\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)} = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {j - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {j - i}. \tag {b.5}
$$

for example,

$$
\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(0)} = \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}
$$

$$
\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(1)} = \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}.
$$

corresponding to (b.1-b.4), we then define four families of matrices

$$
\mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} ^ {(j)}, \qquad \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)},
$$

$$
\mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {\top}, \quad \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \tag {b.6}
$$

where  $c_{2k,j}, c_{2k+1,j}, \bar{c}_{2k,j}, \bar{c}_{2k+1,j}$  are certain rectangular partial moment coefficients, defined in section b.1 below. we show in (b.15) below that  $\bar{c}_{2k+1,j} = \gamma \cdot c_{2k+1,j}$ , so that

$$
\mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} = \gamma \cdot \left(\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}\right) ^ {\top}. \tag {b.7}
$$

the limits of (b.1-b.4) will be identified as the entries of  $\lim_{m,n\to \infty}\mathbf{h}_t^{(2k)},\mathbf{i}_t^{(2k + 1)},\mathbf{j}_t^{(2k + 1)},\mathbf{l}_t^{(2k)}$

b.1. coefficients for "partial moments". let  $\{\kappa_{2k}\}_{k\geq 1}$  be the rectangular free cumulants for the moment sequence (5.11) with aspect ratio  $\gamma = m / n$ . recall from section 2.4 the second cumulant sequence  $\bar{\kappa}_{2k} = \gamma \cdot \kappa_{2k}$  for all  $k\geq 1$ . for notational convenience, we set

$$
\kappa_ {0} = 1, \qquad \bar {\kappa} _ {0} = 1.
$$

we define four sequences of combinatorial coefficients, denoted by

$$
c _ {2 k, j}, \qquad \bar {c} _ {2 k, j}, \qquad c _ {2 k + 1, j}, \qquad \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j}
$$

for integers  $k, j \geq 0$ . these sequences are defined by the initializations

$$
c _ {0, 0} = \bar {c} _ {0, 0} = 1, \quad c _ {0, j} = \bar {c} _ {0, j} = 0 \quad \text {f o r} j \geq 1 \tag {b.8}
$$

and by the recursions, for all  $j,k\geq 0$

$$
c _ {2 k + 1, j} = \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j + 1} c _ {2 k, m} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1 - m)} \tag {b.9}
$$

$$
\bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} = \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j + 1} \bar {c} _ {2 k, m} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (j + 1 - m)} \tag {b.10}
$$

$$
c _ {2 k + 2, j} = \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j} c _ {2 k + 1, m} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (j - m)} \tag {b.11}
$$

$$
\bar {c} _ {2 k + 2, j} = \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, m} \kappa_ {2 (j - m)}. \tag {b.12}
$$

let

$$
\mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k, \ell) = \left\{\pi \in \mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k): s \cap \{1, \ldots , \ell \} \neq s \mathrm {f o r a l l} s \in \pi \right\}
$$

be the subset of non-crossing partitions  $\pi \in \mathrm{nc}'(2k)$  where no set  $s \in \pi$  is contained in  $\{1, \dots, \ell\}$ . we set  $\mathrm{nc}'(2k, 0) = \mathrm{nc}'(2k)$ . recall the moment-cumulant relations (2.6), where  $e(\pi)$  and  $o(\pi)$  count the number of sets  $s \in \pi$  whose smallest element is even and odd, and  $\bar{m}_{2k}$  is defined from  $m_{2k}$  by (2.4). then these coefficients admit the following interpretations.

lemma b.1. for each  $k \geq 0$ ,

$$
c _ {2 k + 1, j} = \sum_ {\pi \in \mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k + 2 j + 2, 2 j + 1)} \gamma^ {e (\pi)} \prod_ {s \in \pi} \kappa_ {| s |}, \qquad \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} = \sum_ {\pi \in \mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k + 2 j + 2, 2 j + 1)} \gamma^ {o (\pi)} \prod_ {s \in \pi} \kappa_ {| s |}, \quad (\mathrm {b}. 1 3)
$$

and for each  $k \geq 1$ ,

$$
c _ {2 k, j} = \sum_ {\pi \in \mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k + 2 j, 2 j)} \gamma^ {e (\pi)} \prod_ {s \in \pi} \kappa_ {| s |}, \quad \bar {c} _ {2 k, j} = \sum_ {\pi \in \mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k + 2 j, 2 j)} \gamma^ {o (\pi)} \prod_ {s \in \pi} \kappa_ {| s |}. \tag {b.14}
$$

in particular, for all  $j,k\geq 0$  we have

$$
c _ {1, j} = \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)}, \qquad \bar {c} _ {1, j} = \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (j + 1)}, \qquad c _ {2 k, 0} = m _ {2 k}, \qquad \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} = \bar {m} _ {2 k}.
$$

finally, for all  $j,k\geq 0$  , we have

$$
\bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} = \gamma \cdot c _ {2 k + 1, j}. \tag {b.15}
$$

proof. let us show (b.13-b.14) by induction on  $k$ . by the initialization (b.8) and the recursions (b.9) and (b.10), we have  $c_{1,j} = \kappa_{2(j+1)}$  and  $\bar{c}_{1,j} = \bar{\kappa}_{2(j+1)}$  for all  $j \geq 0$ . since the sets of each partition in  $\mathrm{nc}'(2j + 2)$  must have even cardinality,  $\mathrm{nc}'(2j + 2, 2j + 1)$  consists of only the partition  $\pi$  with the single set  $\{1, \dots, 2j + 2\}$  and this partition has  $e(\pi) = 0$  and  $o(\pi) = 1$ . applying  $\bar{\kappa}_{2j+2} = \gamma \cdot \kappa_{2j+2}$ , this shows both identities of (b.13) for  $k = 0$ .

assuming that (b.13) holds for some  $k \geq 0$ , we now check (b.14) for  $k + 1$ . if  $\pi \in \mathrm{nc}'(2k + 2j + 2, 2j) \setminus \mathrm{nc}'(2k + 2j + 2, 2j + 1)$ , then there is a set  $s \in \pi$  containing  $2j + 1$  that is a subset of  $\{1, \ldots, 2j + 1\}$ . since  $\pi$  is non-crossing and  $s$  has even cardinality, this set must be of the form  $s = \{2m + 2, \ldots, 2j + 1\}$  for some  $m \in \{0, \ldots, j - 1\}$ . this set  $s$  has cardinality  $2(j - m)$ , and its smallest element is even. removing  $s$  from  $\pi$  yields a bijection between all such partitions  $\pi$  and the partitions  $\pi' \in \mathrm{nc}(2k + 2m + 2, 2m + 1)$ . thus, applying the induction hypothesis (b.13) with  $m$  in place of  $j$ ,

$$
c_{2k + 1,m}\cdot \gamma \kappa_{2(j - m)} = \sum_{\substack{\pi \in \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j)\backslash \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j + 1)\\ \{2m + 2,\ldots ,2j + 1\} \in \pi}}\gamma^{e(\pi)}\prod_{s\in \pi}\kappa_{|s|},
$$

$$
\bar{c}_{2k + 1,m}\cdot \kappa_{2(j - m)} = \sum_{\substack{\pi \in \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j)\backslash \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j + 1)\\ \{2m + 2,\ldots ,2j + 1\} \in \pi}}\gamma^{o(\pi)}\prod_{s\in \pi}\kappa_{|s|}.
$$

summing over  $m = 0,\ldots ,j - 1$ , combining with the induction hypothesis (b.13) applied for  $j$ , and recalling that  $\kappa_0 = \bar{\kappa}_0 = 1$  while  $\bar{\kappa}_{2j} = \gamma \cdot \kappa_{2j}$  for  $j\geq 1$ , we obtain

$$
\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j} c _ {2 k + 1, m} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (j - m)} = \sum_ {\pi \in \mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k + 2 j + 2, 2 j)} \gamma^ {e (\pi)} \prod_ {s \in \pi} \kappa_ {| s ^ {\prime} |},
$$

$$
\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, m} \kappa_ {2 (j - m)} = \sum_ {\pi \in \mathrm {n c} ^ {\prime} (2 k + 2 j + 2, 2 j)} \gamma^ {o (\pi)} \prod_ {s \in \pi} \kappa_ {| s ^ {\prime} |}.
$$

recognizing the left sides as  $c_{2(k + 1),j}$  and  $\bar{c}_{2(k + 1),j}$  by (b.11-b.12), this shows (b.14) for  $k + 1$ .

now assuming that (b.14) holds for some  $k \geq 1$ , we check (b.13) for  $k$ . if  $\pi \in \mathrm{nc}'(2k + 2j + 2, 2j + 1) \setminus \mathrm{nc}'(2k + 2j + 2, 2j + 2)$ , then similar to the above, there is some set  $s = \{2m + 1, \ldots, 2j + 2\} \in \pi$  for some  $m \in \{0, \ldots, j\}$ , with cardinality  $2(j - m) + 2$  and whose smallest element is odd. removing  $s$  from  $\pi$  yields a bijection between such partitions  $\pi$  and the partitions  $\pi' \in \mathrm{nc}(2k + 2m, 2m)$ . then applying the induction hypothesis (b.14) with  $m$  in place of  $k$ ,

$$
c_{2k,m}\cdot \kappa_{2(j - m) + 2} = \sum_{\substack{\pi \in \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j + 2)\setminus \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j + 1)\\ \{2m + 1,\ldots ,2j + 2\} \in \pi}}\gamma^{e(\pi)}\prod_{s\in \pi}\kappa_{|s|},
$$

$$
\bar{c}_{2k,m}\cdot \gamma \kappa_{2(j - m) + 2} = \sum_{\substack{\pi \in \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j + 2)\backslash \mathrm{nc}^{\prime}(2k + 2j + 2,2j + 1)\\ \{2m + 1,\ldots ,2j + 2\} \in \pi}}\gamma^{o(\pi)}\prod_{s\in \pi}\kappa_{|s|}.
$$

summing over  $m = 0,\ldots ,j$ , combining with the induction hypothesis (b.14) applied for  $j + 1$ , and applying again  $\kappa_0 = \bar{\kappa}_0 = 1$  and  $\bar{\kappa}_{2j} = \gamma \cdot \kappa_{2j}$  for  $j\geq 1$ , we obtain (b.13) for  $k$ . this concludes the induction, showing (b.13) for all  $k\geq 0$  and (b.14) for all  $k\geq 1$ .

the statements  $c_{1,j} = \kappa_{2(j + 1)}$  and  $\bar{c}_{1,j} = \bar{\kappa}_{2(j + 1)}$  are already shown. the statements  $c_{2k,0} = m_{2k}$  and  $\bar{c}_{2k,0} = \bar{m}_{2k}$  follow from  $\mathrm{nc}'(2k,0) = \mathrm{nc}'(2k)$ , together with the moment-cumulant relations (2.6). finally, for the identity (b.15), note that this holds for  $k = 0$  because  $\bar{\kappa}_{2(j + 1)} = \gamma \cdot \kappa_{2(j + 1)}$ . supposing that it holds for  $k - 1$ , we may compose (b.9) and (b.11) to get

$$
\begin{array}{l} c _ {2 k + 1, j} = \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j + 1} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {m} c _ {2 k - 1, p} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (m - p)} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1 - m)} \\ = \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {j + 1} c _ {2 k - 1, p} \sum_ {m = p} ^ {j + 1} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (m - p)} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1 - m)} = \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {j + 1} c _ {2 k - 1, p} \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j - p + 1} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 m} \kappa_ {2 (j - p + 1 - m)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly

$$
\bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} = \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {j + 1} \bar {c} _ {2 k - 1, p} \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j - p + 1} \kappa_ {2 m} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (j - p + 1 - m)}.
$$

comparing these two expressions and applying  $\bar{c}_{2k - 1,p} = \gamma \cdot c_{2k - 1,p}$  for each  $p = 0,\dots ,j + 1$ , we get  $\bar{c}_{2k + 1,j} = \gamma \cdot c_{2k + 1,j}$ . this shows (b.15) for all  $k$ .

b.2. partial moment identities. recalling the definitions of  $\mathbf{h}_t^{(2k)},\mathbf{i}_t^{(2k + 1)},\mathbf{j}_t^{(2k + 1)},\mathbf{l}_t^{(2k)}$  from (b.6), we now collect several identities derived from the recursions for  $c_{2k,j},c_{2k + 1,j},\bar{c}_{2k,j},\bar{c}_{2k + 1,j}$

lemma b.2. for every  $t \geq 1$ ,

$$
\mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(0)} = \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \tag {b.16}
$$

$$
\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(0)} = \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \tag {b.17}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} = \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (b.18) \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \left(\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \mathbf {b} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t}\right) (b.19) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(1)} = \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (b.20) \\ = \gamma \cdot \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \mathbf {a} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}\right) (b.21) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} = \gamma \cdot \left(\mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \mathbf {a} _ {t} + \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}\right) \tag {b.22}
$$

$$
\mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)} = \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \left(\mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \mathbf {b} _ {t} + \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {b} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t}\right) \tag {b.23}
$$

proof. the identities (b.16) and (b.17) follow immediately from the initializations  $c_{0,0} = \bar{c}_{0,0} = 1$  and  $c_{0,j} = \bar{c}_{0,j} = 0$  for all  $j \geq 1$ , and the observations  $\theta_t^{(0)} = \pmb{\delta}_t$  and  $\xi_t^{(0)} = \gamma_t$ .

for (b.18), let us separate the terms of  $\mathbf{i}_t^{(1)}$  ending with  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  from those ending with  $\phi_t^\top$ . applying  $c_{1,j} = \kappa_{2(j + 1)}$ , this yields

$$
\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \left((\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i}\right).
$$

observe that

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} = \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t},
$$

while

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \left(\sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \psi_ {t} \delta_ {t} (\psi_ {t} ^ {\top} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \gamma_ {t} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top} (\psi_ {t} ^ {\top} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i}\right) \\ = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \left(\sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - 1 - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i}\right) \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} = \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}. \\ \end{array}
$$

thus we obtain (b.18). the identity (b.19) follows analogously by separating the terms of  $\mathbf{i}_t^{(1)}$  starting with  $\gamma_t$  from those starting with  $\psi_t$ . (the factor  $\gamma^{-1}$  cancels the factor of  $\gamma$  in the definitions of  $\mathbf{b}_t$  and  $\omega_t$ .) the identities (b.20-b.21) follow from (b.18-b.19) and the relation  $\mathbf{j}_t^{(1)} = \gamma \cdot (\mathbf{i}_t^{(1)})^\top$  from (b.7).

for (b.22), applying (b.12) and the identity  $\bar{c}_{1,m} = \bar{\kappa}_{2(m + 1)} = \gamma \cdot \kappa_{2(m + 1)}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2, j} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} = \gamma \cdot \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (j - m)}\right) \\ \cdot \left(\sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \gamma_ {t} (\phi_ {t} ^ {\top} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \psi_ {t} \delta_ {t} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top} (\phi_ {t} ^ {\top} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - 1 - i}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

collecting terms by powers of  $\psi_t\phi_t$  and its transpose, this is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} = \gamma \cdot \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \left[ \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i + p} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (i + p - m)}\right) (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} \right. \\ + \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i + p + 1} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (i + p + 1 - m)}\right) (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} ]. \qquad \mathrm {(b . 2 4)} \\ \end{array}
$$

from the definitions and the notation  $\kappa_0 = 1$ , we now identify

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \mathbf {a} _ {t} = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (i + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (p + 1)} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} \\ \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (i + p + 1)} \kappa_ {0} (\pmb {\psi} _ {t} \pmb {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \pmb {\gamma} _ {t} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (i + p + 2)} \kappa_ {0} (\pmb {\psi} _ {t} \pmb {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} \pmb {\delta} _ {t} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} = \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {m + 1} \\ \cdot \sum_ {q = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (q + 1)} \left(\sum_ {p = 0} ^ {q} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {q - p} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {p} + \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {q - 1} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {q - 1 - p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {p}\right) \\ = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i - 1} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (i + p - m)}\right) (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} \\ + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i - 1} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (i + p + 1 - m)}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {p} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\pmb {\sigma} _ {t} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {t} = \left(\mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} \pmb {\sigma} _ {t}\right) ^ {\top}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {m = i} ^ {i + p - 1} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (i + p - m)}\right) (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \gamma_ {t} (\phi_ {t} ^ {\top} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} \\ + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {m = i + 1} ^ {i + p} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (i + p + 1 - m)}\right) (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p} \\ \end{array}
$$

summing these four expressions and comparing with (b.24) yields the identity (b.22).

for (b.23), we may write similarly

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)} = \gamma \cdot \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {j} \kappa_ {2 (m + 1)} \kappa_ {2 (j - m)} \gamma^ {- \mathbf {1} \{m = j \}}\right) \\ \cdot \left(\sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\phi_ {t} \psi_ {t}) ^ {i} \delta_ {t} (\psi_ {t} ^ {\top} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} (\phi_ {t} \psi_ {t}) ^ {i} \phi_ {t} \gamma_ {t} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top} (\psi_ {t} ^ {\top} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - 1 - i}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the factor  $\gamma^{-1\{m = j\}}$  comes from the fact that  $\bar{\kappa}_{2(j - m)} = \gamma \kappa_{2(j - m)}$  if  $m < j$ , but  $\bar{\kappa}_{2(j - m)} = \kappa_{2(j - m)} = 1$  for  $m = j$ . this is matched by the observation that  $\mathbf{b}_t^\top \mathbf{\gamma}_t\mathbf{b}_t$ ,  $\mathbf{b}_t^\top \boldsymbol{\psi}_t\boldsymbol{\omega}_t$ , and  $\boldsymbol{\omega}_t\boldsymbol{\psi}_t^\top \mathbf{b}_t$  on the right of (b.23) have a factor of  $\gamma^2$ , whereas the last term  $\boldsymbol{\omega}_t$  has a factor of only  $\gamma$ . then (b.23) follows from an argument analogous to the above, and we omit this for brevity.

lemma b.3. define

$$
\boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \mathbf {a} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {t} + \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right), \quad \mathbf {t} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \mathbf {b} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {b} _ {t} + \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right). \tag {b.25}
$$

then for every  $t \geq 1$  and  $k \geq 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} & (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} & \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t}\right) ^ {j} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {\top} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} (b.26) \\ = c _ {2 k, 0} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(0)} & (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) + \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} 0 & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t}, (b.27) \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} & (\mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} & \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \left( \begin{array}{l l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {j} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t}\right) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {t} _ {t} (b.28) \\ = \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(0)} & (\mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) + \mathbf {t} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} 0 & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {t} _ {t}. (b.29) \\ \end{array}
$$

proof. the arguments are similar to those of lemma a.3. applying (b.18), the definitions of  $\mathbf{i}_t^{(1)}$  and  $\mathbf{a}_t$  from (b.6) and (5.12), and the notation  $\kappa_0 = 1$ , we have

$$
\boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {\top} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} + \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \end{array} \right). \tag {b.30}
$$

then applying the definitions of  $\theta_t^{(j)}$ ,  $\xi_t^{(j)}$ , and  $\mathbf{x}_t^{(j)}$  from (5.9-5.10) and (b.5), we may compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \pmb {\delta} _ {t} (\pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \left(\sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} \pmb {\psi} _ {t}) ^ {i + 1} \pmb {\delta} _ {t} (\pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} \pmb {\psi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} \pmb {\gamma} _ {t} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i}\right) \\ = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t} ^ {(j)} = \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)}, \tag {b.31} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\pmb {\delta} _ {t} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} \pmb {\psi} _ {t}) ^ {i} \pmb {\phi} _ {t} \pmb {\gamma} _ {t} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - i} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} \pmb {\psi} _ {i}) ^ {i + 1} \pmb {\delta} _ {t} \pmb {\psi} _ {i} ^ {\top} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \pmb {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j - 1 - i}\right) \\ = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {\top} = \left(\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}\right) ^ {\top}. \tag {b.32} \\ \end{array}
$$

we may also compute, analogously to (a.20),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} \left(\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}\right) ^ {i} \left(\psi_ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \gamma_ {t} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \left(\psi_ {t} ^ {\top} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {j - i}\right) \cdot \left(\sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, p} \left(\psi_ {t} ^ {\top} \phi_ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {p}\right) \\ + \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 j} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {j}\right) \left(\sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, p + 1} \sum_ {q = 0} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {q} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {p - q}\right) \\ = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i + r + 1} \kappa_ {2 m} c _ {2 k, i + r + 1 - m}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {r} \\ = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, i + r} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {r} = \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}. \tag {b.33} \\ \end{array}
$$

in the last two equalities above, we used the recursion (b.9) and the definitions of  $\mathbf{x}_t^{(j)}$  and  $\mathbf{i}_t^{(2k + 1)}$ . similarly,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 j} \big (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \big) ^ {j} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {i} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {j - i}\right) \cdot \left(\sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {p}\right) \\ + \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 j} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {j}\right) \cdot \sum_ {p = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, p} \left(\sum_ {q = 0} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {q} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {p - q} \right. \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. + \sum_ {q = 0} ^ {p - 1} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {q} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {p - 1 - q}\right) \\ = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i + r + 1} \kappa_ {2 m} c _ {2 k + 1, i + r + 1 - m}\right) (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \psi_ {t} \delta_ {t} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top} (\phi_ {t} ^ {\top} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {r} \right. \\ \left. + \left(\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i + r} \kappa_ {2 m} c _ {2 k + 1, i + r - m}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {i} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {r}\right) \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {r = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\bar {c} _ {2 k + 2, i + r + 1} (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \psi_ {t} \delta_ {t} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top} (\phi_ {t} ^ {\top} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {r} + \bar {c} _ {2 k + 2, i + r} (\psi_ {t} \phi_ {t}) ^ {i} \gamma_ {t} (\phi_ {t} ^ {\top} \psi_ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {r}\right) \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2)}. \tag {b.34} \\ \end{array}
$$

in the last two equalities above, we used the identity  $c_{2k+1,j} = \gamma^{-1}\bar{c}_{2k+1,j}$ , the recursion (b.12), and the definitions of  $\xi_t^{(j)}$  and  $\mathbf{l}_t^{(2k+2)}$ . recalling (b.30), stacking (b.31), (b.32), (b.33), and (b.34), and taking the transpose yields (b.26).

for (b.27), applying again the form (b.30) for  $\upsilon_t^\top$ , we may compute

$$
\mathbf {\boldsymbol {\upsilon}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(\underset {\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} (\mathbf {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} \mathbf {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t}) ^ {j} \mathbf {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t}} {0}\right) = \left(\underset {\sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\ell} \kappa_ {2 j} c _ {2 k, \ell + 1 - j}\right) (\mathbf {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} \mathbf {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t}) ^ {\ell} \mathbf {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t}} {\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} (\mathbf {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} \mathbf {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t}) ^ {j + 1}}\right).
$$

let us apply  $c_{0,0} = 1$ ,  $c_{0,\ell} = 0$  for  $\ell \geq 1$ ,  $c_{1,\ell} = \kappa_{2(\ell +1)}$ , and the recursion (b.9) in the form

$$
c _ {2 k + 1, \ell} - c _ {2 k, 0} \kappa_ {2 (\ell + 1)} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\ell} c _ {2 k, \ell + 1 - j} \kappa_ {2 j}.
$$

this gives

$$
\boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} 0 \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \end{array} \right) = \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {\infty} (c _ {2 k, \ell} - c _ {2 k, 0} c _ {0, \ell}) (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t}) ^ {\ell} \\ \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {\infty} (c _ {2 k + 1, \ell} - c _ {2 k, 0} c _ {1, \ell}) (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {\ell} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \qquad \mathrm {(b . 3 5)}
$$

applying the same computations as leading to (b.32) and (b.34), with the recursion (b.9) in the forms

$$
\sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i + r + 1} \kappa_ {2 m} c _ {2 k, i + r + 2 - m} = c _ {2 k + 1, i + r + 1} - \kappa_ {2 (i + r + 2)} c _ {2 k, 0}, \quad \sum_ {m = 0} ^ {i + r} \kappa_ {2 m} c _ {2 k, i + r + 1 - m} = c _ {2 k + 1, i + r} - \kappa_ {2 (i + r + 1)} c _ {2 k, 0}
$$

replacing the final two steps of (b.34), we have also

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) = \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} (\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)}) ^ {\top} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} (c _ {2 k + 1, j} - \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} c _ {2 k, 0}) \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \binom {\sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(c _ {2 k, \ell + 1} - c _ {2 k, 0} c _ {0, \ell + 1}\right) \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(\ell)}\right) ^ {\top}} {\sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(c _ {2 k + 1, \ell} - c _ {2 k, 0} c _ {1, \ell}\right) \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(\ell)}}. \tag {b.36} \\ \end{array}
$$

stacking (b.35) and (b.36), multiplying on the right by  $\mathbf{\upsilon}_t$ , and then applying (b.26) for  $k$  and also for  $k = 0$ , yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} 0 & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} \\ = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} & (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} & \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2)} \end{array} \right) - c _ {2 k, 0} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(0)} & (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right). \\ \end{array}
$$

rearranging this yields (b.27).

the identities (b.28) and (b.29) follow from writing

$$
\mathbf {t} _ {t} ^ {\top} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} + \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 (j + 1)} (\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)}) ^ {\top} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {\kappa} _ {2 j} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \end{array} \right)
$$

and applying the same arguments, which we omit for brevity.

b.3. conditioning argument. we now prove theorem 5.3 using a conditioning argument similar to the symmetric square setting. recall the definition of  $\lambda \in \mathbb{r}^{\min(m,n)}$  from (5.2). let us define

$$
\boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {m}, \quad \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n}
$$

to be this vector extended by  $m - n$  and  $n - m$  additional 0's, respectively. thus  $\pmb{\lambda} = \pmb{\lambda}_m$  if  $m \leq n$ , and  $\pmb{\lambda} = \pmb{\lambda}_n$  if  $n \leq m$ . by assumption 5.2(c), we then have

$$
\lambda_ {m} \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} \lambda_ {m}, \quad \lambda_ {n} \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} \lambda_ {n}
$$

where  $\lambda_{m}$  denotes a mixture of  $\lambda$  and the point mass at 0 when  $\gamma >1$ , and  $\lambda_{n}$  denotes such a mixture when  $\gamma < 1$ .

let  $\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_{t - 1}^{(2k + 1)}\in \mathbb{r}^{(t - 1)\times t}$  denote the first  $t - 1$  rows of  $\mathbf{i}_t^{(2k + 1)}\in \mathbb{r}^{t\times t}$ . the following extended lemma implies theorem 5.3, where parts (b) and (e) identify the almost sure limits of (b.1-b.4).

lemma b.4. suppose assumption 5.2 holds. almost surely for each  $t = 1,2,3,\ldots$

(a) for all fixed  $j, k \geq 0$ , there exist deterministic limit matrices

$$
(\pmb {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \pmb {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \pmb {\theta} _ {t} ^ {(j, \infty)}, \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \omega_ {t} ^ {\infty}, \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}, \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t - 1} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}) = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} (\pmb {\delta} _ {t}, \pmb {\phi} _ {t}, \pmb {\theta} _ {t} ^ {(j)}, \mathbf {b} _ {t}, \omega_ {t}, \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)}, \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t - 1} ^ {(2 k + 1)})
$$

(b) for some random variables  $r_{1},\ldots ,r_{t},\bar{p}_{1},\ldots ,\bar{p}_{t - 1}$  with finite moments of all orders,

$$
\left(\mathbf {r} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {r} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t - 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m}\right) \xrightarrow {w} \left(r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t}, \bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t - 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m}\right).
$$

for each  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} (r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t}) ] = \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (\bar {p} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {p} _ {t - 1}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t}) ] = \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t - 1} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}.
$$

(c)  $(\mathbf{v}_1,\ldots ,\mathbf{v}_t,\mathbf{z}_1,\ldots ,\mathbf{z}_t,\mathbf{f})\xrightarrow{w}(v_1,\ldots ,v_t,z_1,\ldots ,z_t,f)$  as described in theorem 5.3.  
(d) for all fixed  $j, k \geq 0$ , there exist deterministic limit matrices

$$
(\mathbf {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j, \infty)}, \mathbf {a} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}, \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}) = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} (\mathbf {\gamma} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)}, \mathbf {a} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}, \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)}, \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)})
$$

(e) for some random variables  $p_{1},\ldots ,p_{t},\bar{r}_{1},\ldots ,\bar{r}_{t}$  with finite moments of all orders,

$$
\left(\mathbf {p} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {p} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \xrightarrow {w} \left(p _ {1}, \dots , p _ {t}, \bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right).
$$

for each  $k\geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} \left(p _ {1}, \dots , p _ {t}\right) \right] = \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)},
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (p _ {1}, \ldots , p _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} (p _ {1}, \ldots , p _ {t}) ] = \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}.
$$

$(f)$ $(\mathbf{u}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{u}_{t + 1},\mathbf{y}_1,\dots ,\mathbf{y}_t,\mathbf{e})\xrightarrow{w} (u_1,\dots ,u_{t + 1},y_1,\dots ,y_t,e)$  as described in theorem 5.3.

(g) the matrices

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\infty} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \end{array} \right), \qquad \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} & \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \end{array} \right)
$$

are both non-singular.

proof. denote by  $t^{(a)}, t^{(b)}, \ldots$  the claims of part (a), part (b), etc. up to and including iteration  $t$ . we induct on  $t$ . we will omit details of the argument that are similar to the proof of theorem 4.3.

step 1:  $t = 1$ . we have  $\pmb{\delta}_1 \rightarrow \pmb{\delta}_1^\infty = \mathbb{e}[u_1^2]$ ,  $\phi_1 = 0$ ,  $\mathbf{b}_1 = 0$ , and  $\kappa_{2k} \rightarrow \kappa_{2k}^\infty$  where  $\kappa_{2k}^\infty$  are the rectangular free cumulants defined by the limiting moments

$$
m _ {2 k} ^ {\infty} = \lim  _ {m, n \rightarrow \infty} \frac {1}{m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {\min  (m, n)} \lambda_ {i} ^ {2 k} = \mathbb {e} \left[ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} \right]. \tag {b.37}
$$

then also  $(c_{2k,j}, c_{2k+1,j}, \bar{c}_{2k,j}, \bar{c}_{2k+1,j}) \to (c_{2k,j}^{\infty}, c_{2k+1,j}^{\infty}, \bar{c}_{2k,j}^{\infty}, \bar{c}_{2k+1,j}^{\infty})$ , and claim  $1^{(a)}$  follows from the definitions.

since  $\mathbf{r}_1 = \mathbf{o}\mathbf{u}_1$ , by proposition f.2,

$$
\left(\mathbf {r} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m}\right) \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} \left(r _ {1}, \lambda_ {m}\right) \tag {b.38}
$$

where  $r_{1}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,\mathbb{e}[u_{1}^{2}])$  is independent of  $\lambda_{m}$ . then  $\mathbb{e}[\lambda_m^{2k}r_1^2 ] = \mathbb{e}[\lambda_m^{2k}]\mathbb{e}[r_1^2 ] = m_{2k}^\infty \mathbb{e}[u_1^2 ]$ . note that  $\theta_1^{(j)} = 0$  for  $j\geq 1$ , so  $\mathbf{h}_1^{(2k)} = c_{2k,0}\theta_1^{(0)} = m_{2k}\langle \mathbf{u}_1^2\rangle$ , the last equality using  $m_{2k} = c_{2k,0}$  by lemma b.1. thus  $\mathbf{h}_1^{(2k,\infty)} = m_{2k}^\infty \mathbb{e}[u_1^2 ]$ , and this shows  $1^{(b)}$ .

for  $1^{(c)}$ , note that  $c_{2,0} = m_2 = \kappa_2$ . then  $1^{(b)}$  implies

$$
\frac {1}{n} \| \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1} \| ^ {2} = \frac {m}{n} \cdot \frac {1}{m} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {\min (m, n)} \lambda_ {i} ^ {2} r _ {i 1} ^ {2} \to \gamma \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m} ^ {2} r _ {1} ^ {2} ] = \gamma \kappa_ {2} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {1} ^ {\infty}.
$$

since  $\mathbf{z}_1 = \mathbf{q}^\top \pmb{\lambda}^\top \mathbf{r}_1$ , proposition f.2 shows

$$
\left(\mathbf {z} _ {1}, \mathbf {f}\right) \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} \left(z _ {1}, f\right) \tag {b.39}
$$

where  $z_{1}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,\gamma \kappa_{2}^{\infty}\pmb{\delta}_{1}^{\infty})$  is independent of  $f$ . identifying  $\omega_1^\infty = \gamma \kappa_2^\infty \pmb{\delta}_1^\infty$  and applying proposition e.2 for the joint convergence with  $\mathbf{v}_1 = v_1(\mathbf{z}_1,\mathbf{f})$ , this shows  $1^{(c)}$ .

observe that  $1^{(c)}$  implies  $\gamma_1\to \gamma_1^\infty = \mathbb{e}[v_1^2 ]$ . as  $\partial_{1}v_{1}$  satisfies (2.1) and is continuous on a set of probability 1 under the limit law  $(z_{1},f)$ , we have also  $\psi_{1}\rightarrow \psi_{1}^{\infty} = \mathbb{e}[\partial_{1}v_{1}(z_{1},f)]$  by proposition e.3. then  $1^{(d)}$  follows from the definitions.

for  $1^{(e)}$ , define  $\check{\mathbf{r}}_1 \in \mathbb{r}^n$  as the first  $n$  entries of  $\mathbf{r}_1$  if  $n \leq m$ , or  $\mathbf{r}_1$  extended by  $n - m$  additional i.i.d.  $\mathcal{n}(0, \mathbb{e}[u_1^2])$  random variables if  $n > m$ . by proposition f.2(b),

$$
\left(\check {\mathbf {r}} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \xrightarrow {w} \left(\check {r} _ {1}, \lambda_ {n}\right)
$$

where  $\check{r}_1\sim \mathcal{n}(0,\mathbb{e}[u_1^2])$  is independent of  $\lambda_{n}$ . note that  $\pmb{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbf{r}_{1}\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  may be written as the entrywise product of  $\lambda_{n}$  with  $\check{\mathbf{r}}_1$ , in both cases  $n\leq m$  and  $n > m$ . thus

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} \left(\bar {r} _ {1}, \lambda_ {n}\right), \qquad \bar {r} _ {1} = \lambda_ {n} \check {r} _ {1}.
$$

to analyze the joint convergence with  $\mathbf{p}_1$ , we now condition on  $\mathbf{u}_1, \mathbf{r}_1, \mathbf{z}_1, \mathbf{v}_1, \boldsymbol{\lambda}, \mathbf{e}, \mathbf{f}$ . the law of  $\mathbf{q}$  is then conditioned on the event  $\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\top} \mathbf{r}_1 = \mathbf{q} \mathbf{z}_1$ . as  $\operatorname{var}[z_1] = \gamma \kappa_2^\infty \mathbb{e}[u_1^2] > 0$  by the given assumptions, we have  $n^{-1} \mathbf{z}_1^{\top} \mathbf{z}_1 \neq 0$  for all large  $n$ . then by proposition f.1, the conditional law of  $\mathbf{q}$  is

$$
\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1} (\mathbf {z} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {1}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {z} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \pi_ {(\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {q}} \pi_ {\mathbf {z} _ {1} ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top}
$$

where  $\tilde{\mathbf{q}}\in \mathbb{r}^{(n - 1)\times (n - 1)}$  is an independent haar-orthogonal matrix. so we may replace the update  $\mathbf{p}_1 = \mathbf{q}\mathbf{v}_1$  by

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {1} = \mathbf {p} _ {\parallel} + \mathbf {p} _ {\perp}, \qquad \mathbf {p} _ {\parallel} = \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1} (\mathbf {z} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {1}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {z} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {1}, \qquad \mathbf {p} _ {\perp} = \pi_ {(\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {q}} \pi_ {\mathbf {z} _ {1} ^ {\top}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {1}.
$$

by  $1^{(c)}$  and proposition e.5,  $n^{-1}\mathbf{z}_1^\top \mathbf{z}_1\to \omega_1^\infty$  and  $n^{-1}\mathbf{z}_1^\top \mathbf{v}_1\to \psi_1^\infty \omega_1^\infty$ . then by proposition e.4,

$$
\left(\mathbf {p} _ {\parallel}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} \left(p _ {\parallel}, \bar {r} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right), \qquad p _ {\parallel} = \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {1} ^ {\infty} \cdot \bar {r} _ {1}.
$$

for  $\mathbf{p}_{\perp}$ , observe that

$$
n ^ {- 1} \| \pi_ {\mathbf {z} _ {1} ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {1} \| ^ {2} = n ^ {- 1} \left(\| \mathbf {v} _ {1} \| ^ {2} - \frac {(\mathbf {v} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {1}) ^ {2}}{\| \mathbf {z} _ {1} \| ^ {2}}\right) \to \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {1} ^ {\infty} - (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {2} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {1} ^ {\infty},
$$

so proposition f.2 shows

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {\perp} \xrightarrow {w} p _ {\perp} \sim \mathcal {n} \left(0, \gamma_ {1} ^ {\infty} - \left(\psi_ {1} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {2} \omega_ {1} ^ {\infty}\right) \tag {b.40}
$$

where  $p_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(\bar{r}_1,\lambda_n)$ . then

$$
\left(\mathbf {p} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} \left(p _ {1}, \bar {r} _ {1}, \lambda_ {n}\right), \qquad p _ {1} = \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {1} ^ {\infty} \cdot \bar {r} _ {1} + p _ {\perp}.
$$

since  $p_{\perp}$  has mean 0 and is independent of  $(\bar{r}_1,\lambda_n)$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {1} \bar {r} _ {1} ] = \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {1} ^ {\infty} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} \bar {r} _ {1} ^ {2} ] \\ = \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {1} ^ {\infty} \cdot \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} (\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda}) ^ {k} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1} \\ = \gamma \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {1} ^ {\infty} \cdot \lim  _ {n \rightarrow \infty} m ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top}) ^ {k + 1} \mathbf {r} _ {1} \\ = \gamma \psi_ {1} ^ {\infty} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k + 2} r _ {1} ^ {2} ] = \gamma c _ {2 k + 2, 0} ^ {\infty} \psi_ {1} ^ {\infty} \delta_ {1} ^ {\infty} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last equality applies  $1^{(b)}$ . by the recursion (b.11) and identity (b.15), we have  $c_{2k+2,0} = c_{2k+1,0} = \gamma^{-1}\bar{c}_{2k+1,0}$ . we have also  $\mathbf{x}_1^{(0)} = \boldsymbol{\psi}_1\boldsymbol{\delta}_1$  and  $\mathbf{x}_1^{(j)} = 0$  for  $j \geq 1$ , because  $\boldsymbol{\phi}_1 = 0$ . then  $\mathbf{j}_1^{(2k+1)} = \bar{c}_{2k+1,0}\boldsymbol{\psi}_1\boldsymbol{\delta}_1$ , so the above is simply

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {1} \bar {r} _ {1} \right] = \mathbf {j} _ {1} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}.
$$

similarly, applying the above identity and also  $\mathbb{e}[\lambda_n^{2k}] = \bar{m}_{2k}^{\infty} = \bar{c}_{2k,0}^{\infty}$  by lemma b.1, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {1} ^ {2} ] = (\psi_ {1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {2} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} \bar {r} _ {1} ^ {2} ] + \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {\perp} ^ {2} ] \\ = \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, 0} ^ {\infty} (\psi_ {1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {2} \pmb {\delta} _ {1} ^ {\infty} + \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} ^ {\infty} \cdot (\pmb {\gamma} _ {1} ^ {\infty} - (\psi_ {1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {2} \pmb {\omega} _ {1} ^ {\infty}). \\ \end{array}
$$

identifying  $\omega_{1} = \gamma \kappa_{2}\delta_{1} = \bar{\kappa}_{2}\delta_{1}$ , applying  $\bar{c}_{2k + 1,0} = \bar{c}_{2k,0}\bar{\kappa}_{2} + \bar{c}_{2k,1}$  by (b.10), and then identifying  $\xi_1^{(0)} = \gamma_1$ ,  $\xi_1^{(1)} = \psi_1^2\delta_1$ , and  $\xi_1^{(j)} = 0$  for  $j\geq 2$ , this is

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {1} ^ {2} ] = (\bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, 0} ^ {\infty} - \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {\kappa} _ {2} ^ {\infty}) (\psi_ {1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {2} \pmb {\delta} _ {1} ^ {\infty} + \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} ^ {\infty} \pmb {\gamma} _ {1} ^ {\infty} = \bar {c} _ {2 k, 1} ^ {\infty} \pmb {\xi} _ {1} ^ {(1, \infty)} + \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} ^ {\infty} \pmb {\xi} _ {1} ^ {(0, \infty)} = \mathbf {l} _ {1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}.
$$

this shows  $1^{(e)}$ .

for  $1^{(f)}$ , we now condition on  $\mathbf{u}_1, \mathbf{r}_1, \mathbf{z}_1, \mathbf{v}_1, \mathbf{p}_1, \boldsymbol{\lambda}, \mathbf{e}, \mathbf{f}$ . then the law of  $\mathbf{o}$  is conditioned on the event  $\mathbf{r}_1 = \mathbf{o} \mathbf{u}_1$ . by assumption,  $\mathbb{e}[u_1^2] > 0$ , so  $m^{-1} \mathbf{u}_1^\top \mathbf{u}_1 \neq 0$  for all large  $m$ . then the conditional law of  $\mathbf{o}$  is

$$
\mathbf {r} _ {1} \left(\mathbf {u} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {1}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {u} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \pi_ {\mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\bot}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} \pi_ {\mathbf {u} _ {1} ^ {\bot}} ^ {\top}.
$$

so the update for  $\mathbf{q}_1 = \mathbf{o}^\top \pmb{\lambda}\mathbf{p}_1$  may be replaced by

$$
\mathbf {q} _ {1} = \mathbf {q} _ {\parallel} + \mathbf {q} _ {\perp}, \quad \mathbf {q} _ {\parallel} = \mathbf {u} _ {1} (\mathbf {u} _ {1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {1}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1}, \quad \mathbf {q} _ {\perp} = \pi_ {\mathbf {u} _ {1} ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} ^ {\top} \pi_ {\mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1}.
$$

applying  $1^{(e)}$  and the above observation  $\mathbf{j}_1^{(1)} = \bar{c}_{1,0}\psi_1\pmb {\delta}_1 = \gamma \kappa_2\psi_1\pmb {\delta}_1$ , we have

$$
\frac {1}{m} \mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} = \frac {n}{m} \cdot \frac {1}{n} \mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} \to \gamma^ {- 1} \mathbf {j} _ {1} ^ {(1, \infty)} = \kappa_ {2} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {1} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {1} ^ {\infty} = a _ {1 1} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {1} ^ {\infty},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{m} \left\| \pi_ {\mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} \right\| ^ {2} = \frac {n}{m} \cdot \frac {1}{n} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} \right\| ^ {2} - \frac {\left(\mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1}\right) ^ {2}}{\left\| \mathbf {r} _ {1} \right\| ^ {2}}\right) \\ \rightarrow \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \left(\mathbf {l} _ {1} ^ {(2, \infty)} - \frac {(\mathbf {j} _ {1} ^ {(1 , \infty)}) ^ {2}}{\gamma \cdot \pmb {\delta} _ {1} ^ {\infty}}\right) = \left(\gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \left(\bar {c} _ {2, 1} \pmb {\xi} _ {1} ^ {(1)} + \bar {c} _ {2, 0} \pmb {\xi} _ {1} ^ {(0)} - \gamma \kappa_ {2} ^ {2} \pmb {\psi} _ {1} ^ {2} \pmb {\delta} _ {1}\right)\right) ^ {\infty}. \\ \end{array}
$$

recalling from the above that  $\xi_1^{(1)} = \psi_1^2\pmb{\delta}_1$ , and applying  $\gamma^{-1}\bar{c}_{2,1} = \gamma^{-1}(\bar{c}_{1,0}\kappa_2 + \bar{c}_{1,1}) = \kappa_2^2 +\kappa_4$  and  $\gamma^{-1}\bar{c}_{2,0} = \gamma^{-1}\bar{c}_{1,0} = \kappa_2$ , this yields

$$
\frac {1}{m} \left\| \pi_ {\mathbf {r} _ {1} ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} \right\| ^ {2} \rightarrow \kappa_ {4} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {1} ^ {(1, \infty)} + \kappa_ {2} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {1} ^ {(0, \infty)} = \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {1} ^ {\infty}. \tag {b.41}
$$

so  $\mathbf{q}_{\perp} \to q_{\perp} \sim \mathcal{n}(0, \sigma_1^\infty)$  where this is independent of  $(u_1, e)$ , and

$$
\left(\mathbf {q} _ {1}, \mathbf {u} _ {1}, \mathbf {e}\right) \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} \left(a _ {1 1} ^ {\infty} u _ {1} + q _ {\perp}, u _ {1}, e\right).
$$

then applying  $\mathbf{y}_1 = \mathbf{q}_1 - a_{11}\mathbf{u}_1$ ,  $a_{11} \to a_{11}^{\infty}$  by 1 $^{(d)}$ , and propositions e.4 and e.2,

$$
\left(\mathbf {u} _ {1}, \mathbf {u} _ {2}, \mathbf {y} _ {1}, \mathbf {e}\right) \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} \left(u _ {1}, u _ {2}, y _ {1}, e\right)
$$

where  $y_{1} = q_{\perp}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,\sigma_{1}^{\infty})$  and  $u_{2} = u_{2}(y_{1},e)$ . this yields  $1^{(f)}$ .

for  $1^{(g)}$ , observe first that  $\pmb{\delta}_{1}^{\infty} = \mathbb{e}[u_{1}^{2}] > 0$ ,  $\phi_{1} = 0$ , and  $\omega_1^\infty = \gamma \kappa_2^\infty \mathbb{e}[u_1^2 ] > 0$  by the given assumptions. the schur-complement  $\gamma_1^\infty -(\psi_1^\infty)^2\omega_1^\infty$  in the second matrix of  $1^{(g)}$  is the residual variance of projecting of  $v_{1}$  onto the span of  $z_{1}$ , which is positive by assumption 5.2(f), so the second matrix of  $1^{(g)}$  is invertible. by (b.40), this shows also that

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ p _ {\perp} \right] > 0.
$$
