for the first matrix of  $1^{(g)}$ , it remains to show that  $\pmb{\sigma}_{1}^{\infty} > 0$ . note that by (b.41),  $\pmb{\sigma}_{1}^{\infty}$  is the residual variance of projecting  $\bar{p}_1$  onto the span of  $r_{1}$ . if this were 0, then  $\bar{p}_1 = \alpha r_1$  for some constant  $\alpha \in \mathbb{r}$  with probability 1. applying  $1^{(b)}$  and  $1^{(e)}$ , we then have

$$
0 = \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2} (\bar {p} _ {1} - \alpha r _ {1}) ^ {2} ] = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} m ^ {- 1} \| \pmb {\lambda} ^ {\top} \pmb {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} - \alpha \pmb {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1} \| ^ {2} = \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (\lambda_ {n} ^ {2} p _ {1} - \alpha \bar {r} _ {1}) ^ {2} ],
$$

so also  $\lambda_n^2 p_1 = \alpha \bar{r}_1$  with probability 1. recalling  $p_1 = \psi_1^\infty \cdot \bar{r}_1 + p_\perp$ , this shows  $\lambda_n^2 p_\perp = (\alpha - \psi_1^\infty \cdot \lambda_n^2) \bar{r}_1$ . since  $\lambda_n^2$  is not identically 0, and  $p_\perp$  is independent of  $(\lambda_n, \bar{r}_1)$ , we must then have that  $p_\perp$  is constant with probability 1, but this contradicts that  $p_\perp \sim \mathcal{n}(0, \mathrm{var}[p_\perp])$  where  $\mathrm{var}[p_\perp] > 0$  as argued above. so  $\sigma_1^\infty > 0$ , and the first matrix of  $1^{(g)}$  is also invertible.

step 2: analysis of  $\mathbf{r}_{t+1}$ . suppose that  $t^{(a-g)}$  holds. to show  $t+1^{(a)}$ , observe that the limits  $\pmb{\delta}_{t+1}^{\infty}$  and  $\pmb{\phi}_{t+1}^{\infty}$  exist by  $t^{(f)}$ , proposition e.3, and the given conditions for the functions  $\partial_{s'}u_s$ . furthermore, by  $t^{(d)}$  and  $t^{(f)}$ , the limits  $\theta_{t+1}^{(j,\infty)}$ ,  $\mathbf{b}_{t+1}^{\infty}$ ,  $\omega_{t+1}^{\infty}$ , and  $\mathbf{h}_{t+1}^{(2k,\infty)}$  also exist because these matrices do not depend on  $\mathbf{v}_{t+1}$  or its derivatives. each term constituting  $\mathbf{x}_{t+1}^{(j)}$  in (b.5) may be written as either  $\psi_{t+1}$  or  $\gamma_{t+1}\phi_{t+1}^{\top}$  times a matrix that depends only on  $\pmb{\delta}_{t+1}$ ,  $\pmb{\phi}_{t+1}\pmb{\psi}_{t+1}$ , and  $\pmb{\phi}_{t+1}\pmb{\gamma}_{t+1}\pmb{\phi}_{t+1}^{\top}$ . then the first  $t$  rows of  $\mathbf{x}_{t+1}^{(j)}$  also do not depend on  $\mathbf{v}_{t+1}$  or its derivatives, so  $\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_t^{(2k+1,\infty)}$  exists. this establishes  $t+1^{(a)}$ .

let us now show  $t + 1^{(b)}$ . define the matrices

$$
\mathbf {u} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} \mathbf {u} _ {1} & \dots & \mathbf {u} _ {t} \end{array} \right), \quad \mathbf {r} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} \mathbf {r} _ {1} & \dots & \mathbf {r} _ {t} \end{array} \right), \quad \mathbf {z} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} \mathbf {z} _ {1} & \dots & \mathbf {z} _ {t} \end{array} \right),
$$

$$
\mathbf {v} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} \mathbf {v} _ {1} & \dots & \mathbf {v} _ {t} \end{array} \right), \quad \mathbf {p} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} \mathbf {p} _ {1} & \dots & \mathbf {p} _ {t} \end{array} \right), \quad \mathbf {y} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} \mathbf {y} _ {1} & \dots & \mathbf {y} _ {t} \end{array} \right).
$$

conditional on the amp iterates up to  $\mathbf{u}_{t + 1}$ , the law of  $\mathbf{o}$  is conditioned on

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {r} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & - \mathbf {a} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) = \mathbf {o} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t} & \mathbf {y} _ {t} \end{array} \right).
$$

let us introduce

$$
\mathbf {m} _ {t} = m ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t} & \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {y} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t} & \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {y} _ {t} \end{array} \right),
$$

noting that by  $t^{(f)}, t^{(g)}$ , and proposition e.5,

$$
\mathbf {m} _ {t} \rightarrow \mathbf {m} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left(\begin{array}{c c}\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\infty}&\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\\\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top}&\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\end{array}\right)
$$

where  $\mathbf{m}_t^\infty$  is invertible. then by proposition f.1, for all large  $n$ , the conditional law of  $\mathbf{o}$  is

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {r} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & - \mathbf {a} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {m} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \cdot m ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) + \pi_ {(\mathbf {r} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} \pi_ {(\mathbf {u} _ {t}, \mathbf {y} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top}
$$

where  $\tilde{\mathbf{o}}\in \mathbb{r}^{(m - 2t)\times (m - 2t)}$  is an independent haar-orthogonal matrix. we may thus replace the update for  $\mathbf{r}_{t + 1}$  by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} = \mathbf {r} _ {\parallel} + \mathbf {r} _ {\perp} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {\parallel} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {r} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & - \mathbf {a} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {m} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \cdot m ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {\perp} = \pi_ {(\mathbf {r} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} \pi_ {(\mathbf {u} _ {t}, \mathbf {y} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $\mathbf{r}_{\parallel}$ , define

$$
\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbb {e} [ u _ {1} u _ {t + 1} ] \\ \vdots \\ \mathbb {e} [ u _ {t} u _ {t + 1} ] \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {t}, \qquad \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbb {e} [ \partial_ {1} u _ {t + 1} (y _ {1}, \ldots , y _ {t}, e) ] \\ \vdots \\ \mathbb {e} [ \partial_ {t} u _ {t + 1} (y _ {1}, \ldots , y _ {t}, e) ] \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {t},
$$

which are the last columns of  $\pmb{\delta}_{t + 1}^{\infty}$  and  $(\phi_{t + 1}^{\infty})^{\top}$  with their last entries removed. observe that

$$
m ^ {- 1} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} \rightarrow \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \qquad m ^ {- 1} \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} \rightarrow \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

then by arguments similar to the proof of theorem 4.3,

$$
\mathbf {r} _ {\parallel} \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} r _ {\parallel} = \left(r _ {1} \quad \dots \quad r _ {t} \quad \bar {p} _ {1} \quad \dots \quad \bar {p} _ {t}\right)\left(\boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \binom {\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\infty}} {\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}} \tag {b.42}
$$

and  $\mathbf{\upsilon}_t^\infty$  is the limit of  $\mathbf{\upsilon}_t$  defined in (b.25). also,

$$
\mathbf {r} _ {\perp} \xrightarrow {w} r _ {\perp} \sim \mathcal {n} \left(0, \mathbb {e} [ u _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] - \left(\left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}\right)
$$

where this limit  $r_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(r_1,\ldots ,r_t,\bar{p}_1,\ldots ,\bar{p}_t,\lambda_m)$ . so

$$
\left(\mathbf {r} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m}\right) \xrightarrow {w} \left(r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t + 1}, \bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m}\right), \quad r _ {t + 1} = r _ {| |} + r _ {\perp}. \tag {b.44}
$$

we have

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ r _ {\perp} \right] > 0 \tag {b.45}
$$

because this is the residual variance of projecting  $u_{t + 1}$  onto the span of  $(u_1, \ldots, u_t, y_1, \ldots, y_t)$ , which is positive by assumption 5.2(f).

let us now introduce the block notation

$$
\mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)} = \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)} & \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)} \\ (\mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}) ^ {\top} & h _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)} \end{array} \right), \quad \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)} = \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)} & \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)} \end{array} \right). \tag {b.46}
$$

to conclude the proof of  $t + 1^{(b)}$ , it remains to show that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} \right] = \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)} \tag {b.47}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} \right] = \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)} \tag {b.48}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} ^ {2} \right] = h _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}. \tag {b.49}
$$

for (b.47), observe that by  $t^{(b)}$  and  $t^{(e)}$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t}) ] = \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} (\bar {p} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {p} _ {t}) ] = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} \frac {1}{m} \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top}) ^ {2 k} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} \frac {1}{n} \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {\lambda} (\mathbf {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {\lambda}) ^ {2 k} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} \left(p _ {1}, \dots , p _ {t}\right) \right] \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)} = (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}) ^ {\top}, \\ \end{array}
$$

the last equality applying (b.7). then applying (b.44), (b.42), and the independence of  $r_{\perp}$  from  $(r_1, \ldots, r_t, \bar{p}_1, \ldots, \bar{p}_t, \lambda_m)$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} ] = \left(\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {2 k} & (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {2 k + 1}) ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}.
$$

applying the first row of the identity (b.26), and the definitions of  $\theta_{t + 1}^{(j)}$ ,  $\mathbf{x}_{t + 1}^{(j)}$ , and  $\mathbf{h}_{t + 1}^{(2k)}$  from (5.9), (b.5), and (b.6),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} \right] = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \left(\phi_ {t} \psi_ {t}\right) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {\infty} \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top}\right) _ {1: t, t + 1} ^ {\infty} \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) _ {1: t, t + 1} ^ {\infty} = \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

for (b.48), observe that also by  $t^{(e)}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ (\bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} (\bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t}) ] = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} \frac {1}{m} \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top}) ^ {2 k} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} \frac {1}{n} \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\mathbf {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {\lambda}) ^ {2 k + 2} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (p _ {1}, \ldots , p _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k + 2} (p _ {1}, \ldots , p _ {t}) ] = \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2, \infty)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

so

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (\bar {p} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {p} _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} ] = \left(\left(\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} \quad \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2)}\right) \mathbf {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}.
$$

applying the second row of the identity (b.26),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ (\bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} ] = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right) ^ {\infty} \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\psi_ {t + 1} \phi_ {t + 1}\right) ^ {j} \psi_ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)} \phi_ {t + 1} ^ {\top}\right) _ {1: t, t + 1} ^ {\infty} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
= \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k + 1, j} \mathbf {x} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) _ {1: t, t + 1} ^ {\infty} = \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}.
$$

for (b.49), applying again (b.44) and a computation similar to the proof of theorem 4.3, we have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \left(\left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {- 1}) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} & (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} & \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2)} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty} + \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {\perp} ^ {2} ]
$$

where, by independence of  $r_{\perp}$  and  $\lambda_{m}$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {\perp} ^ {2} ] = c _ {2 k, 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\mathbb {e} [ u _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] - \left(\left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {- 1}) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(0)} & (\mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {- 1} (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t})\right) ^ {\infty}\right).
$$

combining these and applying the identity (b.27),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} r _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] \\ = c _ {2 k, 0} ^ {\infty} \mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {t + 1} ^ {2} \right] + \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j + 1} \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {\infty} \\ = \left(c _ {2 k, 0} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} + \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \big (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} + (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} + \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j - 1)} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \big)\right) _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {\infty} \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} c _ {2 k, j} \theta_ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {\infty} = h _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

this completes the proof of  $t + 1^{(b)}$ .

let us make here the following additional observation: this also shows

$$
\left(\mathbf {p} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {p} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \xrightarrow {w} \left(p _ {1}, \dots , p _ {t}, \bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \tag {b.50}
$$

for a certain limit  $\bar{r}_{t + 1}$ , which is part of the claim in  $t + 1^{(e)}$ . this is because from the decomposition  $\mathbf{r}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{r}_{\parallel} + \mathbf{r}_{\perp}$ , we have  $\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbf{r}_{t + 1} = \boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbf{r}_{\parallel} + \boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbf{r}_{\perp}$ . from the form of  $\mathbf{r}_{\parallel}$  and claim  $t^{(e)}$ , we have

$$
\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {\parallel} \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} \bar {r} _ {\parallel} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c c c c} \bar {r} _ {1} & \dots & \bar {r} _ {t} & \lambda_ {n} ^ {2} p _ {1} & \dots & \lambda_ {n} ^ {2} p _ {t} \end{array} \right) (\mathbf {\upsilon} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \\ \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \end{array} \right).
$$

for  $\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbf{r}_{\perp}$ , let us define  $\check{\mathbf{r}}_{\perp} \in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  to be the first  $n$  entries of  $\mathbf{r}_{\perp}$  if  $n \leq m$ , or  $\mathbf{r}_{\perp}$  extended by an additional  $n - m$  i.i.d.  $\mathcal{n}(0, \mathrm{var}[r_{\perp}])$  variables if  $n > m$ . by proposition f.2(b),  $\check{\mathbf{r}}_{\perp} \xrightarrow{w} \check{r}_{\perp}$  in both cases, where this limit  $\check{r}_{\perp}$  has the same normal law as  $r_{\perp}$  above, and is independent of  $(p_{1}, \ldots, p_{t}, \bar{r}_{1}, \ldots, \bar{r}_{t}, \lambda_{n})$ . since  $\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbf{r}_{\perp}$  is the entrywise product of  $\lambda_{n}$  with  $\check{\mathbf{r}}_{\perp}$ , this shows that (b.50) holds where

$$
\bar {r} _ {t + 1} = \bar {r} _ {\parallel} + \lambda_ {n} \check {r} _ {\perp}.
$$

furthermore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ (p _ {1}, \ldots , p _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} (\bar {r} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}) ] = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda}) ^ {2 k} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \\ = \gamma \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (\bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k} (r _ {1}, \dots , r _ {t + 1}) ] \\ = \gamma \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}, \tag {b.51} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (\bar {r} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} (\bar {r} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}) ] = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} (\boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda}) ^ {k} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \gamma \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t + 1}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2 k + 2} (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t + 1}) ] \\ = \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k + 2, \infty)}. \tag {b.52} \\ \end{array}
$$

step 3: analysis of  $\mathbf{z}_{t+1}$ . we now show  $t + 1^{(c)}$ . conditioning on the iterates up to  $\mathbf{r}_{t+1}$ , the law of  $\mathbf{q}$  is conditioned on

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {p} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & - \mathbf {b} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) = \mathbf {q} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {v} _ {t} & \mathbf {z} _ {t} \end{array} \right)
$$

set

$$
\mathbf {n} _ {t} = n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t} & \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {z} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t} & \mathbf {z} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {t} \end{array} \right),
$$

and note that by  $t^{(c)}$ ,  $t^{(g)}$ , and proposition e.5,

$$
\mathbf {n} _ {t} \rightarrow \mathbf {n} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left(\begin{array}{c c}\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}&\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\\\boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top}&\boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\end{array}\right)
$$

where  $\mathbf{n}_t^\infty$  is invertible. thus, for all large  $n$ , the conditional law of  $\mathbf{q}$  is

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {p} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & - \mathbf {b} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {n} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {z} _ {t} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) + \pi_ {(\mathbf {p} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {q}} \pi_ {(\mathbf {v} _ {t}, \mathbf {z} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top}
$$

where  $\tilde{\mathbf{q}}\in \mathbb{r}^{(n - 2t)\times (n - 2t)}$  is an independent haar-orthogonal matrix. so we may replace the update for  $\mathbf{s}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{q}^\top \boldsymbol {\lambda}^\top \mathbf{r}_{t + 1}$  by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {s} _ {t + 1} = \mathbf {s} _ {| |} + \mathbf {s} _ {\perp} \\ \mathbf {s} _ {\parallel} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {v} _ {t} & \mathbf {z} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {n} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & \mathbf {0} \\ - \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \\ \mathbf {s} _ {\perp} = \pi_ {(\mathbf {v} _ {t}, \mathbf {z} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {q}} ^ {\top} \pi_ {(\mathbf {p} _ {t}, \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \\ \end{array}
$$

applying  $t + 1^{(b)}$  shown above, and recalling the block notation (b.46), observe that

$$
\begin{array}{l} n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \rightarrow \gamma \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (\bar {p} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {p} _ {t}) ^ {\top} r _ {t + 1} ] = \gamma \cdot \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1, \infty)} \\ n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \rightarrow \gamma \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {m} ^ {2} r _ {t + 1} ] = \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2, \infty)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

then by a computation analogous to the proof of theorem 4.3,

$$
\mathbf {n} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & \mathbf {0} \\ - \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \to \left(\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & 0 \\ 0 & \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \end{array} \right) (\mathbf {t} _ {t} ^ {- 1}) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \gamma \cdot \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}
$$

where  $\mathbf{t}_t$  is as defined in (b.25). applying the second row of the identity (b.28) with  $t + 1$  and with  $k = 0$ , and recalling  $\bar{c}_{1,j} = \bar{\kappa}_{2(j + 1)} = \gamma \cdot \kappa_{2(j + 1)}$ , we have

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c} (\mathbf {j} _ {t + 1} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \boldsymbol {\gamma} \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) = \mathbf {t} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\gamma} \cdot \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\gamma} \cdot \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) = \mathbf {t} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right).
$$

writing the block forms

$$
\mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {b} _ {t} & \mathbf {b} _ {t} \\ 0 & 0 \end{array} \right), \qquad \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1, t + 1} \end{array} \right),
$$

and applying  $(\mathbf{j}_{t + 1}^{(1)})^\top = \gamma \cdot \mathbf{i}_{t + 1}^{(1)}$ , this yields

$$
\gamma \cdot \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) = \left(\mathbf {t} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {b} _ {t + 1} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right)\right) _ {(1: t) \cup (t + 2: 2 t + 1), t + 1} = \mathbf {t} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {b} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} \end{array} \right),
$$

where the second equality follows because  $\mathbf{b}_{t + 1}$  is 0 in its lower-right entry while  $\mathbf{t}_{t + 1}^{\top}$  is 0 in rows  $1:t$  and  $t + 2:2t + 1$  of its last column. inverting  $\mathbf{t}_t^\top$  and applying this above,

$$
\mathbf {s} _ {\parallel} \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} s _ {\parallel} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} v _ {1} & \dots & v _ {t} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + \left( \begin{array}{c c c} z _ {1} & \dots & z _ {t} \end{array} \right) (\boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

similar to the proof of theorem 4.3, we have also

$$
\mathbf {s} _ {\perp} \xrightarrow {w} s _ {\perp} \sim \mathcal {n} \left(0, \left(\gamma \cdot h _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2)} - \binom {\gamma \cdot \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)}} {\gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)}} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(0)} & (\mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {j} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) ^ {- 1} \binom {\gamma \cdot \mathbf {i} _ {t} ^ {(1)}} {\gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)}}\right) ^ {\infty}\right)
$$

where  $s_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(v_{1},\ldots ,v_{t},z_{1},\ldots ,z_{t})$  . so

$$
\mathbf {s} _ {t + 1} \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} s _ {t + 1} = \left(\begin{array}{l l l}v _ {1}&\dots&v _ {t}\end{array}\right) \mathbf {b} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + \left(\begin{array}{l l l}z _ {1}&\dots&z _ {t}\end{array}\right)\left(\boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + s _ {\perp}. \tag {b.53}
$$

since  $\mathbf{z}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{s}_{t + 1} - \mathbf{v}_t\mathbf{b}_t$  , this yields

$$
\left(\mathbf {v} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {v} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {z} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {f}\right) \stackrel {{w}} {{\rightarrow}} \left(v _ {1}, \dots , v _ {t + 1}, z _ {1}, \dots , z _ {t + 1}, f\right)
$$

where  $v_{t + 1} = v_{t + 1}(z_1,\ldots ,z_{t + 1},f)$  and

$$
z _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} z _ {1} & \dots & z _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left(\omega_ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \omega_ {t} ^ {\infty} + s _ {\perp}.
$$

thus  $(z_{1},\ldots ,z_{t},z_{t + 1})$  has a multivariate normal distribution. to compute the covariance, observe that since  $s_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(z_{1},\ldots ,z_{t})$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(z _ {1}, \dots , z _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} z _ {t + 1} \right] = \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \left(\boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

for  $\mathbb{e}[z_{t + 1}^2]$ , squaring both sides of (b.53), applying

$$
\mathbb {e} [ s _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {s} _ {t + 1} \| ^ {2} = \lim _ {m, n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \| \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \| ^ {2} = \gamma \cdot h _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2, \infty)},
$$

and rearranging yields

$$
\mathbb {e} [ z _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \left(\left(\gamma \cdot {\bf h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2)} - {\bf b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} {\boldsymbol \gamma} _ {t + 1} {\bf b} _ {t + 1} - {\bf b} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} {\boldsymbol \psi} _ {t + 1} {\boldsymbol \omega} _ {t + 1} - {\boldsymbol \omega} _ {t + 1} {\boldsymbol \psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} {\bf b} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {\infty}\right) _ {t + 1, t + 1}.
$$

applying the identity (b.23), this gives  $\mathbb{e}[z_{t + 1}^2] = \omega_{t + 1,t + 1}^\infty$ , and this concludes the proof of  $t + 1^{(c)}$ . let us make here the following additional observation: we have

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ s _ {\perp} \right] > 0 \tag {b.54}
$$

above. this is because by (b.51) and (b.52) for  $k = 0$ , the quantity  $\mathrm{var}[s_{\perp}]$  above may be seen to be the residual variance of projecting  $\bar{r}_{t + 1}$  onto the linear span of  $(p_1, \dots, p_t, \bar{r}_1, \dots, \bar{r}_t)$ . if this residual variance were 0, then for some constants  $\alpha_1, \dots, \alpha_t, \beta_1, \dots, \beta_t$  we would have  $\bar{r}_{t + 1} = \alpha_1 p_1 + \dots + \alpha_t p_t + \beta_1 \bar{r}_1 + \dots + \beta_t \bar{r}_t$  with probability 1, so that

$$
\begin{array}{l} 0 = \mathbb {e} \left[ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2} \cdot \left(\bar {r} _ {t + 1} - \alpha_ {1} p _ {1} - \dots - \alpha_ {t} p _ {t} - \beta_ {1} \bar {r} _ {1} - \dots - \beta_ {t} \bar {r} _ {t}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ = \lim  _ {m, n \rightarrow \infty} n ^ {- 1} \| \boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} - \alpha_ {1} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} - \dots - \alpha_ {t} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} - \beta_ {1} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1} - \dots - \beta_ {t} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \\ = \gamma \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (\lambda_ {m} ^ {2} r _ {t + 1} - \alpha_ {1} \bar {p} _ {1} - \ldots - \alpha_ {t} \bar {p} _ {t} - \beta_ {1} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2} r _ {1} - \ldots - \beta_ {t} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2} r _ {t}) ^ {2} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

thus also

$$
\lambda_ {m} ^ {2} r _ {t + 1} = \alpha_ {1} \bar {p} _ {1} + \ldots + \alpha_ {t} \bar {p} _ {t} + \beta_ {1} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2} r _ {1} + \ldots + \beta_ {t} \lambda_ {m} ^ {2} r _ {t}
$$

with probability 1. however, recall the decomposition  $r_{t+1} = r_{\parallel} + r_{\perp}$  where  $r_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(r_1, \ldots, r_t, \bar{p}_1, \ldots, \bar{p}_t, \lambda_m)$ . thus we have

$$
\lambda_ {m} ^ {2} r _ {\perp} = f (r _ {1}, \ldots , r _ {t}, \bar {p} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {p} _ {t}, \lambda_ {m})
$$

where the quantity on the right does not depend on  $r_{\perp}$ . since  $\lambda_{m}$  is not identically 0 by the condition  $\mathrm{var}[\lambda] > 0$  in assumption 5.2(f), this implies that  $r_{\perp}$  must be constant almost surely,

contradicting (b.45) already shown. thus, (b.54) holds.

step 4: analysis of  $\mathbf{p}_{t + 1}$ . note that  $t^{(f)}, t + 1^{(c)}$ , and the given conditions for the functions  $\partial_{s'}v_s$  imply the existence of all limits in  $t + 1^{(d)}$ . let us now show  $t + 1^{(e)}$ . the joint convergence with  $\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{\top}\mathbf{r}_{t + 1}$  has been established already in (b.50), so we proceed to analyze  $\mathbf{p}_{t + 1}$ .

for this, let

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {b} _ {t} & \mathbf {b} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {t \times (t + 1)}, \qquad \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} & \mathbf {0} \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {t \times (t + 1)}
$$

be the first  $t$  rows of  $\mathbf{b}_{t + 1}$  and  $\psi_{t + 1}$ , and let

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {a} _ {t} \\ 0 \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {(t + 1) \times t}, \qquad \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t} \\ (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {(t + 1) \times t}
$$

be the first  $t$  columns of  $\mathbf{a}_{t + 1}$  and  $\phi_{t + 1}$ . conditional on the iterates up to  $\mathbf{v}_{t + 1}$ , the law of  $\mathbf{q}$  is now conditioned on

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {p} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) = \mathbf {q} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {v} _ {t} & \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right).
$$

let us denote

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {n}} _ {t} = n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t} & \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} \\ \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t} & \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right),
$$

noting that by  $t + 1^{(c)}$  and proposition e.5,

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {n}} _ {t} \rightarrow \tilde {\mathbf {n}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left(\begin{array}{c c}\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}&\tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}\\\boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top}&\boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}\end{array}\right). \tag {b.55}
$$

the upper-left  $2t \times 2t$  submatrix of  $\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_t^\infty$  is  $\mathbf{n}_t^\infty$ , which is invertible by  $t^{(g)}$ . the schur-complement of its lower-right entry is the residual variance of projecting  $z_{t+1}$  onto the span of  $(v_1, \ldots, v_t, z_1, \ldots, z_t)$ . since  $\mathbf{z}_{t+1} = \mathbf{s}_{t+1} - \mathbf{v}_t \mathbf{b}_t$ , this is the same as the residual variance of projecting  $s_{t+1}$  onto the span of  $(v_1, \ldots, v_t, z_1, \ldots, z_t)$ , which is exactly  $\operatorname{var}[s_\perp]$  by the convergence (b.53) and the fact that  $s_\perp$  is a mean-zero variable independent of  $(v_1, \ldots, v_t, z_1, \ldots, z_t)$ . this was shown to be non-zero in (b.54), so  $\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_t^\infty$  is invertible. thus for all large  $n$ , the conditional law of  $\mathbf{q}$  is

$$
\left(\mathbf {p} _ {t} \quad \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}\right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {n}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) + \pi_ {(\mathbf {p} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {q}} \pi_ {(\mathbf {v} _ {t}, \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top}
$$

so we may replace the update for  $\mathbf{p}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{q}\mathbf{v}_{t + 1}$  by

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {t + 1} = \mathbf {p} _ {\parallel} + \mathbf {p} _ {\perp}
$$

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {\parallel} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {p} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {n}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \cdot n ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) \mathbf {v} _ {t + 1}
$$

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {\perp} = \pi_ {\left(\mathbf {p} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {q}} \pi_ {\left(\mathbf {v} _ {t}, \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t + 1}
$$

for  $\mathbf{p}_{\parallel}$ , define

$$
\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbb {e} [ v _ {1} v _ {t + 1} ] \\ \vdots \\ \mathbb {e} [ v _ {t} v _ {t + 1} ] \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {t}, \qquad \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbb {e} [ \partial_ {1} v _ {t + 1} (z _ {1}, \ldots , z _ {t + 1}, f) ] \\ \vdots \\ \mathbb {e} [ \partial_ {t + 1} v _ {t + 1} (z _ {1}, \ldots , z _ {t + 1}, f) ] \end{array} \right) \in \mathbb {r} ^ {t + 1}
$$

and observe that

$$
n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t + 1} \to \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}, \qquad n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {z} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t + 1} \to \omega_ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

then by a computation similar to the proof of  $t + 1^{(b)}$  above,

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {\parallel} \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} \left(\begin{array}{l l l l l l}p _ {1}&\ldots&p _ {t}&\bar {r} _ {1}&\ldots&\bar {r} _ {t + 1}\end{array}\right) (\tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {- 1} \left(\begin{array}{l}\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\\\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\end{array}\right)
$$

where

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} + \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \\ \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \tilde {\mathbf {b}} _ {t} \end{array} \right). \tag {b.56}
$$

also,

$$
\mathbf {p} _ {\perp} \xrightarrow {w} p _ {\perp} \sim \mathcal {n} \left(0, \mathbb {e} [ v _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] - \left(\left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} & \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}\right)
$$

where  $p_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(p_{1},\ldots ,p_{t},\bar{r}_{1},\ldots ,\bar{r}_{t + 1},\lambda_{n})$  . so

$$
\left(\mathbf {p} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {p} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right) \xrightarrow {w} \left(p _ {1}, \dots , p _ {t + 1}, \bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {n}\right), \quad p _ {t + 1} = p _ {\parallel} + p _ {\perp}. \tag {b.57}
$$

we have

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ p _ {\perp} \right] > 0 \tag {b.58}
$$

because this is the residual variance of projecting  $v_{t + 1}$  onto the span of  $(v_{1},\ldots ,v_{t},z_{1},\ldots ,z_{t + 1})$ , which is positive by assumption 5.2(f).

let us introduce the block notation

$$
\mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k)} = \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} & \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} \\ (\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)}) ^ {\top} & l _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2 k)} \end{array} \right), \quad \mathbf {j} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k + 1)} = \left(\gamma \cdot \left(\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}\right) ^ {\top} \quad \tilde {\mathbf {j}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}\right) \tag {b.59}
$$

where  $\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_t^{(2k + 1)}\in \mathbb{r}^{t\times (t + 1)}$  forms the first  $t$  rows of  $\mathbf{i}_{t + 1}^{(2k + 1)}$  as previously defined, and thus  $\gamma \cdot (\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_t^{(2k + 1)})^\top$  forms the first  $t$  columns of  $\mathbf{j}_{t + 1}^{(2k + 1)}$  by the identity (b.7). to conclude the proof of  $t + 1^{(e)}$ , it remains to show that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} \right] = \tilde {\mathbf {j}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)} \tag {b.60}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(p _ {1}, \dots , p _ {t}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} \right] = \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k, \infty)} \tag {b.61}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} ^ {2} \right] = l _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}. \tag {b.62}
$$

the arguments are similar to those for  $t + 1^{(b)}$ : for (b.60), by the convergence (b.57), the form of  $p_{\parallel}$ , and the identities (b.51-b.52), we have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (\bar {r} _ {1}, \ldots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} ] = \left(\left(\gamma \cdot (\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} \right. \quad \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k + 2)}\right) \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left(\begin{array}{c}\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t}\\\tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t}\end{array}\right)\left. \right) ^ {\infty}.
$$

applying the second row of the identity (b.28),

$$
\left(\mathbf {j} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k + 1)} \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k + 2)}\right) = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) \mathbf {t} _ {t + 1}.
$$

note that  $\tilde{\mathbf{t}}_t$  defined in (b.56) is the submatrix of  $\mathbf{t}_{t + 1}$  with row and column  $t + 1$  removed. furthermore, column  $t + 1$  of  $\phi_{t + 1}$  is 0. thus, removing column  $t + 1$  from both sides of this identity yields

$$
\left(\gamma \cdot (\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k + 2)}\right) = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1}) ^ {j} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t}.
$$

taking the limit  $m, n \to \infty$ , inverting  $\tilde{\mathbf{t}}_t^\infty$ , and applying this above,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} \right] = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1}\right) ^ {j} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} + \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {\infty} \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k + 1, j} \left(\mathbf {x} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) ^ {\top}\right) _ {1: t + 1, t + 1} ^ {\infty} = \tilde {\mathbf {j}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1, \infty)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

here, the last two equalities apply again the fact that the last column of  $\phi_{t+1}$  is 0, and the definitions of  $\mathbf{x}_{t+1}^{(j)}$  and  $\mathbf{j}_{t+1}^{(2k+1)}$  in (b.5) and (b.6).

for (b.61), we have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (p _ {1}, \ldots , p _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} ] = \left(\left(\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} \quad \gamma \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}\right) \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \\ \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}.
$$

applying the first row of the identity (b.28) with  $t + 1$ , and a similar argument of removing the  $t + 1^{\text{th}}$  rows and columns from both sides, we obtain

$$
\left(\mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} \gamma \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}\right) = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} \tilde {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {(j)}\right) \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t}
$$

where  $\tilde{\mathbf{x}}_t^{(j)}$  are the first  $t$  rows of  $\mathbf{x}_{t + 1}^{(j)}$ . then

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (p _ {1}, \ldots , p _ {t}) ^ {\top} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} ] = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} (\bar {c} _ {2 k, j} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t}) ^ {j} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} \tilde {\mathbf {x}} _ {t} ^ {(j)} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t})\right) ^ {\infty} = \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}.
$$

for (b.62), squaring both sides of (b.57) and recalling  $\mathbb{e}[\lambda_n^{2k}] = \bar{m}_{2k}^{\infty} = \bar{c}_{2k,0}^{\infty}$  from lemma b.1, we have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \left(\binom {\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t}} {\tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t}} ^ {\top} (\tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {- 1}) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} & \boldsymbol {\gamma} \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} \\ \boldsymbol {\gamma} \cdot (\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\gamma} \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 2)} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \binom {\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t}} {\tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {\infty} + \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {\perp} ^ {2} ],
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {\perp} ^ {2} ] = \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\mathbb {e} [ v _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] - \binom {\gamma t} {\tilde {\psi} _ {t}} ^ {\top} (\tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {- 1}) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(0)} & \gamma \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \gamma \cdot (\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} & \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \binom {\gamma t} {\tilde {\psi} _ {t}}\right) ^ {\infty}.
$$

applying (b.29) with  $t + 1$ , and removing the  $t + 1^{\text{th}}$  rows and columns from both sides, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2 k)} & \gamma \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)} \\ \gamma \cdot (\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(2 k + 1)}) ^ {\top} & \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2 k + 2)} \end{array} \right) - \bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(0)} & \gamma \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \gamma \cdot (\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} & \gamma \cdot \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) \\ = \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} 0 & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} \tilde {\phi} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1}) ^ {j} \tilde {\phi} _ {t} & \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {t}} _ {t}. \\ \end{array}
$$

then combining the above,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \lambda_ {n} ^ {2 k} p _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] \\ = \left(\bar {c} _ {2 k, 0} \mathbb {e} [ v _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j + 1} (\boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} ^ {\top} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} + \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1}) ^ {j} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} + \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {t})\right) ^ {\infty} \\ = \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \bar {c} _ {2 k, j} \xi_ {t + 1} ^ {(j)}\right) _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {\infty} = l _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2 k, \infty)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

this concludes the proof of  $t + 1^{(e)}$ .

step 5: analysis of  $\mathbf{y}_{t + 1}$ . finally, let us show  $t + 1^{(f)}$  and  $t + 1^{(g)}$ . conditional on the iterates up to  $\mathbf{p}_{t + 1}$ , the law of  $\mathbf{o}$  is now conditioned on

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) = \mathbf {o} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {y} _ {t} \end{array} \right).
$$

let us set

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} = m ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {y} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {y} _ {t} \end{array} \right),
$$

noting that by  $t + 1^{(c)}$

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} \to \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} & \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \tilde {\boldsymbol {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \end{array} \right).
$$

this limit is invertible because its submatrix removing row and column  $t + 1$  is  $\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_t^\infty$  which is invertible by  $t^{(g)}$ , and the schur complement of the  $(t + 1, t + 1)$  entry is exactly  $\operatorname{var}[r_{\perp}]$  from (b.43), which we have shown is non-zero in (b.45). then for all large  $n$ , the conditional law of  $\mathbf{o}$  is

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} & \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathrm {i d} & - \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} \\ \mathbf {0} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \cdot m ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) + \pi_ {(\mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} \pi_ {(\mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {y} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top}
$$

so we may replace the update for  $\mathbf{q}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{o}^{\top}\mathbf{a}\mathbf{p}_{t + 1}$  by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {q} _ {t + 1} = \mathbf {q} _ {\parallel} + \mathbf {q} _ {\perp} \\ \mathbf {q} _ {\parallel} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {y} _ {t} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \operatorname {i d} & \mathbf {0} \\ - \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \operatorname {i d} \end{array} \right) \cdot m ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t + 1} \\ \mathbf {q} _ {\perp} = \pi_ {(\mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {y} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} \tilde {\mathbf {o}} ^ {\top} \pi_ {(\mathbf {r} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t}) ^ {\perp}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t + 1} \\ \end{array}
$$

setting

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {\delta} _ {t + 1} & \mathbf {\delta} _ {t + 1} \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} + \tilde {\mathbf {\phi}} _ {t} \mathbf {\sigma} _ {t} \\ \tilde {\mathbf {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \tilde {\mathbf {\phi}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} \end{array} \right)
$$

and recalling  $\tilde{\mathbf{j}}_1^{(1)}$  from (b.59), by a computation similar to the proof of  $t + 1^{(c)}$  above, we have

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {m}} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathrm {i d} & \mathbf {0} \\ - \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \mathrm {i d} \end{array} \right) \cdot m ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{l} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \\ \mathbf {p} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \end{array} \right) \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t + 1} \to \left(\gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \left( \begin{array}{l l} \mathrm {i d} & 0 \\ 0 & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \end{array} \right) (\tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} ^ {- 1}) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{l} \tilde {\mathbf {j}} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}.
$$

applying the second row of (b.26) with  $t + 1$  and with  $k = 0$ , and recalling  $\mathbf{i}_{t + 1}^{(2k + 1)} = \gamma^{-1} \cdot (\mathbf{j}_{t + 1}^{(2k + 1)})^\top$  and  $c_{1,j} = \kappa_{2(j + 1)}$ , we get

$$
\gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \left( \begin{array}{c} {\bf j} _ {t + 1} ^ {(1)} \\ {\bf l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) = \pmb {\upsilon} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \pmb {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} (\pmb {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \pmb {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top}) ^ {j} \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + 1)} \pmb {\xi} _ {t + 1} ^ {(j)} \end{array} \right) = \pmb {\upsilon} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} {\bf a} _ {t + 1} \\ {\bf \sigma} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right).
$$

hence, writing

$$
\mathbf {a} _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} & \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} \end{array} \right), \qquad \mathbf {\sigma} _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {\sigma} _ {t} & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\top} & \sigma_ {t + 1, t + 1} \end{array} \right),
$$

noting that  $\tilde{\upsilon}_t$  is the matrix  $\upsilon_{t + 1}$  with the last row and column removed, and that  $\mathbf{y}_{t + 1}^{\top}$  is 0 in entries  $1:2t + 1$  of its last column, this yields

$$
\gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \left( \begin{array}{c} \tilde {\mathbf {j}} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) = \tilde {\mathbf {\upsilon}} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c} \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \end{array} \right).
$$

taking the limit  $m, n \to \infty$ , inverting  $(\tilde{\upsilon}_t^\infty)^\top$ , and applying this above,

$$
\mathbf {q} _ {\parallel} \stackrel {{w}} {{\to}} \left( \begin{array}{c c c} u _ {1} & \dots & u _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right) \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + \left( \begin{array}{c c c} y _ {1} & \dots & y _ {t} \end{array} \right) (\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

we have also

$$
\mathbf {q} _ {\perp} \xrightarrow {w} q _ {\perp} \sim \mathcal {n} \left(0, \left(\gamma^ {- 1} \cdot l _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {(2)} - \left( \begin{array}{c} \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {j}} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) ^ {\top} \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {h} _ {t + 1} ^ {(0)} & (\tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} \\ \tilde {\mathbf {i}} _ {t} ^ {(1)} & \gamma^ {- 1} \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right) ^ {- 1} \left( \begin{array}{c} \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \tilde {\mathbf {j}} _ {t} ^ {(1)} \\ \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {\infty}\right)
$$

where  $\mathbf{q}_{\perp}$  is independent of  $(u_{1},\ldots ,u_{t + 1},y_{1},\ldots ,y_{t})$  . then

$$
\mathbf {q} _ {t + 1} \stackrel {w} {\rightarrow} q _ {t + 1} = \left(\begin{array}{l l l}u _ {1}&\dots&u _ {t + 1}\end{array}\right) \tilde {\mathbf {a}} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + \left(\begin{array}{l l l}y _ {1}&\dots&y _ {t}\end{array}\right)\left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + q _ {\perp}. \tag {b.63}
$$

recalling  $\mathbf{y}_{t + 1} = \mathbf{q}_{t + 1} - \mathbf{u}_{t + 1}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_t$ , this yields

$$
\left(\mathbf {u} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {u} _ {t + 2}, \mathbf {y} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {y} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {e}\right) \xrightarrow {w} \left(u _ {1}, \dots , u _ {t + 2}, y _ {1}, \dots , y _ {t + 1}, e\right)
$$

where  $u_{t + 2} = u_{t + 2}(y_1,\ldots ,y_{t + 1},e)$  and

$$
y _ {t + 1} = \left( \begin{array}{c c c} y _ {1} & \dots & y _ {t} \end{array} \right) (\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} + q _ {\perp}.
$$

so  $(y_{1},\ldots ,y_{t},y_{t + 1})$  has a multivariate normal limit. to compute the covariance, note that

$$
\mathbb {e} \big [ (y _ {1}, \ldots , y _ {t}) ^ {\top} y _ {t + 1} \big ] = \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}) ^ {- 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty} = \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} ^ {\infty}.
$$

squaring both sides of (b.63), applying  $\mathbb{e}[q_{t + 1}^2 ] = \lim_{m,n\to \infty}m^{-1}\| \pmb {\lambda}\mathbf{p}_{t + 1}\| ^2 = \gamma^{-1}l_{t + 1,t + 1}^{(2,\infty)}$ , and rearranging,

$$
\mathbb {e} [ y _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = \big (\gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbf {l} _ {t + 1} ^ {(2)} - \mathbf {a} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \pmb {\delta} _ {t + 1} \mathbf {a} _ {t + 1} - \mathbf {a} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \pmb {\phi} _ {t + 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {t + 1} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t + 1} \pmb {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {t + 1} \big) _ {t + 1, t + 1} ^ {\infty}.
$$

applying (b.22), this is  $\sigma_{t + 1,t + 1}^{\infty}$ . this concludes the proof of  $t + 1^{(f)}$ .

finally, for the invertibility claim of  $t + 1^{(g)}$ , let us first observe that

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ q _ {\perp} \right] > 0 \tag {b.64}
$$

above. this is because  $\operatorname{var}[q_{\perp}]$  is the residual variance of projecting  $\bar{p}_{t+1}$  onto the span of  $(r_1, \ldots, r_{t+1}, \bar{p}_1, \ldots, \bar{p}_t)$ . if this were 0, then for some constants  $\alpha_1, \ldots, \alpha_{t+1}, \beta_1, \ldots, \beta_t$ , we would have

$$
\begin{array}{l} 0 = \mathbb {e} \left[ \lambda_ {m} ^ {2} \cdot \left(\bar {p} _ {t + 1} - \alpha_ {1} r _ {1} - \dots - \alpha_ {t + 1} r _ {t + 1} - \beta_ {1} \bar {p} _ {1} - \dots - \beta_ {t} \bar {p} _ {t}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ = \lim _ {m, n \rightarrow \infty} m ^ {- 1} \| \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t + 1} - \alpha_ {1} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {1} - \ldots - \alpha_ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r} _ {t + 1} - \beta_ {1} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {1} - \ldots - \beta_ {t} \boldsymbol {\lambda} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} \mathbf {p} _ {t} \| ^ {2} \\ = \gamma^ {- 1} \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (\lambda_ {n} ^ {2} p _ {t + 1} - \alpha_ {1} \bar {r} _ {1} - \ldots - \alpha_ {t + 1} \bar {r} _ {t + 1} - \beta_ {1} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2} p _ {1} - \ldots - \beta_ {n} \lambda_ {n} ^ {2} p _ {t}) ^ {2} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

so

$$
\lambda_ {n} ^ {2} p _ {\perp} = f \left(p _ {1}, \dots , p _ {t}, \bar {r} _ {1}, \dots , \bar {r} _ {t + 1}, \lambda_ {n}\right)
$$

for some quantity on the right not depending on  $p_{\perp}$ . this contradicts the independence of  $p_{\perp}$  from  $p_{1}, \ldots, p_{t}, \bar{r}_{1}, \ldots, \bar{r}_{t+1}, \lambda_{n}$ , the assumption  $\operatorname{var}[\lambda_{n}] > 0$ , and the condition  $\operatorname{var}[p_{\perp}] > 0$  already shown in (b.58). so (b.64) holds.

to show the invertibility of

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} & \boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \\ \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} (\boldsymbol {\phi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}) ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \end{array} \right), \tag {b.65}
$$

note that its upper-left  $(2t + 1) \times (2t + 1)$  submatrix is  $\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_t^\infty$ , which we have shown is invertible. the schur-complement of its lower-right entry is the residual variance of projecting  $y_{t+1}$  onto  $(u_1, \ldots, u_t, y_1, \ldots, y_{t+1})$ . as  $\mathbf{y}_{t+1} = \mathbf{q}_{t+1} - \mathbf{u}_{t+1} \tilde{\mathbf{a}}_t$ , this is equivalently the residual variance of projecting  $q_{t+1}$  onto  $(u_1, \ldots, u_t, y_1, \ldots, y_{t+1})$ , which is exactly  $\operatorname{var}[q_\perp]$  by (b.63) and the fact that  $q_\perp$  is a mean-zero variable independent of  $(u_1, \ldots, u_t, y_1, \ldots, y_{t+1})$ . since  $\operatorname{var}[q_\perp] > 0$ , this shows that (b.65) is invertible.

to show the invertibility of

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} & \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \\ \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \left(\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty}\right) ^ {\top} & \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t + 1} ^ {\infty} \end{array} \right), \tag {b.66}
$$

note that its submatrix removing row and column  $t + 1$  is  $\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_t^\infty$ , which we have also shown is invertible. the schur-complement of the  $(t + 1, t + 1)$  entry is the residual variance of projecting  $v_{t+1}$  onto the span of  $(v_1, \ldots, v_t, z_1, \ldots, z_{t+1})$ , which is non-zero by assumption 5.2(f). thus (b.66) is invertible. this shows  $t + 1^{(g)}$ , and concludes the induction and the proof.

# appendix c. analysis of amp for pca

in this appendix, we prove theorems 3.1 and 3.4. we also complete the verification of eq. (3.32), showing that the rectangular amp algorithm achieves lower matrix mean-squared error than the sample pcs.

c.1. state evolution for pca. we prove theorems 3.1(a) and 3.4(a), using the general results of corollaries 4.4 and 5.4.

proof of theorem 3.1(a). we may write the amp iterations (3.3-3.4) as

$$
\mathbf {f} _ {t} = \mathbf {u} _ {*} \cdot (\alpha / n) \mathbf {u} _ {*} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t} + \mathbf {w} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - b _ {t 1} \mathbf {u} _ {1} - \ldots - b _ {t t} \mathbf {u} _ {t}, \qquad \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} = u _ {t + 1} (\mathbf {f} _ {t}).
$$

approximating  $(\alpha /n)\mathbf{u}_{*}^{\top}\mathbf{u}_{t}\approx \alpha \cdot \mathbb{e}[u_{*}u_{t}] = \mu_{t}^{\infty}$ , we consider the auxiliary amp sequence initialized at  $\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_1 = \mathbf{u}_1$  and defined by

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t} = \mathbf {w} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t} - \tilde {b} _ {t 1} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} - \ldots - \tilde {b} _ {t t} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t}, \qquad \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t + 1} = \tilde {u} _ {t + 1} (\tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {*}) \equiv u _ {t + 1} (\tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t} + \mu_ {t} ^ {\infty} \mathbf {u} _ {*}).
$$

here, the debiasing coefficients  $\tilde{b}_{t1},\ldots ,\tilde{b}_{tt}$  are the values of the last column of  $\tilde{\mathbf{b}}_t$ , defined by (4.4) and (4.7) with the iterates  $\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_t$  and the free cumulants  $\tilde{\kappa}_k$  of  $\mathbf{w}$ . the partial derivatives that define (4.4) are given by  $\partial_s\tilde{u}_{t + 1}(\cdot) = \tilde{u}_{t + 1}'(\cdot)$  if  $s = t$  and  $\partial_s\tilde{u}_{t + 1}(\cdot) = 0$  otherwise, where  $\tilde{u}_{t + 1}'(\cdot)$  denotes the derivative in its first argument  $\tilde{z}_t$ .

this auxiliary amp sequence is of the general form (4.2-4.3) with side information  $\mathbf{e} = \mathbf{u}_{*}$ . by the given differentiability and lipschitz assumption for  $u_{t + 1}(\cdot)$ , the conditions of corollary 4.4 are satisfied for  $\tilde{u}_{t + 1}(\cdot)$ , so we have for each fixed  $t\geq 1$  that

$$
\left(\tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t + 1}, \tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {*}\right) \stackrel {{w _ {2}}} {{\rightarrow}} \left(\tilde {u} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {u} _ {t + 1}, \tilde {z} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {z} _ {t}, u _ {*}\right).
$$

here,  $(\tilde{z}_1,\ldots ,\tilde{z}_t)\sim \mathcal{n}(0,\tilde{\sigma}_t^\infty)$  where  $\tilde{\sigma}_t^\infty$  is defined by (4.5) and (4.7) for this auxiliary amp sequence,  $\tilde{u}_{1} = u_{1}$ ,  $\tilde{u}_{s + 1} = \tilde{u}_{s + 1}(\tilde{z}_s,u_*)$  for  $s\geq 1$ , and  $(\tilde{z}_1,\dots ,\tilde{z}_t)$  is independent of  $(\tilde{u}_1,u_*)$ . defining

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {t} = \tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t} + \mu_ {t} ^ {\infty} \mathbf {u} _ {*}, \qquad \tilde {f} _ {t} = \tilde {z} _ {t} + \mu_ {t} ^ {\infty} u _ {*},
$$

this implies

$$
\left(\tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t + 1}, \tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {*}\right) \stackrel {{w _ {2}}} {{\rightarrow}} \left(\tilde {u} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {u} _ {t + 1}, \tilde {f} _ {1}, \dots , \tilde {f} _ {t}, u _ {*}\right). \tag {c.1}
$$

since each derivative  $\partial_s\tilde{u}_{t + 1}$  is non-zero only for  $s = t$  , the covariance matrix  $\pmb{\sigma}_t^\infty$  has the entries

$$
\tilde {\sigma} _ {s t} ^ {\infty} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {s - 1} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {t - 1} \tilde {\kappa} _ {j + k + 2} ^ {\infty} \left(\prod_ {i = s - j + 1} ^ {s} \mathbb {e} [ \tilde {u} _ {i} ^ {\prime} (\tilde {z} _ {i - 1}, u _ {*}) ]\right) \left(\prod_ {i = t - k + 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {e} [ \tilde {u} _ {i} ^ {\prime} (\tilde {z} _ {i - 1}, u _ {*}) ]\right) \mathbb {e} [ \tilde {u} _ {s - j} \tilde {u} _ {t - k} ] \quad \tag {c.2}
$$

where the summand for  $(j,k)$  corresponds to  $\phi_t^j\delta_t(\phi_t^k)^\top$  in the definitions (4.5) and (4.7).

we conclude the proof by showing that the joint law of this limit in (c.1) coincides with the limit described in theorem 3.1(a), and that  $(\mathbf{u}_1, \ldots, \mathbf{u}_{t+1}, \mathbf{f}_1, \ldots, \mathbf{f}_t, \mathbf{u}_*)$  for the original amp algorithm converges to the same limit. observe first that the  $n-1$  smallest eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{x}$  are interlaced with the  $n$  eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{w}$ . letting  $m_k$  be as defined in (3.5), and denoting the moments of the empirical spectral distribution of  $\mathbf{w}$  by

$$
\tilde {m} _ {k} = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \lambda_ {i} ^ {k},
$$

this interlacing and the condition  $\| \mathbf{w}\| \leq c_0$  imply  $|m_k - \tilde{m}_k|\to 0$  and  $m_{k},\tilde{m}_{k}\rightarrow m_{k}^{\infty} = \mathbb{e}[\lambda^{k}]$  for each fixed  $k\geq 1$  as  $n\to \infty$ . hence also for each fixed  $k\geq 1$ ,

$$
\left| \kappa_ {k} - \tilde {\kappa} _ {k} \right|\rightarrow 0 \quad \text {a n d} \quad \kappa_ {k}, \tilde {\kappa} _ {k} \rightarrow \kappa_ {k} ^ {\infty} \tag {c.3}
$$

where  $\{\kappa_k^\infty\}_{k \geq 1}$  are the free cumulants of  $\lambda$ .

we now check inductively that, almost surely for each fixed  $t = 0,1,2,\ldots$  as  $n\to \infty$

$$
n ^ {- 1} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {s} - \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {s} \right\| ^ {2} \rightarrow 0 \text {f o r a l l} s \leq t + 1, \quad n ^ {- 1} \left\| \mathbf {f} _ {s} - \tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {s} \right\| ^ {2} \rightarrow 0 \text {f o r a l l} s \leq t, \tag {c.4}
$$

and

$$
\left(\mathbf {u} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1}, \mathbf {f} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {f} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {*}\right) \stackrel {{w _ {2}}} {{\rightarrow}} \left(u _ {1}, \dots , u _ {t + 1}, f _ {1}, \dots , f _ {t}, u _ {*}\right) \tag {c.5}
$$

where the joint law of this limit is as in theorem 3.1 and coincides with the limit in (c.1)

for the base case  $t = 0$ , we have  $\| \mathbf{u}_1 - \tilde{\mathbf{u}}_1 \| = 0$ ,  $(\mathbf{u}_1, \mathbf{u}_*) \xrightarrow{w} (u_1, u_*) = (\tilde{u}_1, u_*)$  by assumption, and the remaining claims are vacuous. assume inductively that these claims hold for  $t - 1$ . then for all  $s, s' \leq t$ ,

$$
\lim _ {n \to \infty} \left\langle \mathbf {u} _ {s} \mathbf {u} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \right\rangle = \mathbb {e} [ u _ {s} u _ {s ^ {\prime}} ] = \mathbb {e} [ \tilde {u} _ {s} \tilde {u} _ {s ^ {\prime}} ] = \lim _ {n \to \infty} \left\langle \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {s} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \right\rangle ,
$$

and similarly for all  $s \leq t$ ,

$$
\lim _ {n \to \infty} \langle u _ {s} ^ {\prime} (\mathbf {f} _ {s - 1}) \rangle = \mathbb {e} [ u _ {s} ^ {\prime} (f _ {s - 1}) ] = \mathbb {e} [ \tilde {u} _ {s} ^ {\prime} (\tilde {z} _ {s - 1}, u _ {*}) ] = \lim _ {n \to \infty} \langle \partial_ {s - 1} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {s} \rangle .
$$

combining with (c.3) and comparing (3.8) with (c.2), this shows that  $\tilde{\sigma}_t^\infty$  coincides with  $\sigma_t^\infty$ , and hence that the limit laws in (c.1) and (c.5) coincide for  $t$ .

comparing (3.6) with the general definition of  $\tilde{\mathbf{b}}_t$  from (4.7), this also shows that  $|b_{st} - \tilde{b}_{st}| \to 0$  as  $n \to \infty$ , for all  $s, t \leq t$ . denoting

$$
\mathbf {z} _ {t} = \mathbf {w} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - b _ {t 1} \mathbf {u} _ {1} - \dots - b _ {t t} \mathbf {u} _ {t},
$$

and applying also  $\| \mathbf{w}\| \leq c_0$  and  $n^{-1}\| \mathbf{u}_s - \tilde{\mathbf{u}}_s\| ^2\to 0$  for all  $s\le t$  by the induction hypothesis, we obtain  $n^{-1}\| \mathbf{z}_t - \tilde{\mathbf{z}}_t\| ^2\to 0$ . since  $\mathbf{f}_t = \mathbf{u}_*\cdot (\alpha /n)\mathbf{u}_*^\top \mathbf{u}_t + \mathbf{z}_t$ ,  $\tilde{\mathbf{f}}_t = \mathbf{u}_*\cdot \mu_t^\infty +\tilde{\mathbf{z}}_t$ , and  $(\alpha /n)\mathbf{u}_*^\top \mathbf{u}_t\to \alpha \cdot \mathbb{e}[u_*u_t] = \mu_t^\infty$  by the induction hypothesis (c.5), this shows

$$
n ^ {- 1} \left\| \mathbf {f} _ {t} - \tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {t} \right\| ^ {2} \rightarrow 0. \tag {c.6}
$$

then, as the function  $u_{t + 1}$  is lipschitz,

$$
n ^ {- 1} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} - \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t + 1} \right\| ^ {2} = n ^ {- 1} \left\| u _ {t + 1} \left(\mathbf {f} _ {t}\right) - u _ {t + 1} \left(\tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {t}\right)\right\| ^ {2} \rightarrow 0. \tag {c.7}
$$

this shows (c.4) for  $t$ . applying proposition e.4, this implies that  $(\mathbf{u}_1, \ldots, \mathbf{u}_{t+1}, \mathbf{f}_1, \ldots, \mathbf{f}_t, \mathbf{u}_*)$  must have the same empirical limit in  $w_2$  as  $(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_1, \ldots, \tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t+1}, \tilde{\mathbf{f}}_1, \ldots, \tilde{\mathbf{f}}_t, \mathbf{u}_*)$ . together with (c.1) and the coincidence of the two joint limit laws in (c.1) and (c.5) that was already established, this shows (c.5) for  $t$ , concluding the induction and the proof.

proof of theorem 3.4(a). we may write the amp iterations (3.19-3.22) as

$$
\mathbf {g} _ {t} = \mathbf {v} _ {*} \cdot (\alpha / m) \mathbf {u} _ {*} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t} + \mathbf {w} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {t} - b _ {t 1} \mathbf {v} _ {1} - \dots - b _ {t, t - 1} \mathbf {v} _ {t - 1}, \quad \mathbf {v} _ {t} = v _ {t} (\mathbf {g} _ {t})
$$

$$
\mathbf {f} _ {t} = \mathbf {u} _ {*} \cdot (\alpha / m) \mathbf {v} _ {*} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {t} + \mathbf {w} \mathbf {v} _ {t} - a _ {t 1} \mathbf {u} _ {1} - \ldots - a _ {t t} \mathbf {u} _ {t}, \qquad \mathbf {u} _ {t + 1} = u _ {t + 1} (\mathbf {f} _ {t}).
$$

approximating  $(\alpha /m)\mathbf{u}_{*}^{\top}\mathbf{u}_{t}\approx \alpha \cdot \mathbb{e}[u_{*}u_{t}] = \nu_{t}^{\infty}$  and  $(\alpha /m)\mathbf{v}_{*}^{\top}\mathbf{v}_{t}\approx \alpha /\gamma \cdot \mathbb{e}[v_{*}v_{t}] = \mu_{t}^{\infty}$ , we consider the auxiliary amp sequence initialized at  $\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_1 = \mathbf{u}_1$  and defined by

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t} = \mathbf {w} ^ {\top} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t} - \tilde {b} _ {t 1} \tilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {1} - \ldots - \tilde {b} _ {t, t - 1} \tilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {t - 1}, \qquad \tilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} = \tilde {v} _ {t} (\tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t}, \mathbf {v} _ {*}) \equiv v _ {t} (\tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t} + \nu_ {t} ^ {\infty} \mathbf {v} _ {*}),
$$

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} = \mathbf {w} \tilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} - \tilde {a} _ {t 1} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t} - \dots - \tilde {a} _ {t t} \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t}, \quad \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t + 1} = \tilde {u} _ {t + 1} (\tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {*}) \equiv u _ {t + 1} (\tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} + \mu_ {t} ^ {\infty} \mathbf {u} _ {*}).
$$

here, the debiasing coefficients are the last columns of  $\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_t$  and  $\tilde{\mathbf{b}}_t$  defined by (5.12) with the iterates  $\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_t,\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_t$  and with rectangular free cumulants  $\tilde{\kappa}_{2k}$  of  $\mathbf{w}$ . the partial derivatives in (5.7) and (5.8) are given by  $\partial_s\tilde{u}_{t + 1}(\cdot) = \tilde{u}_{t + 1}'(\cdot)$  if  $s = t$  and 0 otherwise, and  $\partial_s\tilde{v}_t(\cdot) = \tilde{v}_t'(\cdot)$  if  $s = t$  and 0 otherwise, where  $\tilde{u}_{t + 1}'(\cdot)$  and  $\tilde{v}_t'(\cdot)$  denote their derivatives with respect to the first arguments  $\tilde{y}_t$  and  $\tilde{z}_t$ .

this auxiliary amp sequence is of the form (5.3-5.6) with side information  $\mathbf{e} = \mathbf{u}_{*}$  and  $\mathbf{f} = \mathbf{v}_{*}$ . setting

$$
\tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {t} = \tilde {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} + \mu_ {t} ^ {\infty} \mathbf {u} _ {*}, \qquad \tilde {\mathbf {g}} _ {t} = \tilde {\mathbf {z}} _ {t} + \nu_ {t} ^ {\infty} \mathbf {v} _ {*},
$$

corollary 5.4 then implies for each fixed  $t \geq 1$  that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\tilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {t}, \tilde {\mathbf {g}} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {\mathbf {g}} _ {t}, \mathbf {v} _ {*}\right) \stackrel {{w _ {2}}} {{\rightarrow}} \left(\tilde {v} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {v} _ {t}, \tilde {g} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {g} _ {t}, v _ {*}\right) \\ \left(\tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {t + 1}, \tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {t}, \mathbf {u} _ {*}\right) \xrightarrow {w _ {2}} \left(\tilde {u} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {u} _ {t + 1}, \tilde {f} _ {1}, \ldots , \tilde {f} _ {t}, u _ {*}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where these limits are described by  $\tilde{f}_t = \tilde{y}_t + \mu_t^\infty u_*$ ,  $\tilde{g}_t = \tilde{z}_t + \nu_t^\infty v_*$ ,  $(\tilde{y}_1, \ldots, \tilde{y}_t) \sim \mathcal{n}(0, \tilde{\sigma}_t^\infty)$  and  $(\tilde{z}_1, \ldots, \tilde{z}_t) \sim \mathcal{n}(0, \tilde{\omega}_t^\infty)$ . here, the forms for  $\tilde{\sigma}_t^\infty$  and  $\tilde{\omega}_t^\infty$  are given by (3.24) and (3.26) defined for this auxiliary sequence: in the definitions (5.13), summing the first terms of  $\theta_t^{(j)}$  and  $\xi_t^{(j)}$  in (5.9-5.10) yields the terms with coefficient  $\kappa_{2(j+k+1)}$  in (3.24) and (3.26), while summing the second terms of (5.9-5.10) yields the terms with coefficient  $\kappa_{2(j+k+2)}$ .

the proof is concluded by a similar comparison argument as in the preceding proof of theorem 3.1(a), showing that these joint limit laws coincide with those of theorem 3.4 and that the original amp sequence converges also to these joint laws. we omit the details for brevity.  $\square$

c.2. analysis of state evolutions. we now prove theorems 3.1(b) and 3.4(b). for notational simplicity, we will drop all superscripts  $\infty$  in this section, so that  $\kappa_{k},\pmb{\sigma}_{t},\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  etc. are all understood as their deterministic  $n\to \infty$  limits. we use the entrywise notation

$$
\pmb {\delta} _ {t} = (\delta_ {s t}) _ {s, t = 1} ^ {t}, \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} = (\sigma_ {s t}) _ {s, t = 1} ^ {t}, \pmb {\mu} _ {t} = (\mu_ {t}) _ {t = 1} ^ {t}, \pmb {\gamma} _ {t} = (\gamma_ {s t}) _ {s, t = 1} ^ {t}, \pmb {\omega} _ {t} = (\omega_ {s t}) _ {s, t = 1} ^ {t}, \pmb {\nu} _ {t} = (\nu_ {t}) _ {t = 1} ^ {t}.
$$

the proofs will apply a contractive mapping argument to show that the matrices  $\pmb{\delta}_t$ ,  $\pmb{\sigma}_t$ ,  $\pmb{\gamma}_t$ , and  $\pmb{\omega}_t$  all converge in a certain normed space. fix an arbitrary constant  $\zeta \in (0,1)$ , say

$$
\zeta = 1 / 2.
$$

we consider the space of "infinite matrices"  $\mathbf{x} = (x_{st}:s,t\leq 0)$ , indexed by the non-positive integers. the index  $(0,0)$  should be interpreted as the lower-right corner of  $\mathbf{x}$ . we equip this space with a weighted  $\ell_{\infty}$ -norm

$$
\| \mathbf {x} \| _ {\zeta} = \sup  _ {s, t \leq 0} \zeta^ {| s | \vee | t |} | x _ {s t} |, \qquad | s | \vee | t | = \max  (| s |, | t |).
$$

thus the weight is  $\zeta^k$  for the  $2k + 1$  coordinate pairs

$$
(s, t) = (- k, 0), (- k, - 1), \dots , (- k, - k + 1), (- k, - k), (- k + 1, - k), \dots , (- 1, - k), (0, - k).
$$

define  $\mathcal{x} = \{\mathbf{x} : \| \mathbf{x}\|_{\zeta} < \infty\}$ , and observe that  $\mathcal{x}$  is complete under  $\|\cdot\|_{\zeta}$ . for any compact interval  $i \subset \mathbb{r}$ , denote

$$
\mathcal {x} _ {i} = \left\{\mathbf {x}: \mathbf {x} _ {s t} \in i \text {f o r a l l} s, t \leq 0 \right\} \subset \mathcal {x}. \tag {c.8}
$$

then  $\mathcal{x}_i$  is closed in  $\mathcal{x}$ , and hence  $\mathcal{x}_i$  is also complete under the norm  $\| \cdot \|_{\zeta}$ .

we will embed the matrices  $\pmb{\delta}_t, \pmb{\sigma}_t, \pmb{\gamma}_t, \pmb{\omega}_t$  as elements  $\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}, \mathbf{z}, \mathbf{w} \in \mathcal{x}$ , with the coordinate identifications

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta_ {s t} = x _ {s - t, t - t}, \quad \sigma_ {s t} = y _ {s - t, t - t}, \quad \gamma_ {s t} = z _ {s - t, t - t}, \quad \omega_ {s t} = w _ {s - t, t - t} \\ x _ {s t} = y _ {s t} = z _ {s t} = w _ {s t} = 0 \quad \text {i f} \quad s \leq - t \text {o r} t \leq - t. \tag {c.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

thus  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$ ,  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$ ,  $\pmb{\gamma}_{t}$ , and  $\pmb{\omega}_{t}$  fill out the lower-right  $t \times t$  corners of the corresponding sequences in  $\mathcal{x}$ , with their lower-right  $(t, t)$  entries identified with the coordinate  $(0, 0)$  of  $\mathcal{x}$ . zero-padding is applied for the remaining entries of  $(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}, \mathbf{z}, \mathbf{w})$  not belonging to this corner. the proofs will then have two main steps:

(1) for large  $t$ , the state evolution that maps these matrices from iterate  $t$  to iterate  $t + 1$  will be approximated by a fixed map that is independent of  $t$ , where the approximation is in the norm  $\| \cdot \|_{\zeta}$ .  
(2) this map will be shown to be contractive over certain sub-domains of  $\mathcal{x}$  with respect to  $\| \cdot \|_{\zeta}$ , and hence these matrices will converge to a fixed point of this map.

c.2.1. symmetric square matrices. we first show theorem 3.1(b). recall that the amp algorithm is given by (3.3-3.4), where we take  $u_{t+1}(\cdot)$  to be the single-iterate posterior mean denoiser in (3.10). differentiating (3.10) in  $f$ , we obtain

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial f} \eta (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \mathrm {c o v} \left[ u _ {*}, \frac {\partial}{\partial f} \left(- \frac {(f - \mu u _ {*}) ^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}\right) \bigg | f = f \right] = \frac {\mu}{\sigma^ {2}} \mathrm {v a r} [ u _ {*} \mid f = f ].
$$

then

$$
