u _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime} (f _ {t}) = \frac {\mu_ {t}}{\sigma_ {t t}} \mathrm {v a r} [ u _ {*} \mid f _ {t} = f _ {t} ].
$$

observe that for all  $t\geq 1$

$$
\mathbb {e} \bigl [ \mathrm {v a r} [ u _ {*} \mid f _ {t} ] \bigr ] = \mathbb {e} \bigl [ (u _ {*} - u _ {t + 1}) ^ {2} \bigr ] = \mathbb {e} [ u _ {*} ^ {2} ] - \mathbb {e} [ u _ {t + 1} ^ {2} ] = 1 - \delta_ {t + 1, t + 1}.
$$

so (3.8) may be written more explicitly as

$$
\sigma_ {s t} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {s - 1} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {t - 1} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} \left(\prod_ {i = s - j + 1} ^ {s} \frac {\mu_ {i - 1}}{\sigma_ {i - 1 , i - 1}} (1 - \delta_ {i i})\right) \left(\prod_ {i = t - k + 1} ^ {t} \frac {\mu_ {i - 1}}{\sigma_ {i - 1 , i - 1}} (1 - \delta_ {i i})\right) \delta_ {s - j, t - k}. \qquad (\mathrm {c . 1 0})
$$

in this expression, we have

$$
\mu_ {1} = \alpha \cdot \mathbb {e} [ u _ {1} u _ {*} ] = \alpha \varepsilon , \qquad \mu_ {i} = \alpha \cdot \mathbb {e} [ u _ {i} u _ {*} ] = \alpha \cdot \mathbb {e} [ u _ {i} ^ {2} ] = \alpha \delta_ {i i} \mathrm {f o r} i \geq 2.
$$

for a sufficiently large constant  $c > 0$  depending on  $c_0$  and  $\varepsilon$ , we define the intervals

$$
i _ {\delta} = \left[ 1 - \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}}, 1 \right], \qquad i _ {\sigma} = \left[ \frac {1}{2} \kappa_ {2}, \frac {3}{2} \kappa_ {2} \right],
$$

and the corresponding domains  $\mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}},\mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}\subset \mathcal{x}$  by (c.8). motivated by the forms (c.10) and (c.11), we will approximate the map  $(\pmb{\delta}_t,\pmb{\sigma}_{t - 1})\mapsto \pmb{\sigma}_t$  by a fixed map  $h^\sigma :\mathcal{x}_{i_\delta}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma}\to \mathcal{x}$ , defined entrywise by

$$
h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} \left(\prod_ {i = s - j + 1} ^ {s} \frac {\alpha x _ {i - 1 , i - 1}}{y _ {i i}} (1 - x _ {i i})\right) \left(\prod_ {i = t - k + 1} ^ {t} \frac {\alpha x _ {i - 1 , i - 1}}{y _ {i i}} (1 - x _ {i i})\right) x _ {s - j, t - k}. \tag {c.12}
$$

(note that the embedding of  $\sigma_{t-1}$  in  $\mathcal{x}$  has indices that are offset from those of  $\sigma_t$  and  $\delta_t$  by 1, so  $y_{ii}$  appears instead of  $y_{i-1,i-1}$ .) we will approximate the map  $(\delta_t, \sigma_t) \mapsto \delta_{t+1}$  by a fixed map  $h^\delta: \mathcal{x}_{i_\delta} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma} \to \mathcal{x}$ , defined as

$$
h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) = \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}} \big [ \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}} [ u _ {*} \mid f _ {s} ] \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}} [ u _ {*} \mid f _ {t} ] \big ]
$$

where the expectations are with respect to the  $(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})$ -dependent joint law

$$
(f _ {s}, f _ {t}) = (\alpha x _ {s s}, \alpha x _ {t t}) u _ {*} + (z _ {s}, z _ {t}), \qquad (z _ {s}, z _ {t}) \sim \mathcal {n} \left(0, \pi \left( \begin{array}{c c} y _ {s s} & y _ {s t} \\ y _ {t s} & y _ {t t} \end{array} \right)\right) \text {i n d e p e n d e n t o f} u _ {*}.
$$

here, we denote by  $\pi : i_{\sigma}^{2 \times 2} \to i_{\sigma}^{2 \times 2}$  the map

$$
\pi \left( \begin{array}{c c} a & b \\ c & d \end{array} \right) = \left( \begin{array}{c c} a & \min  (\sqrt {b c}, \sqrt {a d}) \\ \min  (\sqrt {b c}, \sqrt {a d}) & d \end{array} \right) \tag {c.13}
$$

whose image is always symmetric positive-semidefinite, so that the above bivariate normal law is always well-defined. (if  $m$  is already symmetric positive-semidefinite, then  $\pi(m) = m$ .)

the following lemma establishes the lipschitz bounds for  $h^{\sigma}$  and  $h^{\delta}$ .

lemma c.1. in the setting of theorem 3.1(b), there exist constants  $c, \alpha_0 > 0$  such that for all  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  and  $(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}), (\mathbf{x}', \mathbf{y}') \in \mathcal{x}_{i_\delta} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma}$ :

(a)  $h^{\sigma}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$  and  $\| h^{\sigma}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}) - h^{\sigma}(\mathbf{x}',\mathbf{y}')\|_{\zeta}\leq c\alpha \| \mathbf{x} - \mathbf{x}'\|_{\zeta} + (c / \alpha)\| \mathbf{y} - \mathbf{y}'\|_{\zeta}$  
(b)  $h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}$  and  $\| h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}) - h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\|_{\zeta}\leq (c / \alpha)\| \mathbf{x} - \mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{\zeta} + (c / \alpha^{2})\| \mathbf{y} - \mathbf{y}^{\prime}\|_{\zeta}$

proof. let  $c, c', c, \ldots$  denote constants depending only on  $c_0$  and  $\varepsilon$  and changing from instance to instance. for part (a), let us write (c.12) as

$$
h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) = \sum_ {j, k = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} h _ {s t} ^ {(j, k)} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}).
$$

observe that for  $(j,k) = (0,0)$ , we have simply  $h_{st}^{(0,0)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}) = x_{st}$ . by the given domains of  $\mathbf{x}$  and  $\mathbf{y}$ , for all other  $(j,k)$ , we have the bounds  $x_{i-1,i-1} \leq 1$ ,  $y_{ii} \geq \kappa_2/2$ , and  $1 - x_{ii} \leq c/\alpha^2$  in the products defining (c.12). there are  $j + k$  factors of the form  $(\alpha x_{i-1,i-1}/y_{ii})(1 - x_{ii})$ , yielding

$$
\left| h _ {s t} ^ {(j, k)} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \right| \leq (c / \alpha) ^ {j + k}.
$$

applying  $|\kappa_{j + k + 2}| \leq (16c_0)^{j + k + 2}$  by proposition f.3, for  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  sufficiently large, this implies

$$
| h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - \kappa_ {2} x _ {s t} | = \left| \sum_ {(j, k) \neq (0, 0)} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} h _ {s t} ^ {(j, k)} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \right| \leq \kappa_ {2} / 3.
$$

applying  $x_{st} \in [1 - c / \alpha^2, 1]$ , this yields  $h_{st}^{\sigma}(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}) \in i_{\sigma} = [\kappa_2 / 2, 3\kappa_2 / 2]$  for  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  sufficiently large. then  $h^{\sigma}(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}) \in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$ .

to show the lipschitz bound for  $h^\sigma$ , for  $\lambda \in [0,1]$  we set

$$
\mathbf {x} ^ {\lambda} = \lambda \mathbf {x} + (1 - \lambda) \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \quad \mathbf {y} ^ {\lambda} = \lambda \mathbf {y} + (1 - \lambda) \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}.
$$

then

$$
\left| h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| = \left| \int_ {0} ^ {1} \frac {d}{d \lambda} h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\lambda}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\lambda}\right) d \lambda \right| \leq \sup  _ {\lambda \in [ 0, 1 ]} \left| \frac {d}{d \lambda} h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\lambda}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\lambda}\right) \right|. \tag {c.14}
$$

by the chain rule,

$$
\frac {d}{d \lambda} h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\lambda}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\lambda}) = \sum_ {p, q \leq 0} \left(x _ {p q} - x _ {p q} ^ {\prime}\right) \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial x _ {p q}} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\lambda}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\lambda}\right) + \sum_ {p \leq 0} \left(y _ {p p} - y _ {p p} ^ {\prime}\right) \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial y _ {p p}} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\lambda}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\lambda}\right). \tag {c.15}
$$

we establish a uniform bound for these partial derivatives. for any  $\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$ , applying the above bound for  $(\alpha x_{i - 1,i - 1} / y_{ii})(1 - x_{ii})$ , we have

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial x _ {p p}} \right| \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} c \alpha^ {2} (c / \alpha) ^ {j + k} & \text {i f} p \in \{s - j + 1, \ldots , s \} \cup \{t - k + 1, \ldots , t \} \\ (c / \alpha) ^ {j + k} & \text {i f} p = s - j \text {o r} p = t - k \end{array} \right.
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial y _ {p p}} \right| \leq (c / \alpha) ^ {j + k} \quad \text {i f} p \in \{s - j + 1, \dots , s \} \cup \{t - k + 1, \dots , t \}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial x _ {s - j , t - k}} \right| \leq (c / \alpha) ^ {j + k},
$$

and all other partial derivatives of  $h_{st}^{(j,k)}$  are 0. multiplying by  $\kappa_{j+k+2}$ , applying  $|\kappa_{j+k+2}| \leq (16c_0)^{j+k+2}$ , and summing over  $j, k \geq 0$ , this implies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial x _ {p p}} \right| \leq \sum_ {j, k \geq 0} | \kappa_ {j + k + 2} | \cdot \left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial x _ {p p}} \right| \\ \leq \mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \} \sum_ {k \geq 0} c \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {(s - p) + k} + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq t \} \sum_ {j \geq 0} c \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {j + (t - p)} \\ + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \} \sum_ {j \geq s + 1 - p} \sum_ {k \geq 0} c \alpha^ {2} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {j + k} + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq t \} \sum_ {k \geq t + 1 - p} \sum_ {j \geq 0} c \alpha^ {2} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {j + k} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first two terms are the contributions from  $p = s - j$  and  $p = t - k$ , and the latter two terms are the contributions from  $p \in \{s - j + 1, \ldots, s\}$  and  $p \in \{t - k + 1, \ldots, t\}$ . for  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  sufficiently large, this simplifies to the bound

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial x _ {p p}} \right| \leq c \alpha \left(\mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \} \cdot \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {s - p} + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq t \} \cdot \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {t - p}\right).
$$

we have similarly

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial y _ {p p}} \right| \leq \sum_ {j, k \geq 0} | \kappa_ {j + k + 2} | \cdot \left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial y _ {p p}} \right| \\ \leq \mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \} \sum_ {j \geq s + 1 - p} \sum_ {k \geq 0} c \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {j + k} + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq t \} \sum_ {k \geq t + 1 - p} \sum_ {j \geq 0} c \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {j + k} \\ \leq \frac {c}{\alpha} \left(\mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \} \cdot \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {s - p} + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq t \} \cdot \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {t - p}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and, for  $p \neq q$ ,

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial x _ {p q}} \right| \leq \sum_ {j, k \geq 0} | \kappa_ {j + k + 2} | \cdot \left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial x _ {p q}} \right| \leq c \cdot \mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \mathrm {a n d} q \leq t \} \cdot \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {s - p + t - q}.
$$

applying these bounds to (c.14) and (c.15),

$$
\begin{array}{l} | h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) | \leq c \alpha \sum_ {p \leq s} | x _ {p p} - x _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {s - p} + c \alpha \sum_ {p \leq t} | x _ {p p} - x _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {t - p} \\ + \frac {c}{\alpha} \sum_ {p \leq s} | y _ {p p} - y _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {s - p} + \frac {c}{\alpha} \sum_ {p \leq t} | y _ {p p} - y _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {t - p} \\ + c \sum_ {p \leq s} \sum_ {q \leq t} | x _ {p q} - x _ {p q} ^ {\prime} | \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {s - p + t - q}. \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $\alpha >\alpha_0$  large enough, we may bound the terms above using

$$
\sum_ {p \leq s} | x _ {p p} - x _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {s - p} \leq \sup _ {p \leq s} | x _ {p p} - x _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | \zeta^ {| p |} \cdot \sum_ {p \leq s} \zeta^ {- | p |} (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {s - p} \leq c \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} \cdot \zeta^ {- | s |},
$$

$$
\sum_ {p \leq s} | y _ {p p} - y _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {s - p} \leq \sup _ {p \leq s} | y _ {p p} - y _ {p p} ^ {\prime} | \zeta^ {| p |} \cdot \sum_ {p \leq s} \zeta^ {- | p |} (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {s - p} \leq c \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} \cdot \zeta^ {- | s |},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p \leq s} \sum_ {q \leq t} | x _ {p q} - x _ {p q} ^ {\prime} | (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {s - p + t - q} \leq \sup _ {p \leq s \text {a n d} q \leq t} | x _ {p q} - x _ {p q} ^ {\prime} | \zeta^ {| p | \vee | q |} \cdot \sum_ {p \leq s} \sum_ {q \leq t} \zeta^ {- (| p | \vee | q |)} (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {s - p + t - q} \\ \leq c \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} \cdot \zeta^ {- (| s | \vee | t |)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

then

$$
\| h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} = \sup _ {s, t \leq 0} | h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) | \zeta^ {| s | \vee | t |} \leq c \alpha \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha} \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta},
$$

yielding the lipschitz bound in part (a).

for part (b), let us denote

$$
\eta (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \mathbb {e} [ u _ {*} \mid f = f ], \qquad v (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \operatorname {v a r} [ u _ {*} \mid f = f ] \qquad \qquad (\mathrm {c}. 1 6)
$$

in the scalar model  $f = \mu \cdot u_{*} + z$ , where  $z \sim \mathcal{n}(0, \sigma^2)$  is independent of  $u_{*}$ . observe that

$$
\mathrm {v a r} [ u _ {*} \mid f ] = \mathrm {v a r} [ \mu^ {- 1} (f - z) \mid f ] = (1 / \mu) ^ {2} \mathrm {v a r} [ z \mid f ],
$$

so that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ v (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) \right] = \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {v a r} \left[ u _ {*} \mid f \right] \right] = (1 / \mu) ^ {2} \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {v a r} [ z \mid f ] \right] \leq (1 / \mu) ^ {2} \operatorname {v a r} [ z ] = (\sigma / \mu) ^ {2}. \tag {c.17}
$$

for ease of notation, let us write  $\mathbb{e}$  for  $\mathbb{e}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}$  in the definition of the function  $h^{\delta}$ . we denote

$$
u _ {t} = \mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {*} \mid f _ {t} \right] = \eta \left(f _ {t} \mid \alpha x _ {t t}, y _ {t t}\right).
$$

observe that the above then implies

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (u _ {*} - u _ {t}) ^ {2} ] = \mathbb {e} [ v (f _ {t} | \alpha x _ {t t}, y _ {t t}) ] \leq y _ {t t} / (\alpha x _ {t t}) ^ {2}.
$$

for  $\mathbf{x} \in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}$  and  $\mathbf{y} \in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$ , applying  $y_{tt} \leq (3/2)\kappa_2$  and  $x_{tt}^2 \geq 3/4$  for all  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  sufficiently large, this shows

$$
\mathbb {e} \big [ (u _ {*} - u _ {t}) ^ {2} \big ] \leq 2 \kappa_ {2} / \alpha^ {2}.
$$

now applying  $\mathbb{e}[u_{*}u_{t}] = \mathbb{e}[u_{t}^{2}]$  and  $\mathbb{e}[u_{*}^{2}] = 1$ , we may write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {s} u _ {t} \right] - 1 = \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(u _ {s} - u _ {*}\right) \left(u _ {t} - u _ {*}\right) \right] + \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {s} ^ {2} \right] - 1\right) + \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {t} ^ {2} \right] - 1\right) \\ = \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(u _ {s} - u _ {*}\right) \left(u _ {t} - u _ {*}\right) \right] - \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(u _ {s} - u _ {*}\right) ^ {2} \right] - \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(u _ {t} - u _ {*}\right) ^ {2} \right] \tag {c.18} \\ \geq - \frac {3}{2} \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ (u _ {s} - u _ {*}) ^ {2} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ (u _ {t} - u _ {*}) ^ {2} \right]\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

so  $\mathbb{e}[u_s u_t] \geq 1 - 6\kappa_2 / \alpha^2$ . by cauchy-schwarz, also  $\mathbb{e}[u_s u_t] \leq \mathbb{e}[u_s^2]^{1/2} \mathbb{e}[u_t^2]^{1/2} \leq \mathbb{e}[u_*^2] = 1$ , so  $h^\delta(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}) \in \mathcal{x}_{i_\delta}$ .

to show the lipschitz bound for  $h^\delta$ , from (c.18) we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| = \left| \mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {s} u _ {t} \right] - \mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {s} ^ {\prime} u _ {t} ^ {\prime} \right] \right| \\ \leq \left| \mathbb {e} \big [ (u _ {s} - u _ {*}) (u _ {t} - u _ {*}) \big ] - \mathbb {e} \big [ (u _ {s} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) (u _ {t} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) \big ] \right| \\ + \left| \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {s} - u _ {*}) ^ {2} ] - \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {s} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) ^ {2} ] \right| + \left| \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {t} - u _ {*}) ^ {2} ] - \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {t} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) ^ {2} ] \right| \tag {c.19} \\ \end{array}
$$

so it suffices to bound these three terms individually. we demonstrate the bound for the first term: let us write

$$
\left( \begin{array}{c} f _ {s} \\ f _ {t} \end{array} \right) = \left( \begin{array}{c} \mu_ {s} \\ \mu_ {t} \end{array} \right) u _ {*} + \left( \begin{array}{c c} \beta_ {s s} & \beta_ {s t} \\ \beta_ {s t} & \beta_ {t t} \end{array} \right) \left( \begin{array}{c} w _ {s} \\ w _ {t} \end{array} \right)
$$

where

$$
(\mu_ {s}, \mu_ {t}) = (\alpha x _ {s s}, \alpha x _ {t t}), \qquad \left( \begin{array}{c c} \beta_ {s s} & \beta_ {s t} \\ \beta_ {s t} & \beta_ {t t} \end{array} \right) = \left(\pi \left( \begin{array}{c c} y _ {s s} & y _ {s t} \\ y _ {t s} & y _ {t t} \end{array} \right)\right) ^ {1 / 2}, \qquad (w _ {s}, w _ {t}) \sim \mathcal {n} (0, \mathrm {i d}).
$$

here  $(\cdot)^{1/2}$  denotes the positive-semidefinite matrix square-root, given explicitly for  $2 \times 2$  matrices by

$$
m ^ {1 / 2} = \frac {1}{\sqrt {\operatorname {t r} m + 2 \sqrt {\det m}}} (m + \sqrt {\det m} \cdot \operatorname {i d} _ {2 \times 2}). \tag {c.20}
$$

then

$$
u _ {s} = \eta (f _ {s} \mid \alpha x _ {s s}, y _ {s s}) = \eta (f _ {s} \mid \mu_ {s}, \beta_ {s s} ^ {2} + \beta_ {s t} ^ {2}), \qquad u _ {t} = \eta (f _ {t} \mid \alpha x _ {t t}, y _ {t t}) = \eta (f _ {t} \mid \mu_ {t}, \beta_ {s t} ^ {2} + \beta_ {t t} ^ {2}).
$$

for  $\lambda \in [0,1]$ , writing the same forms for  $f_s', f_t', u_s', u_t'$ , we define the linear interpolations

$$
\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} = \lambda \mu_ {s} + (1 - \lambda) \mu_ {s} ^ {\prime}, \qquad \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} = \lambda \beta_ {s s} + (1 - \lambda) \beta_ {s s} ^ {\prime}, \qquad f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} = \lambda f _ {s} + (1 - \lambda) f _ {s} ^ {\prime},
$$

and similarly for  $\mu_t, \beta_{st}, \beta_{tt}, f_t$ . finally, we define

$$
\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} = (\beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} + (\beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}, \qquad \sigma_ {t t} ^ {\lambda} = (\beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} + (\beta_ {t t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2},
$$

$$
u _ {s} ^ {\lambda} = \eta \left(f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} \mid \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}, \sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}\right), \quad u _ {t} ^ {\lambda} = \eta \left(f _ {t} ^ {\lambda} \mid \mu_ {t} ^ {\lambda}, \sigma_ {t t} ^ {\lambda}\right). \tag {c.21}
$$

denoting  $\partial_{\lambda}$  as the derivative in  $\lambda$ , we then have

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (u _ {s} - u _ {*}) (u _ {t} - u _ {*}) ] - \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {s} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) (u _ {t} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) ] = \int_ {0} ^ {1} \partial_ {\lambda} \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - u _ {*}) (u _ {t} ^ {\lambda} - u _ {*}) ] d \lambda
$$

where this latter expectation is over the underlying random variables  $(u_{*},w_{s},w_{t})$ . the law of  $(u_{*},w_{s},w_{t})$  does not depend on  $\lambda$ , so we may take the derivative inside this expectation, yielding

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {s} - u _ {*}) (u _ {t} - u _ {*}) ] - \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {s} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) (u _ {t} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}) ] \right| \\ \leq \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left| \partial_ {\lambda} u _ {s} ^ {\lambda} \cdot \left(u _ {t} ^ {\lambda} - u _ {*}\right) \right| + \left| \left(u _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - u _ {*}\right) \cdot \partial_ {\lambda} u _ {t} ^ {\lambda} \right| \right] d \lambda \tag {c.22} \\ \end{array}
$$

observe from (c.20) and the condition  $y_{ss}, y_{st}, y_{ts}, y_{tt} > 0$  that  $\beta_{ss}, \beta_{st}, \beta_{tt} > 0$ . then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \leq 2 \lambda^ {2} (\beta_ {s s} ^ {2} + \beta_ {s t} ^ {2}) + 2 (1 - \lambda) ^ {2} (\beta_ {s s} ^ {\prime 2} + \beta_ {s t} ^ {\prime 2}) = 2 \lambda^ {2} y _ {s s} + 2 (1 - \lambda) ^ {2} y _ {s s} ^ {\prime} \\ \sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \geq \lambda^ {2} (\beta_ {s s} ^ {2} + \beta_ {s t} ^ {2}) + (1 - \lambda) ^ {2} (\beta_ {s s} ^ {\prime 2} + \beta_ {s t} ^ {\prime 2}) = \lambda^ {2} y _ {s s} + (1 - \lambda) ^ {2} y _ {s s} ^ {\prime}. \\ \end{array}
$$

recalling that  $\mathbf{x} \in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}$  and  $\mathbf{y} \in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$ , we get the bounds

$$
\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} \leq \alpha , \quad \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} \geq \alpha - c / \alpha , \quad \sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \leq c, \quad \sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \geq c. \tag {c.23}
$$

for some constants  $c, c > 0$ . applying these bounds together with (c.17) yields  $\mathbb{e}[(u_s^\lambda - u_*)^2] \leq c / \alpha^2$ . the same argument holds for  $\mathbb{e}[(u_t^\lambda - u_*)^2]$ . then applying cauchy-schwarz to (c.22),

$$
\left| \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(u _ {s} - u _ {*}\right) \left(u _ {t} - u _ {*}\right) \right] - \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(u _ {s} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}\right) \left(u _ {t} ^ {\prime} - u _ {*}\right) \right] \right| \leq \frac {c}{\alpha} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\partial_ {\lambda} u _ {s} ^ {\lambda}\right) ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} + \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\partial_ {\lambda} u _ {t} ^ {\lambda}\right) ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2}\right) d \lambda . \tag {c.24}
$$

we proceed to bound  $\mathbb{e}[(\partial_{\lambda}u_s^{\lambda})^2 ]$ : recall  $\eta (f\mid \mu ,\sigma^2)$  and  $v(f\mid \mu ,\sigma^2)$  from (c.16), and define in addition

$$
k (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \mathrm {c o v} [ u _ {*}, u _ {*} ^ {2} \mid f = f ].
$$

differentiating the explicit form for  $\eta(f \mid \mu, \sigma^2)$  in (3.10) yields

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial f} \eta (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \operatorname {c o v} \left[ u _ {*}, \frac {\partial}{\partial f} \left(- \frac {(f - \mu u _ {*}) ^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}\right) | f = f \right] = \frac {\mu}{\sigma^ {2}} \cdot v (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2})
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial \mu} \eta (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \mathrm {c o v} \left[ u _ {*}, \frac {\partial}{\partial \mu} \left(- \frac {(f - \mu u _ {*}) ^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}\right) \bigg | f = f \right] = \frac {f}{\sigma^ {2}} v (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) - \frac {\mu}{\sigma^ {2}} k (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2})
$$

$$
\frac {\partial}{\partial \sigma^ {2}} \eta (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \mathrm {c o v} \left[ u _ {*}, \frac {\partial}{\partial \sigma^ {2}} \left(- \frac {(f - \mu u _ {*}) ^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {2}}\right) \bigg | f = f \right] = - \frac {f \mu}{\sigma^ {4}} v (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) + \frac {\mu^ {2}}{2 \sigma^ {4}} k (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}).
$$

let us write as shorthand

$$
v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} = v (f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} \mid \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}, \sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}), \qquad k _ {s} ^ {\lambda} = k (f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} \mid \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}, \sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}).
$$

then applying the chain rule to differentiate (c.21),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \partial_ {\lambda} u _ {s} ^ {\lambda} = \frac {\partial \eta}{\partial f} \cdot \left(\partial_ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} \cdot u _ {*} + \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \cdot w _ {s} + \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda} \cdot w _ {t}\right) + \frac {\partial \eta}{\partial \mu} \cdot \partial_ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \frac {\partial \eta}{\partial \sigma^ {2}} \cdot \left(2 \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \cdot \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} + 2 \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda} \cdot \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda}\right) \\ = \left(\frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} u _ {*} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \frac {1}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - \frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} k _ {s} ^ {\lambda}\right) \partial_ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \left(\frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} w _ {s} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - \frac {2 \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{(\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}} f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \frac {(\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}}{(\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}} k _ {s} ^ {\lambda}\right) \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \\ + \left(\frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} w _ {t} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - \frac {2 \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{(\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}} f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \frac {(\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda}}{(\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}} k _ {s} ^ {\lambda}\right) \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda} \\ \equiv \mathrm {i} \cdot \partial_ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \mathrm {i i} \cdot \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} + \mathrm {i i i} \cdot \partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda}. \tag {c.25} \\ \end{array}
$$

we bound the expected squares of these coefficients i, ii, iii: applying (c.23) and cauchy-schwarz,

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (u _ {*} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} ] \leq \mathbb {e} [ u _ {*} ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2} \mathbb {e} [ (v _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2} \leq c \cdot \mathbb {e} [ (v _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} [ (f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} ] \leq \mathbb {e} [ (f _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2} \mathbb {e} [ (v _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2} \leq c \alpha^ {2} \mathbb {e} [ (v _ {t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

then the coefficient for  $\partial_{\lambda}\mu_s^{\lambda}$  in (c.25) has expected square bounded as

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \mathrm {i} ^ {2} ] = \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} u _ {*} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \frac {1}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - \frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} k _ {s} ^ {\lambda}\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq c \alpha^ {2} \left(\mathbb {e} [ (v _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2} + \mathbb {e} [ (k _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} ]\right).
$$

recalling the identity

$$
v (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \operatorname {v a r} [ u _ {*} \mid f ] = \operatorname {v a r} [ \mu^ {- 1} (f - \sigma w) \mid f ] = (\sigma / \mu) ^ {2} \operatorname {v a r} [ w \mid f ], \qquad \mathrm {(c . 2 6)}
$$

we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ v (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) ^ {4} \right] = (\sigma / \mu) ^ {8} \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {v a r} [ w \mid f ] ^ {4} \right] \\ = (\sigma / \mu) ^ {8} \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbb {e} [ (w - \mathbb {e} [ w \mid f ]) ^ {2} \mid f ] ^ {4} \right] \\ \leq (\sigma / \mu) ^ {8} \mathbb {e} [ (w - \mathbb {e} [ w \mid f ]) ^ {8} ] \leq c (\sigma / \mu) ^ {8}, \tag {c.27} \\ \end{array}
$$

the last inequality applying  $w \sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$  and proposition f.4. so  $\mathbb{e}[(v_s^\lambda)^4] \leq c\alpha^{-8}$ . we also have the identity

$$
\begin{array}{l} k (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) = \operatorname {c o v} \left[ u _ {*}, u _ {*} ^ {2} \mid f \right] = \mu^ {- 3} \operatorname {c o v} \left[ f - \sigma w, (f - \sigma w) ^ {2} \mid f \right] \\ = \left(2 \sigma^ {2} f / \mu^ {3}\right) \operatorname {v a r} [ w \mid f ] - \left(\sigma^ {3} / \mu^ {3}\right) \operatorname {c o v} [ w, w ^ {2} \mid f ], \tag {c.28} \\ \end{array}
$$

so

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ k (f \mid \mu , \sigma^ {2}) ^ {2} ] \\ \leq \left(8 \sigma^ {4} / \mu^ {6}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ f ^ {2} \operatorname {v a r} [ w \mid f ] ^ {2} \right] + \left(2 \sigma^ {6} / \mu^ {6}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {c o v} [ w, w ^ {2} \mid f ] ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \left(8 \sigma^ {4} / \mu^ {6}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ f ^ {4} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {v a r} [ w \mid f ] ^ {4} \right] ^ {1 / 2} + \left(2 \sigma^ {6} / \mu^ {6}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {v a r} [ w \mid f ] ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {v a r} \left[ w ^ {2} \mid f \right] ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq c (\sigma^ {4} / \mu^ {6}) \mathbb {e} [ f ^ {4} ] ^ {1 / 2} + c (\sigma / \mu) ^ {6} \\ \end{array}
$$

by similar arguments. then  $\mathbb{e}[(k_s^\lambda)^2 ]\leq c\alpha^{-4}$ , and we obtain

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \mathrm {i} ^ {2} \right] \leq c / \alpha^ {2}. \tag {c.29}
$$

for the coefficients of  $\partial_{\lambda}\beta_{ss}^{\lambda}$  and  $\partial_{\lambda}\beta_{st}^{\lambda}$  in (c.25), we first apply a cancellation of the leading-order term: comparing the identities (c.26) and (c.28), we have

$$
k _ {s} ^ {\lambda} = \frac {2}{\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}} f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - \frac {\left(\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}\right) ^ {3 / 2}}{\left(\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}\right) ^ {3}} \mathrm {c o v} [ w, w ^ {2} \mid f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} ]
$$

where  $w \sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$  is the gaussian variable such that  $f_{s}^{\lambda} = \mu_{s}^{\lambda} \cdot u_{*} + \sqrt{\sigma_{ss}^{\lambda}} \cdot w$ . then the coefficient of  $\partial_{\lambda} \beta_{ss}^{\lambda}$  in (c.25) is

$$
\mathrm {i i} = \frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} w _ {s} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - \frac {2 \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{(\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}} f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} + \frac {(\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}}{(\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}} k _ {s} ^ {\lambda} = \frac {\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}}{\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}} w _ {s} v _ {s} ^ {\lambda} - \frac {\beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}}{\mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda} (\sigma_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {1 / 2}} \mathrm {c o v} [ w, w ^ {2} | f _ {s} ^ {\lambda} ].
$$

similar arguments as above yield  $\mathbb{e}[(w_s^2 v_s^\lambda)^2 ]\leq c\alpha^{-4}$  and  $\mathbb{e}[\mathrm{cov}[w,w^2\mid f_s^\lambda ]^2 ]\leq c$  . thus we obtain the bound

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \pi^ {2} \right] \leq c / \alpha^ {2}. \tag {c.30}
$$

for the coefficient of  $\partial_{\lambda}\beta_{st}^{\lambda}$ , the same argument shows

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \mathrm {i i i} ^ {2} \right] \leq c / \alpha^ {2}. \tag {c.31}
$$

applying (c.29), (c.30), and (c.31) to (c.25) yields

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ (\partial_ {\lambda} u _ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} \right] \leq \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \left((\partial_ {\lambda} \mu_ {s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} + (\partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s s} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} + (\partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}\right).
$$

the same argument applies for  $u_{t}^{\lambda}$  to show

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ (\partial_ {\lambda} u _ {t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} \right] \leq \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \left((\partial_ {\lambda} \mu_ {t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} + (\partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {s t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2} + (\partial_ {\lambda} \beta_ {t t} ^ {\lambda}) ^ {2}\right).
$$

by the definition of our linear interpolation,  $\partial_{\lambda}\mu_s^{\lambda} = \mu_s - \mu_s'$  which does not depend on  $\lambda$ , and similarly for the other derivatives above. then applying this to (c.24) yields a bound of

$$
\frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \bigl (| \mu_ {s} - \mu_ {s} ^ {\prime} | + | \mu_ {t} - \mu_ {t} ^ {\prime} | + | \beta_ {s s} - \beta_ {s s} ^ {\prime} | + | \beta_ {s t} - \beta_ {s t} ^ {\prime} | + | \beta_ {t t} - \beta_ {t t} ^ {\prime} | \bigr)
$$

for the first term in (c.19).

the same argument applied with  $s = t$  bounds the other two terms of (c.19), and we obtain

$$
| h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) | \leq \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \big (| \mu_ {s} - \mu_ {s} ^ {\prime} | + | \mu_ {t} - \mu_ {t} ^ {\prime} | + | \beta_ {s s} - \beta_ {s s} ^ {\prime} | + | \beta_ {s t} - \beta_ {s t} ^ {\prime} | + | \beta_ {t t} - \beta_ {t t} ^ {\prime} | \big)
$$

observe that  $|\mu_s - \mu_s'| = \alpha |x_{ss} - x_{ss}'|$ . furthermore, it may be verified from (c.13) and (c.20) that the map  $(y_{ss}, y_{st}, y_{ts}, y_{tt}) \mapsto (\beta_{ss}, \beta_{st}, \beta_{tt})$  is lipschitz over  $y_{ss}, y_{st}, y_{ts}, y_{tt} \in i_\sigma$ , since  $i_\sigma$  is bounded away from 0. then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}\right) \right| \leq \frac {c}{\alpha} \left(| x _ {s s} - x _ {s s} ^ {\prime} | + | x _ {t t} - x _ {t t} ^ {\prime} |\right) \\ + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \left(| y _ {s s} - y _ {s s} ^ {\prime} | + | y _ {s t} - y _ {s t} ^ {\prime} | + | y _ {t s} - y _ {t s} ^ {\prime} | + | y _ {t t} - y _ {t t} ^ {\prime} |\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

this implies

$$
\| h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} = \sup  _ {s, t \leq 0} | h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) _ {s, t} - h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) _ {s, t} | \zeta^ {| s | \vee | t |} \leq \frac {c}{\alpha} \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta},
$$

which shows part (b).

the next lemma establishes the approximation of the state evolution for  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$  by the fixed map  $h^{\sigma}$ , and the state evolution for  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  by the fixed map  $h^{\delta}$ .

lemma c.2. in the setting of theorem 3.1(b), there exist constants  $c, \alpha_0 > 0$  such that for all  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  and  $t \geq 1$ :

(a)  $\delta_{st} \in i_{\delta}$  and  $\sigma_{st} \in i_{\sigma}$  for all  $s, t \in \{2, \ldots, t\}$  whereas  $\delta_{st} \in [-1, 1]$  and  $\sigma_{st} \in [-3\kappa_2/2, 3\kappa_2/2]$  if  $s = 1$  and  $t \in \{1, \ldots, t\}$  or  $t = 1$  and  $s \in \{1, \ldots, t\}$ .  
(b) consider  $\pmb{\delta}_{t},\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$  as elements of  $\mathcal{x}$ , with the coordinate identifications and zero-padding of (c.9). then for any  $(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c \alpha \| \mathbf {x} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {y} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1} \| _ {\zeta} + c \zeta^ {t / 2}.
$$

(c) similarly, for any  $(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$

$$
\| \pmb {\delta} _ {t + 1} - h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \pmb {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {y} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + c \zeta^ {t}.
$$

proof. for part (a), the arguments are similar to those in the proof of lemma c.1: we induct on  $t$ . note that  $\delta_{11} = \mathbb{e}[u_1^2] \leq 1$  so the claim holds for  $\pmb{\delta}_{1}$ . suppose that the claims hold for  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  and  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t-1}$ . to establish the claim for  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$ , for any  $s, t \geq 1$  we may write (3.8) as

$$
\sigma_ {s t} = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {s - 1} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {t - 1} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} \sigma_ {s t} ^ {(j, k)}.
$$

observe that  $\sigma_{st}^{(0,0)} = \delta_{st}$ , which belongs to  $i_{\delta}$  if  $s,t\geq 2$  and to  $[-1,1]$  otherwise. observe also that  $|\sigma_{st}^{(j,k)}|\leq (c / \alpha)^{j + k}$  for all  $(j,k)\neq (0,0)$  by the same argument as in lemma c.1(a). (here, each factor  $1 - \delta_{ii}$  has an index  $i\geq 2$ , so this is at most  $c / \alpha^2$ .) for sufficiently large  $\alpha$ , applying  $|\kappa_{j + k + 2}|\leq (16c_0)^{j + k + 2}$  and summing over  $(j,k)\neq (0,0)$  shows the claim for  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$ . now suppose that the claims of part (a) hold for  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  and  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$ , and consider  $\pmb{\delta}_{t + 1}$ . for  $s = 1$  or  $t = 1$ , we apply  $|\delta_{s,t + 1}| = |\mathbb{e}[u_1u_{t + 1}]|\leq \mathbb{e}[u_1^2 ]^{1 / 2}\mathbb{e}[u_{t + 1}^2 ]^{1 / 2}\leq 1$ . for  $s,t\geq 2$ , the argument of (c.18) in lemma c.1(b) shows

$$
\delta_ {s t} - 1 \geq - \frac {3}{2} \left(\frac {\sigma_ {s - 1 , s - 1}}{\mu_ {s - 1} ^ {2}} + \frac {\sigma_ {t - 1 , t - 1}}{\mu_ {t - 1} ^ {2}}\right).
$$

we recall that  $\mu_{j} = \alpha \delta_{jj}$  if  $j\geq 2$  and  $\mu_{1} = \alpha \varepsilon$ . for sufficiently large  $\alpha$ , this implies  $\delta_{st}\in i_{\delta}$  in both cases (where the constant  $c$  defining  $i_{\delta}$  depends on  $\varepsilon$ ), so the claim holds for  $\pmb{\delta}_{t + 1}$ . this concludes the induction and establishes part (a).

for part (b), let us now index the entries of  $\delta_t, \sigma_{t-1}, \sigma_t$  by  $s, t \leq 0$ , to coincide with the indices of  $\mathcal{x}$ . let  $\mathbf{x}' \in \mathcal{x}_{i_\delta}$  be  $\delta_t$  with each coordinate projected onto the interval  $i_\delta$ , and let  $\mathbf{y}' \in \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma}$  be the analogous projection of  $\sigma_{t-1}$  onto  $i_\sigma$ . we first bound  $\|\sigma_t - h^\sigma(\mathbf{x}', \mathbf{y}')\|_\zeta$ . observe that by part (a) already shown,  $\mathbf{x}'$  must coincide with  $\delta_t$  in the lower-right  $(t-1) \times (t-1)$  corner, and  $\mathbf{y}'$  must coincide with  $\sigma_{t-1}$  in the lower-right  $(t-2) \times (t-2)$  corner. applying again  $|\kappa_{j+k+2}| \leq (16c_0)^{j+k+2}$ , we may write

$$
(\pmb {\sigma} _ {t}) _ {s t} = \sum_ {0 \leq j, k <   t / 4} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t}) _ {s t} ^ {(j, k)} + r _ {s t}
$$

where this remainder satisfies  $|r_{st}| \leq c(c' / \alpha)^{t / 4}$  for  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  sufficiently large. similarly, we may write

$$
h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) = \sum_ {0 \leq j, k <   t / 4} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} h _ {s t} ^ {(j, k)} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) + r _ {s t} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime})
$$

where  $|r_{st}(\mathbf{x}',\mathbf{y}')| \leq c(c' / \alpha)^{t/4}$ . comparing the forms of (3.8) and (c.12), observe that for  $s, t > -t/2$  and  $j, k < t/4$ , we have  $(\pmb{\sigma}_t)_{st}^{(j,k)} = h_{st}^{(j,k)}(\mathbf{x}',\mathbf{y}')$  because these are identical functions of the entries of the lower-right  $(3t/4) \times (3t/4)$  sub-matrices of  $\pmb{\delta}_t$  and  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t-1}$ . so

$$
| (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t}) _ {s t} - h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) | \leq c (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {t / 4} \mathrm {f o r a l l} s, t > - t / 2.
$$

applying the trivial bound

$$
| (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t}) _ {s t} - h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) | \leq | (\pmb {\sigma} _ {t}) _ {s t} | + | h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) | \leq 3 \kappa_ {2} \mathrm {f o r} s \leq - t / 2 \mathrm {o r} t \leq - t / 2,
$$

we obtain

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {t / 4} + c \zeta^ {t / 2} \leq c \zeta^ {t / 2}
$$

for  $\alpha >\alpha_0$  large enough. by lemma c.1(a) and the definitions of  $\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}$ , we have also

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c \alpha \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} \\ \leq c \alpha \| \mathbf {x} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {y} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1} \| _ {\zeta}, \\ \end{array}
$$

and combining these shows part (b).

for part (c), now let  $\mathbf{x}'$  and  $\mathbf{y}'$  be the coordinate-wise projections of  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  and  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$  onto  $i_{\delta}$  and  $i_{\sigma}$ . we bound  $\| \pmb{\delta}_{t + 1} - h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x}',\mathbf{y}')\|_{\zeta}$ . observe that  $\mathbf{x}'$  and  $\mathbf{y}'$  coincide with  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  and  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$  in their lower-right  $(t - 1)\times (t - 1)$  corners, and each  $2\times 2$  principal minor of  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$  must be positive-semidefinite because  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$  is a covariance matrix. thus

$$
\big (\pmb {\delta} _ {t + 1} \big) _ {s t} = h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \mathrm {f o r a l l} s, t \in \{- t + 2, \dots , 0 \}.
$$

applying the trivial bound  $|(\delta_{t + 1})_{st} - h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x}',\mathbf{y}')_{st}| \leq 2$  for the remaining  $s,t$ , and

$$
\| h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}
$$

$$
\leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {y} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta}
$$

similar to the above, we obtain part (c).

proof of theorem 3.1(b). theorem 3.1(a) shows

$$
\lim _ {n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {u} _ {t} \| ^ {2} = \mathbb {e} [ u _ {t} ^ {2} ] = \delta_ {t t}, \quad \lim _ {n \to \infty} n ^ {- 1} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {*} = \mathbb {e} [ u _ {t} u _ {*} ] = \mathbb {e} [ u _ {t} ^ {2} ] = \delta_ {t t}.
$$

thus, it suffices to show that  $\delta_{tt} \to \delta_*$  as  $t \to \infty$ , where  $(\delta_*, \sigma_*) \in i_\delta \times i_\sigma$  is the unique fixed point of (3.14).

consider the map  $g:\mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}\to \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$  that is the successive composition of

$$
(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \mapsto (\mathbf {x}, h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y})), \qquad (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \mapsto (h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}), \mathbf {y})
$$

which approximates  $(\pmb{\delta}_{t},\pmb{\sigma}_{t - 1})\mapsto (\pmb{\delta}_{t + 1},\pmb{\sigma}_{t})$ . writing its components as  $g = (g_{x},g_{y})$ , lemma c.1 implies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| g _ {y} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - g _ {y} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c \alpha \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} \\ \| g _ {x} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - g _ {x} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| g _ {y} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - g _ {y} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \\ \leq (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha^ {3}) \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}. \\ \end{array}
$$

then defining the norm  $\| \cdot \|_{\zeta ,\alpha}$  on the product space  $\mathcal{x}_{i_\delta} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma}$  by

$$
\| (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} = \| \mathbf {x} \| _ {\zeta} + (1 / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {y} \| _ {\zeta},
$$

this shows

$$
\| g (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - g \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}\right) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}\right) - \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}\right) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \leq \tau \| \left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}\right) - \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}\right) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha}
$$

for some constant  $\tau \in (0,1)$  and all  $\alpha >\alpha_0$  sufficiently large. thus  $g$  is a contraction on  $\mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$  in this norm, and admits a unique fixed point  $(\mathbf{x}_{*},\mathbf{y}_{*})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$  by the banach fixed point theorem.

we claim that this fixed point is such that  $\mathbf{x}_*$  equals a constant  $\delta_* \in i_{\delta}$  and  $\mathbf{y}_*$  equals a constant  $\sigma_* \in i_{\sigma}$  in every coordinate. by the definitions of the functions  $h^{\delta}$  and  $h^{\sigma}$ , such a pair is a fixed point if and only if

$$
\sigma_ {*} = \sum_ {j, k = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {j + k + 2} \left(\frac {\alpha \delta_ {*}}{\sigma_ {*}} (1 - \delta_ {*})\right) ^ {j + k} \delta_ {*}, \qquad \delta_ {*} = \mathbb {e} [ \mathbb {e} [ u _ {*} \mid f ] ^ {2} ]
$$

in the model  $f = \alpha \delta_{*}u_{*} + z$  where  $z\sim \mathcal{n}(0,\sigma_{*})$ . these equations may be rewritten as

$$
\sigma_ {*} = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} (k + 1) \kappa_ {k + 2} \left(\frac {\alpha \delta_ {*}}{\sigma_ {*}} (1 - \delta_ {*})\right) ^ {k} \delta_ {*} = \delta_ {*} r ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\alpha \delta_ {*} (1 - \delta_ {*})}{\sigma_ {*}}\right)
$$

$$
1 - \delta_ {*} = \mathbb {e} [ (u _ {*} - \mathbb {e} [ u _ {*} \mid f ]) ^ {2} ] = \mathrm {m m s e} \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} ^ {2}}{\sigma_ {*}}\right)
$$

which is exactly the pair of fixed point equations (3.14).

to argue that such a fixed point exists and is unique in  $i_{\delta} \times i_{\sigma}$ , consider the pair of scalar maps

$$
h _ {\mathrm {s c}} ^ {\sigma} (\delta_ {*}, \sigma_ {*}) = \delta_ {*} r ^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\alpha \delta_ {*} (1 - \delta_ {*})}{\sigma_ {*}}\right), \qquad h _ {\mathrm {s c}} ^ {\delta} (\delta_ {*}, \sigma_ {*}) = 1 - \mathrm {m m s e} \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} ^ {2}}{\sigma_ {*}}\right) = \mathbb {e} [ \mathbb {e} [ u _ {*} | f ] ^ {2} ].
$$

denote their composition as  $g_{\mathrm{sc}}(\delta_{*}, \sigma_{*})$ . specializing lemma c.1 to pairs  $(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y})$  and  $(\mathbf{x}', \mathbf{y}')$  where  $\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{x}', \mathbf{y}, \mathbf{y}'$  are each equal to a constant in every coordinate, our preceding arguments imply that  $g_{\mathrm{sc}}: i_{\delta} \times i_{\sigma} \to i_{\delta} \times i_{\sigma}$  is a contraction with respect to the norm  $\| (\delta, \sigma) \| = |\delta| + (1 / \alpha^2) |\sigma|$ . then there exists a unique fixed point  $(\delta_{*}, \sigma_{*}) \in i_{\delta} \times i_{\sigma}$  for  $g_{\mathrm{sc}}$ , by the banach fixed point theorem applied to this scalar setting. so the fixed point  $(\mathbf{x}_{*}, \mathbf{y}_{*})$  for  $g$  must be such that  $\mathbf{x}_{*}$  is constant and equal to  $\delta_{*}$ , and  $\mathbf{y}_{*}$  is constant and equal to  $\sigma_{*}$ , by uniqueness of  $(\mathbf{x}_{*}, \mathbf{y}_{*})$ .

finally, to conclude the proof, fix any  $\varepsilon > 0$ . let  $g^{t_0} = g \circ \ldots \circ g$  denote the  $t_0$ -fold composition of  $g$ . for the above contraction rate  $\tau$  of this function  $g$ , the banach fixed point theorem implies quantitatively, for any  $\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y} \in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$ ,

$$
\| g ^ {t _ {0}} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - (\mathbf {x} _ {*}, \mathbf {y} _ {*}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \leq \tau^ {t _ {0}} \| (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - (\mathbf {x} _ {*}, \mathbf {y} _ {*}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \leq c \tau^ {t _ {0}}
$$

where the second inequality holds because  $\mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$  is bounded under  $\| \cdot \|_{\zeta, \alpha}$ . then for all large enough  $t_0$ , we have

$$
\| g ^ {t _ {0}} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) - (\mathbf {x} _ {*}, \mathbf {y} _ {*}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} <   \varepsilon / 2.
$$

by lemma c.2(b) and (c), for any  $(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$  and any  $t\geq 1$ , also

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}\right) - g (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \right\| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \\ = \| \pmb {\delta} _ {t + 1} - h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y})) \| _ {\zeta} + (1 / \alpha^ {2}) \| \pmb {\sigma} _ {t} - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta} \\ \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \pmb {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + c \zeta^ {t} + [ (c / \alpha^ {2}) + (1 / \alpha^ {2}) ] \big (c \alpha \| \mathbf {x} - \pmb {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {y} - \pmb {\sigma} _ {t - 1} \| _ {\zeta} + c \zeta^ {t / 2} \big) \\ \leq \tau \| (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1}) - (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} + c ^ {\prime} \zeta^ {t / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

iterating this bound,

$$
\| (\pmb {\delta} _ {t + t _ {0}}, \pmb {\sigma} _ {t + t _ {0} - 1}) - g ^ {t _ {0}} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \leq \tau^ {t _ {0}} \| (\pmb {\delta} _ {t}, \pmb {\sigma} _ {t - 1}) - (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} + c ^ {\prime} \zeta^ {t / 2} (1 - \tau) ^ {- 1}.
$$

for all large enough  $t_0$  and  $t$ , this is also at most  $\varepsilon / 2$ . thus, combining with the above,

$$
\limsup_{t\to \infty}\left| \delta_{tt} - \delta_{*}\right|\leq \limsup_{t\to \infty}\left\| \left(\boldsymbol{\delta}_{t + 1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{t}\right) - \left(\mathbf{x}_{*},\mathbf{y}_{*}\right)\right\|_{\zeta ,\alpha}\leq \varepsilon .
$$

here  $\varepsilon > 0$  is arbitrary, so  $|\delta_{tt} - \delta_*| \to 0$  as desired.

c.2.2. rectangular matrices. we now prove theorem 3.4(b) using a similar argument.

recall that the amp algorithm is given by (3.19-3.22), where  $v_{t}(\cdot)$  and  $u_{t + 1}(\cdot)$  are the single-iterate posterior-mean denoisers in (3.27). as in the symmetric square setting, we have

$$
u _ {t + 1} ^ {\prime} (f _ {t}) = \frac {\mu_ {t}}{\sigma_ {t t}} \operatorname {v a r} [ u _ {*} \mid f _ {t} = f _ {t} ] = \frac {\mu_ {t}}{\sigma_ {t t}} (1 - \delta_ {t + 1, t + 1})
$$

$$
v _ {t} ^ {\prime} (g _ {t}) = \frac {\nu_ {t}}{\omega_ {t t}} \mathrm {v a r} [ v _ {*} | g _ {t} = g _ {t} ] = \frac {\nu_ {t}}{\omega_ {t t}} (1 - \gamma_ {t t}).
$$

here,

$$
\mu_ {t} = (\alpha / \gamma) \gamma_ {t t}, \qquad \nu_ {t} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \alpha \varepsilon & \text {i f} t = 1 \\ \alpha \delta_ {t t} & \text {i f} t \geq 2. \end{array} \right.
$$

for a sufficiently large constant  $c > 0$ , we define the intervals

$$
i _ {\delta} = i _ {\gamma} = \left[ 1 - \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}}, 1 \right], \quad i _ {\sigma} = \left[ \frac {1}{2} \kappa_ {2}, \frac {3}{2} \kappa_ {2} \right], \quad i _ {\omega} = \left[ \frac {1}{2} \gamma \kappa_ {2}, \frac {3}{2} \gamma \kappa_ {2} \right]
$$

and the corresponding domains  $\mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}},\mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}},\mathcal{x}_{i_{\gamma}},\mathcal{x}_{i_{\omega}}$  . we then define four maps  $h^\omega ,h^\gamma ,h^\sigma ,h^\delta$  ..  $\mathcal{x}_{i_\delta}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_\gamma}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_\omega}\to \mathcal{x}$  that respectively approximate the state evolution functions

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1}, \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t - 1}, \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t - 1}\right) \mapsto \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t}
$$

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1}, \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t - 1}, \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t}\right) \mapsto \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t}
$$

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1}, \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t}\right) \mapsto \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}
$$

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t}\right) \mapsto \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1}.
$$

substituting the above forms of the derivatives into (3.24) and (3.26), and identifying  $(\pmb{\delta}, \pmb{\sigma}, \pmb{\gamma}, \pmb{\omega}) \leftrightarrow (\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}, \mathbf{z}, \mathbf{w})$  with the appropriate offsets of indices, we may define these maps to have the entries

$$
\begin{array}{l} h _ {s t} ^ {\omega} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) = \gamma \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\left(\prod_ {i = s - j + 1} ^ {s} \times \prod_ {i = t - k + 1} ^ {t}\right) \frac {\alpha z _ {i i}}{\gamma y _ {i i}} (1 - x _ {i i}) \frac {\alpha x _ {i - 1 , i - 1}}{w _ {i i}} (1 - z _ {i i})\right) \left(\kappa_ {2 (j + k + 1)} x _ {s - j, t - k} \right. \\ + \kappa_ {2 (j + k + 2)} \frac {\alpha z _ {s - j , s - j}}{\gamma y _ {s - j , s - j}} (1 - x _ {s - j, s - j}) \frac {\alpha z _ {t - k , t - k}}{\gamma y _ {t - k , t - k}} (1 - x _ {t - k, t - k}) z _ {s - j, t - k} \bigg), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
h _ {s t} ^ {\gamma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) = \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {w}} \left[ \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {w}} [ v _ {*} \mid g _ {s} ] \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {w}} [ v _ {*} \mid g _ {t} ] \right]
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {\infty} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\left(\prod_ {i = s - j + 1} ^ {s} \times \prod_ {i = t - k + 1} ^ {t}\right) \frac {\alpha x _ {i i}}{w _ {i i}} (1 - z _ {i i}) \frac {\alpha z _ {i - 1 , i - 1}}{\gamma y _ {i i}} (1 - x _ {i i})\right) \left(\kappa_ {2 (j + k + 1)} z _ {s - j, t - k} \right. \\ \left. + \kappa_ {2 (j + k + 2)} \frac {\alpha x _ {s - j , s - j}}{w _ {s - j , s - j}} (1 - z _ {s - j, s - j}) \frac {\alpha x _ {t - k , t - k}}{w _ {t - k , t - k}} (1 - z _ {t - k, t - k}) x _ {s - j, t - k}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
h _ {s t} ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) = \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {z}, \mathbf {y}} \big [ \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {z}, \mathbf {y}} [ u _ {*} \mid f _ {s} ] \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {z}, \mathbf {y}} [ u _ {*} \mid f _ {t} ] \big ],
$$

where these expectations are taken with respect to the  $(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})$  -dependent joint laws

$$
(g _ {s}, g _ {t}) = (\alpha x _ {s s}, \alpha x _ {t t}) v _ {*} + \mathcal {n} \left(0, \pi \left( \begin{array}{c c} w _ {s s} & w _ {s t} \\ w _ {t s} & w _ {t t} \end{array} \right)\right)
$$

$$
(f _ {s}, f _ {t}) = ((\alpha / \gamma) z _ {s s}, (\alpha / \gamma) z _ {t t}) u _ {*} + \mathcal {n} \left(0, \pi \left( \begin{array}{c c} y _ {s s} & y _ {s t} \\ y _ {t s} & y _ {t t} \end{array} \right)\right)
$$

and  $\pi (\cdot)$  is as defined in (c.13). note that  $h^\gamma$  depends only on  $(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{w})$ , while  $h^{\delta}$  depends only on  $(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{y})$ .

the following establishes lipschitz bounds for these functions, and is analogous to lemma c.1.

lemma c.3. in the setting of theorem 3.4(b), there exist constants  $c, \alpha_0 > 0$  such that for all  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  and  $(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}, \mathbf{z}, \mathbf{w}), (\mathbf{x}', \mathbf{y}', \mathbf{z}', \mathbf{w}') \in \mathcal{x}_{i_\delta} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\gamma} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\omega}$ :

(a)  $h^{\sigma}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}$ $h^\omega (\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_\omega}$  , and

$$
\| h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c (\| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \|) _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) (\| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}),
$$

$$
\| h ^ {\omega} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - h ^ {\omega} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c (\| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \|) _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) (\| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}).
$$

(b)  $h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}$ $h^\gamma (\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_\gamma}$  , and

$$
\| h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta},
$$

$$
\| h ^ {\gamma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - h ^ {\gamma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}.
$$

proof. for part (a), the argument is similar to lemma c.1(a). we denote by  $c, c', c, \ldots$  constants that depend only on  $c_0, \varepsilon, \gamma$ . let us write

$$
h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) = \sum_ {j, k = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (j + k + 1)} h _ {s t, 0} ^ {(j, k)} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) + \kappa_ {2 (j + k + 2)} h _ {s t, 1} ^ {(j, k)} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}).
$$

for both  $a = 0$  and  $a = 1$ , we have

$$
| h _ {s t, a} ^ {(j, k)} | \leq c (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k + a)}.
$$

applying  $|\kappa_{2j}| \leq c^{2j}$  from proposition f.3(b), for  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  large enough, we obtain  $h_{st}^{\sigma}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w}) \in i_{\sigma}$ . we may also verify the bounds, for both  $a = 0$  and  $a = 1$ ,

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , a} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial y _ {p p}} \right| \leq c \left(c ^ {\prime} / \alpha\right) ^ {2 (j + k + a)} \quad \text {i f} p \in \{s - j + 1, \dots , s \} \text {o r} p \in \{t - k + 1, \dots , t \}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , a} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial w _ {p p}} \right| \leq c (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k + a)} \quad \text {i f} p \in \{s - j + 1 - a, \dots , s \} \text {o r} p \in \{t - k + 1 - a, \dots , t \}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , 0} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial x _ {p p}} \right| \leq c \alpha^ {2} (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k)} \quad \text {i f} p \in \{s - j + 1, \dots , s \} \text {o r} p \in \{t - k + 1, \dots , t \}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , 1} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial x _ {p p}} \right| \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} c \alpha^ {2} (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k + 1)} & \text {i f} p \in \{s - j + 1, \dots , s \} \text {o r} p \in \{t - k + 1, \dots , t \} \\ c (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k + 1)} & \text {i f} p = s - j \text {o r} p = t - k \end{array} \right.
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , 0} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial z _ {p p}} \right| \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} c \alpha^ {2} (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k)} & \text {i f} p \in \{s - j + 1, \dots , s \} \text {o r} p \in \{t - k + 1, \dots , t \} \\ c (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k)} & \text {i f} p = s - j \text {o r} t - k \end{array} \right.
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , 1} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial z _ {p p}} \right| \leq c \alpha^ {2} (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k + 1)} \quad \text {i f} p \in \{s - j, \dots , s \} \text {o r} p \in \{t - k, \dots , t \}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , 0} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial z _ {s - j , t - k}} \right| \leq c (c ^ {\prime} / \alpha) ^ {2 (j + k)}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t , 1} ^ {(j , k)}}{\partial x _ {s - j , t - k}} \right| \leq c \left(c ^ {\prime} / \alpha\right) ^ {2 (j + k + 1)}
$$

and all other partial derivatives are 0. multiplying by  $\kappa_{2(j + k + 1)}$  and  $\kappa_{2(j + k + 2)}$ , applying the bound  $|\kappa_{2j}| \leq c^{2j}$  from proposition f.3(b), and summing over  $j, k \geq 0$ , we obtain

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial x _ {p p}} \right|, \left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial z _ {p p}} \right| \leq c \left(\mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2 (s - p)} + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq t \} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2 (t - p)}\right)
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial y _ {p p}} \right|, \left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial w _ {p p}} \right| \leq c \left(\mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2 (s - p + 1)} + \mathbf {1} \{p \leq t \} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2 (s - p + 1)}\right)
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial z _ {p q}} \right| \leq c \cdot \mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \text {a n d} q \leq t \} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2 (s - p + t - q)}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial h _ {s t} ^ {\sigma}}{\partial x _ {p q}} \right| \leq c \cdot \mathbf {1} \{p \leq s \text {a n d} q \leq t \} \left(\frac {c ^ {\prime}}{\alpha}\right) ^ {2 (s - p + t - q + 1)}.
$$

then applying the same argument as in lemma c.1(a), we obtain

$$
\| h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c (\| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \|) _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) (\| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}).
$$

the proof for  $h^\omega$  is analogous, and part (a) follows.

part (b) is a direct consequence of lemma c.1(b), since  $h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y}, \mathbf{z}, \mathbf{w})$  is a function only of  $(\mathbf{z}, \mathbf{y})$  that has the same form as  $h^{\delta}(\mathbf{x}, \mathbf{y})$  in lemma c.1(b), and similarly for  $h^{\gamma}$ .

the next lemma now follows from lemma c.3 via the same argument as lemma c.2, and we omit the proof for brevity.

lemma c.4. in the setting of theorem 3.4(b), there exist constants  $c, \alpha_0 > 0$  such that for all  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  and  $t \geq 1$ :

(a) each entry of  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$ ,  $\pmb{\sigma}_{t}$ ,  $\pmb{\gamma}_{t}$ , and  $\pmb{\omega}_{t}$  belongs respectively to  $i_{\delta}$ ,  $i_{\sigma}$ ,  $i_{\gamma}$ , and  $i_{\omega}$ , except for entries in the first row or column of  $\pmb{\delta}_{t}$  and  $\pmb{\omega}_{t}$  which belong to  $[-1,1]$  and  $[-3\gamma \kappa_{2}/2, 3\gamma \kappa_{2}/2]$ .

(b) for any  $(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})\in \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\gamma}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\omega}}$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} - h ^ {\omega} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) \right\| _ {\zeta} \leq c \big (\left\| \mathbf {x} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \right\| _ {\zeta} + \left\| \mathbf {z} - \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t - 1} \right\| _ {\zeta} \big) \\ + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \left(\| \mathbf {y} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {w} - \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t - 1} \| _ {\zeta}\right) + c \zeta^ {t / 2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} - h ^ {\gamma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {x} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {w} - \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + c \zeta^ {t}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} - h ^ {\sigma} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) \right\| _ {\zeta} \leq c \left(\left\| \mathbf {x} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t} \right\| _ {\zeta} + \left\| \mathbf {z} - \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \right\| _ {\zeta}\right) \\ + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \left(\| \mathbf {y} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t - 1} \| _ {\zeta} + \| \mathbf {w} - \boldsymbol {\omega} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta}\right) + c \zeta^ {t / 2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {t + 1} - h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq (c / \alpha) \| \mathbf {z} - \boldsymbol {\gamma} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {y} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {t} \| _ {\zeta} + c \zeta^ {t}
$$

proof of theorem 3.4(b). given theorem 3.4(a), it suffices to show that  $\delta_{tt} \to \delta_*$  and  $\gamma_{tt} \to \gamma_*$  as  $t \to \infty$ , where  $(\delta_*, \sigma_*, \gamma_*, \omega_*, x_*) \in i_\delta \times i_\sigma \times i_\gamma \times i_\omega \times \mathbb{r}$  is the unique fixed point of (3.28).

we define the map  $g: \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\gamma}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\omega}} \to \mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\gamma}} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\omega}}$  as the successive composition of the four maps

$$
\left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}\right) \mapsto \left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, h ^ {\omega} \left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}\right)\right)
$$

$$
\left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}\right) \mapsto \left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, h ^ {\gamma} \left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}\right), \mathbf {w}\right)
$$

$$
\left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}\right) \mapsto \left(\mathbf {x}, h ^ {\sigma} \left(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}\right), \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}\right)
$$

$$
(\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) \mapsto (h ^ {\delta} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}), \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w})
$$

writing its components as  $g = (g_x, g_y, g_z, g_w)$ , lemma c.3 may be applied to show that

$$
\| g _ {w} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - g _ {w} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + c \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}
$$

$$
\| g _ {z} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - g _ {z} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq \frac {c}{\alpha} \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {4}} \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {4}} \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}
$$

$$
\| g _ {y} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - g _ {y} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq c \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {4}} \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}
$$

$$
\| g _ {x} (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - g _ {x} (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta} \leq \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}} \| \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {3}} \| \mathbf {z} - \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {4}} \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta} + \frac {c}{\alpha^ {5}} \| \mathbf {w} - \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime} \| _ {\zeta}.
$$

then defining the norm  $\| \cdot \|_{\zeta ,\alpha}$  on  $\mathcal{x}_{i_\delta} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\sigma} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\gamma} \times \mathcal{x}_{i_\omega}$  by

$$
\| (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} = \| \mathbf {x} \| _ {\zeta} + (1 / \alpha) \| \mathbf {z} \| _ {\zeta} + (1 / \alpha^ {2}) \| \mathbf {y} \| _ {\zeta} + (1 / \alpha^ {3}) \| \mathbf {w} \| _ {\zeta},
$$

we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| g (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - g (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \leq (c / \alpha^ {2}) \| (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - (\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \\ \leq \tau \| (\mathbf {x}, \mathbf {y}, \mathbf {z}, \mathbf {w}) - \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {y} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {z} ^ {\prime}, \mathbf {w} ^ {\prime}\right) \| _ {\zeta , \alpha} \\ \end{array}
$$

for some  $\tau \in (0,1)$  and  $\alpha >\alpha_0$  sufficiently large. so  $g$  admits a unique fixed point  $(\mathbf{x}_{*},\mathbf{y}_{*},\mathbf{z}_{*},\mathbf{w}_{*})\in$ $\mathcal{x}_{i_{\delta}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\sigma}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\gamma}}\times \mathcal{x}_{i_{\omega}}$

by the same argument as in the proof of theorem 3.1(b) for the symmetric square setting, this fixed point  $(\mathbf{x}_{*},\mathbf{y}_{*},\mathbf{z}_{*},\mathbf{w}_{*})$  must be equal to scalar constants  $(\delta_{*},\sigma_{*},\gamma_{*},\omega_{*})$  in every coordinate, where these constants satisfy

$$
\sigma_ {*} = \sum_ {j, k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*} (1 - \delta_ {*}) (1 - \gamma_ {*})}{\gamma \sigma_ {*} \omega_ {*}}\right) ^ {j + k} \left(\kappa_ {2 (j + k + 1)} \gamma_ {*} + \kappa_ {2 (j + k + 2)} \frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} ^ {3} (1 - \gamma_ {*}) ^ {2}}{\omega_ {*} ^ {2}}\right)
$$

$$
\omega_ {*} = \sum_ {j, k = 0} ^ {\infty} \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*} (1 - \delta_ {*}) (1 - \gamma_ {*})}{\gamma \sigma_ {*} \omega_ {*}}\right) ^ {j + k} \left(\gamma \kappa_ {2 (j + k + 1)} \delta_ {*} + \gamma \kappa_ {2 (j + k + 2)} \frac {\alpha^ {2} \gamma_ {*} ^ {3} (1 - \delta_ {*}) ^ {2}}{\gamma \sigma_ {*} ^ {2}}\right)
$$

$$
\delta_ {*} = 1 - \mathrm {m m s e} \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2} \gamma_ {*} ^ {2}}{\gamma^ {2} \sigma_ {*}}\right), \qquad \gamma_ {*} = 1 - \mathrm {m m s e} \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} ^ {2}}{\omega_ {*}}\right).
$$

(the fixed point  $(\delta_{*},\sigma_{*},\gamma_{*},\omega_{*})$  to these equations exists by the banach fixed point theorem specialized to the scalar setting.) writing

$$
r ^ {\prime} (x) = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 k} \cdot k x ^ {k - 1} = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (k + 1)} \cdot (k + 1) x ^ {k}
$$

$$
s (x) = \left(\frac {r (x)}{x}\right) ^ {\prime} = \sum_ {k = 2} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 k} \cdot (k - 1) x ^ {k - 2} = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \kappa_ {2 (k + 2)} \cdot (k + 1) x ^ {k},
$$

we see that the above equations are equivalent to the fixed point equations (3.28). the proof is concluded using the same contractive mapping argument as in theorem 3.1(b).

c.3. verification of eq. (3.32). denote  $t(z) = (1 + z)(1 + \gamma z)$ . let us first show that the values  $\delta_{\mathrm{pca}}$  and  $\gamma_{\mathrm{pca}}$  in (3.29-3.30) may be written equivalently as

$$
\delta_ {\mathrm {p c a}} = \frac {t (r (x)) - x t ^ {\prime} (r (x)) r ^ {\prime} (x)}{1 + \gamma r (x)}, \quad \gamma_ {\mathrm {p c a}} = \frac {t (r (x)) - x t ^ {\prime} (r (x)) r ^ {\prime} (x)}{1 + r (x)}. \tag {c.32}
$$

to see this, let us define  $\varphi(z)$ ,  $\bar{\varphi}(z)$ , and  $d(z)$  as in (3.31), and define also

$$
m (z) = \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} m _ {2 k} ^ {\infty} z ^ {k}.
$$

from [bgn12, eq. (8)], the rectangular r-transform is given by  $r(z) = u(z(d^{-1}(z))^2 - 1)$ , where  $u(z)$  is a function defined such that  $t(u(z - 1)) = z$ . thus  $t(r(z)) = z(d^{-1}(z))^2$ , and differentiating on both sides yields

$$
t (r (z)) - z t ^ {\prime} (r (z)) r ^ {\prime} (z) = \frac {- 2 z ^ {2} d ^ {- 1} (z)}{d ^ {\prime} (d ^ {- 1} (z))}. \tag {c.33}
$$

next, applying series expansions for  $\varphi(z)$  and  $\bar{\varphi}(z)$  and substituting into  $d(z)$ , we obtain

$$
\varphi (z) = z ^ {- 1} \left(1 + m (z ^ {- 2})\right), \qquad \bar {\varphi} (z) = z ^ {- 1} \left(1 + \gamma m (z ^ {- 2})\right), \qquad d (z) = z ^ {- 2} t (m (z ^ {- 2})).
$$

by (2.7), the rectangular r-transform satisfies the identity  $m(z) = r(zt(m(z)))$ . then  $m(z^{-2}) = r(d(z))$ , so

$$
d (z) = z ^ {- 1} \bigl (1 + r (d (z)) \bigr) \cdot \bar {\varphi} (z) = \varphi (z) \cdot z ^ {- 1} \bigl (1 + \gamma r (d (z)) \bigr).
$$

hence, applying this with  $d^{-1}(z)$  in place of  $z$  and rearranging,

$$
\frac {z}{1 + r (z)} = \frac {\bar {\varphi} (d ^ {- 1} (z))}{d ^ {- 1} (z))}, \quad \frac {z}{1 + \gamma r (z)} = \frac {\varphi (d ^ {- 1} (z))}{d ^ {- 1} (z)}. \tag {c.34}
$$

applying these identities (c.33) and (c.34) for  $z = x$ , we see that (c.32) coincides with the definitions (3.29-3.30), as desired.

now we proceed to verify (3.32). as in remark 3.2, applying the mmse inequality (3.17) to the second and third fixed point equations of (3.28) and rearranging, we obtain

$$
\sigma_ {*} \geq \sigma_ {\mathrm {l b}} \equiv \frac {\alpha^ {2} \gamma_ {*} ^ {2} (1 - \delta_ {*})}{\gamma^ {2} \delta_ {*}}, \quad \omega_ {*} \geq \omega_ {\mathrm {l b}} \equiv \frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} ^ {2} (1 - \gamma_ {*})}{\gamma_ {*}}. \tag {c.35}
$$

we apply the following argument to "substitute" these inequalities into the remaining fixed-point equations: fixing  $\delta_{*} \in i_{\delta}$  and  $\gamma_{*} \in i_{\gamma}$ , denote

$$
x (\sigma , \omega) = \frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*} (1 - \delta_ {*}) (1 - \gamma_ {*})}{\gamma \sigma \omega}
$$

$$
f (\sigma , \omega) = \delta_ {*} r ^ {\prime} (x (\sigma , \omega)) + \frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} ^ {4} (1 - \gamma_ {*}) ^ {2}}{\gamma_ {*} \omega^ {2}} s (x (\sigma , \omega)),
$$

$$
g (\sigma , \omega) = \gamma \gamma_ {*} r ^ {\prime} (x (\sigma , \omega)) + \frac {\alpha^ {2} \gamma_ {*} ^ {4} (1 - \delta_ {*}) ^ {2}}{\gamma \delta_ {*} \sigma^ {2}} s (x (\sigma , \omega)).
$$

the fourth and fifth fixed point equations of (3.28) may be written as  $(\delta_{*} / \gamma_{*})\sigma_{*} = f(\sigma_{*},\omega_{*})$  and  $(\gamma_{*} / \delta_{*})\omega_{*} = g(\sigma_{*},\omega_{*})$ . so for any constant  $\eta \in \mathbb{r}$ ,  $(\sigma_{*},\omega_{*})$  solves the equation

$$
0 = f (\sigma , \omega) + \eta \cdot g (\sigma , \omega) - \frac {\delta_ {*}}{\gamma_ {*}} \sigma - \frac {\eta \cdot \gamma_ {*}}{\delta_ {*}} \omega . \tag {c.36}
$$

let us denote

$$
x = x (\sigma_ {\mathrm {l b}}, \omega_ {\mathrm {l b}}) = \gamma / \alpha^ {2}
$$

and pick this constant  $\eta$  to solve the linear equation

$$
\left(\eta \alpha^ {2} - r ^ {\prime} (x) - \frac {\eta \gamma^ {3}}{\alpha^ {2}} s (x)\right) \big (1 + r (x) \big) = \big (\frac {\alpha^ {2}}{\gamma^ {2}} - \eta \gamma r ^ {\prime} (x) - \frac {1}{\alpha^ {2}} s (x) \big) \big (1 + \gamma r (x) \big).
$$

note that for all  $\alpha >\alpha_0$  sufficiently large, we have  $\eta \approx [(1 + \gamma r(x)) / \gamma^2 ] / (1 + r(x))\approx 1 / \gamma^2$ , which is of constant order. we claim that for any  $\delta_{*}\in i_{\delta}$  and  $\gamma_{*}\in i_{\gamma}$ , the right side of (c.36) is decreasing as a function of  $\sigma \in [\sigma_{\mathrm{lb}},\infty)$  and  $\omega \in [\omega_{\mathrm{lb}},\infty)$ . to see this, observe first that since  $1 - \delta_{*}\leq c / \alpha^{2}$  and  $1 - \gamma_{*}\leq c / \alpha^{2}$ , we have  $|x(\sigma ,\omega)|\leq c / \alpha^{2}$  for parameters in these domains. then to compute the derivatives of  $f(\sigma ,\omega)$  and  $g(\sigma ,\omega)$ , we may apply the series expansions

$$
r ^ {\prime} (x (\sigma , \omega)) = \kappa_ {2} ^ {\infty} + 2 \kappa_ {4} ^ {\infty} \cdot \frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*} (1 - \delta_ {*}) (1 - \gamma_ {*})}{\gamma \sigma \omega} + \ldots
$$

$$
s (x (\sigma , \omega)) = \kappa_ {4} ^ {\infty} + 2 \kappa_ {6} ^ {\infty} \cdot \frac {\alpha^ {2} \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*} (1 - \delta_ {*}) (1 - \gamma_ {*})}{\gamma \sigma \omega} + \ldots
$$

which are convergent for  $\alpha >\alpha_0$  sufficiently large, and differentiate these term-by-term. we may thus verify the bounds

$$
| \partial_ {\sigma} r ^ {\prime} (x (\sigma , \omega)) |, | \partial_ {\omega} r ^ {\prime} (x (\sigma , \omega)) |, | \partial_ {\sigma} s (x (\sigma , \omega)) |, | \partial_ {\omega} s (x (\sigma , \omega)) | \leq \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}}, \qquad | s (x (\sigma , \omega)) | \leq c,
$$

which imply

$$
| \partial_ {\sigma} f (\sigma , \omega) |, | \partial_ {\omega} f (\sigma , \omega) |, | \partial_ {\sigma} g (\sigma , \omega) |, | \partial_ {\omega} g (\sigma , \omega) | \leq \frac {c}{\alpha^ {2}}.
$$

then the derivatives in  $(\sigma, \omega)$  of the right side of (c.36) are negative for all  $\alpha > \alpha_0$  sufficiently large, yielding the desired monotonicity.

since  $(\sigma_{*},\omega_{*})$  satisfies (c.36) with equality, we may then substitute (c.35) to obtain

$$
0 \leq f \left(\sigma_ {\mathrm {l b}}, \omega_ {\mathrm {l b}}\right) + \eta \cdot g \left(\sigma_ {\mathrm {l b}}, \omega_ {\mathrm {l b}}\right) - \frac {\delta_ {*}}{\gamma_ {*}} \sigma_ {\mathrm {l b}} - \frac {\eta \cdot \gamma_ {*}}{\delta_ {*}} \omega_ {\mathrm {l b}}. \tag {c.38}
$$

applying the forms of  $\sigma_{\mathrm{lb}}$ ,  $\omega_{\mathrm{lb}}$ ,  $f$ , and  $g$  and rearranging, we arrive at

$$
\left(\eta \alpha^ {2} - r ^ {\prime} (x) - \frac {\eta \gamma^ {3}}{\alpha^ {2}} s (x)\right) \delta_ {*} + \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2}}{\gamma^ {2}} - \eta \gamma r ^ {\prime} (x) - \frac {1}{\alpha^ {2}} s (x)\right) \gamma_ {*} \leq \left(\frac {\alpha^ {2}}{\gamma^ {2}} + \eta \alpha^ {2}\right) \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*}.
$$

now applying the identity (c.37), we may write this as

$$
a (x) \left(\left(1 + \gamma r (x)\right) \delta_ {*} + (1 + r (x)) \gamma_ {*}\right) \leq b (x) \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*}. \tag {c.39}
$$

here, solving explicitly the equation (c.37) for  $\eta$  and applying also  $s(x) = r'(x) / x - r(x) / x^2$ , these values  $a(x)$  and  $b(x)$  may be computed after some algebraic simplification to be

$$
a (x) = \frac {(1 + r (x)) (1 + \gamma r (x)) - x (1 + \gamma + 2 \gamma r (x)) r ^ {\prime} (x)}{\gamma x [ (1 + r (x)) (1 + \gamma r (x)) + x r ^ {\prime} (x) (1 - \gamma) ]}
$$

$$
b (x) = \frac {2 (1 + r (x)) (1 + \gamma r (x))}{\gamma x [ (1 + r (x)) (1 + \gamma r (x)) + x r ^ {\prime} (x) (1 - \gamma) ]}.
$$

note that for  $\alpha >\alpha_0$  sufficiently large (and hence small  $x = \gamma /\alpha^2$ ), the numerators and denominators of  $a(x)$  and  $b(x)$  are all positive. then clearing the denominators of  $a(x)$  and  $b(x)$  in (c.39) and applying to the left side

$$
(1 + \gamma r (x)) \delta_ {*} + (1 + r (x)) \gamma_ {*} \geq 2 \sqrt {(1 + \gamma r (x)) (1 + r (x)) \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*}}, \tag {c.40}
$$

we obtain

$$
\sqrt {(1 + r (x)) (1 + \gamma r (x)) \delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*}} \geq (1 + r (x)) (1 + \gamma r (x)) - x (1 + \gamma + 2 \gamma r (x)) r ^ {\prime} (x).
$$

recalling the notation  $t(z) = (1 + z)(1 + \gamma z)$ , this may be rewritten as

$$
\delta_ {*} \gamma_ {*} \geq \frac {[ t (r (x)) - x t ^ {\prime} (r (x)) r ^ {\prime} (x) ] ^ {2}}{t (r (x))},
$$

where the right side coincides with  $\delta_{\mathrm{pca}}\gamma_{\mathrm{pca}}$  by (c.32). this establishes (3.32).
